他这位新朋友读了，大喜；又读一遍，把这份喜悦讲了出来；赫克托这才头一回懂得，同情之力竟能使人对自己才能的自觉加倍。他重新拾起那部他本以为已完工的作品，以更开阔的眼光、更敏锐的判断、更明晰的意图，再从头至尾过了一遍；结果是，经由朋友的批评，以及那首诗在自己那探问着的心里新的映照，他发现有许多地方非得重新斟酌不可；之后，他把稿子仔细又极其工整地誊清一遍，再托给这位朋友；朋友又读一遍，比上次更为满意，便提议赫克托把它拿给另一位朋友看——那人的耳朵对某位出版商是敞开的。这第二位朋友的有利评判，那位出版商耐心地听了，并允诺一定给这部稿子以应有的重视。

===Original===
On this part of my story there is no occasion to linger; for, strange thing to tell—strange, I mean, from the unlikelihood of its happening—the poem found the sympathetic spot in the heart of the publisher, who had happily not delegated the task to his reader, but read it himself; and he made Hector the liberal offer to undertake all the necessary expenses, giving him a fair share of resulting profits.

===Chinese===
故事的这一段我无需多作停留；因为说来稀奇——我的意思是，按其发生之难以意料而言实在稀奇——那首诗正中那位出版商心底柔软之处，所幸他并未把这事交给他的审读人，而是亲自读了；他向赫克托提出了相当慷慨的条件：由他承担一切必要的费用，事成之后分赫克托一份公道的红利。

===Original===
Stranger yet, the poem was so far a success that the whole edition, not a large one, was sold, with a result in money necessarily small but far from unsatisfactory to Hector. At the publisher’s suggestion, this first volume was soon followed by another; and thus was Hector fairly launched on the uncertain sea of a literary life; happy in this, that he was not entirely dependent on literature for his bodily sustenance, but was in a position otherwise to earn at least his bread and cheese. For some time longer he continued to have no experience of the killing necessity of writing for his daily bread, beneath which so many aspiring spirits sink prematurely exhausted and withered; this was happily postponed, for there are as much Providence and mercy in the orderly arrangement of our trials as in their inevitable arrival.

===Chinese===
更奇的是，这诗集竟算是成功了：整版——虽说印数不多——全部售罄，所得钱款虽必然不多，对赫克托而言却已相当满意。经出版商提议，这头一卷之后不久便跟上了第二卷；就这样，赫克托可算是被正式送上了文学生涯那变幻莫测的大海；他可庆幸的一点是：他并非全然靠笔墨糊口，另外还能挣到至少一日三餐的面包奶酪。此后又有相当一段时间，他未尝经历过为糊口而非写不可的那种致命的必要——多少有志之士正是在这重压之下早早力竭而枯萎；这一重负有幸被推迟了，因为试炼的有序安排之中，与其无可避免的到来之中，同样有着神意与怜悯。

===Original===
His reception by what is called the public was by no means so remarkable or triumphant as to give his well-wishers any ground for anxiety as to its possible moral effect upon him; but it was a great joy to him that his father was much interested and delighted in the reception of the poem by the Reviews in general. He was so much gratified, indeed, that he immediately wrote to him stating his intention of supplementing his income by half as much more.

===Chinese===
所谓"公众"对他的接纳，并未达到那样引人瞩目或凯旋般的地步，以至于会让他的亲友担心这可能对他的品性有何不良影响；但有一件事令他大喜过望：他父亲对各家评论刊物对这部诗集的反响，表现出浓厚的兴趣和由衷的欢欣。他父亲着实高兴，立刻写信给他，说要在他原有收入之外再添上一半，以资助他。

===Original===
This reflected opinion of others wrought also to the mollifying of his mother’s feelings toward him; but those with which she regarded Annie they only served to indurate, as the more revealing the girl’s unworthiness of him. And although at first she regarded with favor her husband’s kind intention toward Hector, she faced entirely round when he showed her a letter he had from his son thanking him for his generosity, and communicating his intention of begging Annie to come to him and be married at once.

===Chinese===
这种他人折射回来的评价，也缓和了他母亲对他的感情；至于她看待安妮的态度，这评价反倒使之更加刚硬，仿佛愈发坐实了那姑娘配不上他。起先她倒还赞成丈夫对赫克托的那番好意，可当他把儿子来信拿给她看——信里谢了他的慷慨，并告知打算求安妮到他这里来、即刻成亲——她顿时整个儿翻了脸。

===Original===
Annie was living at home, feeding on Hector’s letters, and strengthened by her mother’s sympathy. She was teaching regularly at the High School, and adding a little to their common income by giving a few music lessons, as well as employing her needle in a certain kind of embroidery a good deal sought after, in which she excelled. She had heard nothing of his having begun to distinguish himself, neither had yet seen one of the reviews of his book, for no one had taken the trouble to show her any of them.

===Chinese===
安妮住在家里，以赫克托的信为食粮，又得母亲的同情作扶持。她在中学里按部就班地教书，另外还授几堂音乐课，给家用添一点进项，又拿针线做一种颇受欢迎、自己也拿手的刺绣。至于他已开始崭露头角的事，她一无所闻，他书里的任何一篇书评她也没见过，因为谁也没费心拿给她看过。

===Original===
One day, however, as she stood waiting a moment for something she wanted in the principal bookshop of the town, a little old lady, rather shabbily dressed, came in, whom she heard say to the shopman, in a gentle voice, and with the loveliest smile:

===Chinese===
然而有一天，她正在城中那家最大的书店里站着，等她要的一件东西，一位衣着颇为寒素的小老太太走了进来，安妮听见她以温柔的声音、带着最可亲的笑容，对店伙计说道：

===Original===
“Have you another copy of this new poem by your townsman, young Macintosh?”

===Chinese===
"贵同乡小麦金托什的那本新诗集，您这儿还有别的本子吗？"

===Original===
“I am sorry I have not, ma’am,” answered the shopman; “but I can get you one by return of post.”

===Chinese===
"很抱歉没有了，太太，"店伙计答道，"不过下一班邮我就能给您弄来一册。"

===Original===
“Do, if you please, and send it me at once. I am very glad to hear it promises to be a great success. I am sure it quite deserves it. I have already read it through twice. You may remember you got me a copy the other day. I cannot help thinking it an altogether remarkable production, especially for so young a man. He is quite young, I believe?”

===Chinese===
"麻烦您了，就请给我弄一册，并尽快寄来。我很高兴听说它大有畅销之势。我敢说它完全当得起。我已经从头到尾读过两遍。您也许记得，前些日子您替我弄到了一册。我不禁觉得这是一部十分不凡的作品，尤其是出自这样一位年轻人之手。他相当年轻，是吧？"

===Original===
“Yes, ma’am—to have already published a book. But as to any wonderful success, there is so little sale for poetry nowadays. I believe the one you had yourself, my lady, is the only one we have been asked for.”

===Chinese===
"是的，太太——就已经出了一本书而言。至于多大的成功，时下诗是太不好卖了。我想您自己拿去的那一册，夫人，怕是我们被问起的唯一一册了。"

===Original===
“Much will depend,” said the lady, “on whether it finds a channel of its own soon enough. But get me another copy, anyhow—and as soon as you can, please. I want to send it to my daughter. There is matter between those Quaker-like boards that I have found nowhere else. I want my daughter to have it, and I cannot part with my own copy,” concluded the old lady, and with the words she walked out of the shop, leaving Annie bewildered, and with the strange feeling of a surprise, which yet she had been expecting. For what else but such success could come to Hector? Had it not been drawing nearer and nearer all the time? And for a moment she seemed again to stand, a much younger child than now, amid the gusty whirling of the dead leaves about her feet, once more on the point of stooping to pick up what might prove a withered leaf, but was in reality a pound-note, the thing which had wrought her so much misery, and was now filling her cup of joy to the very brim. The book the old lady had talked of could be no other than Hector’s book. No other than Hector could have written it. What a treasure there was in the world that she had never seen! How big was it? what was it like? She was sure to know it the moment her eyes fell upon it. But why had he never told her about it? He might have wanted to surprise her, but she was not the least surprised. She had known it all the time! He had never talked about what he was writing, and still less would he talk of what he was going to write. Intentions were not worthy of his beautiful mouth! Perhaps he did not want her to read it yet. When he did, he would send her a copy. And, oh! when would her mother be able to read it? Was it a very dear book? There could be no thought of their buying it! Between them, she and her mother could not have shillings enough for that. When the right time came, he would send it. Then it would be twice as much hers as if she had bought it for herself.

===Chinese===
"那多半要看它能否及时找到一条属于自己的路了，"那位女士说，"但无论如何给我弄一册——越快越好，麻烦您。我想寄给我女儿。在那两块教友派似的朴素封面之间，藏着我在别处遍寻不得的东西。我要我女儿也有一本，至于我自己这本，我可舍不得脱手。"老太太说完这话便出了店门，留下安妮又惊又惑，怀着一种奇怪的感觉——一种她其实早就在盼望着的意外。因为赫克托除了这样的成功，还能有什么别的呢？它不是一直在一步步临近吗？有那么一刻，她仿佛又成了一个比眼下小得多的孩子，站在她脚边纷纷扬扬、随风乱舞的枯叶之中，又到了俯身去拾那片也许只是枯叶、其实却是一张一镑钞票的东西的节骨眼上——正是这东西曾给她带来那么多苦楚，如今又把她的喜悦之杯斟到了齐沿。老太太所谈的那本书，除了赫克托的书，不可能是别的。除了赫克托，没有人写得出它。世上竟有她从未见过的这样一件宝物！它有多大？是什么样子？她相信她一眼便能把它认出来。可他为什么从来没告诉过她呢？他也许本想给她一个惊喜，可她一点也不觉得意外。她一直都明白着呢！他从不曾谈起自己正在写什么，更不会谈起将要写什么。种种打算，都不配从他那张好看的嘴里讲出来！他也许还不想让她读它。等他想了，自会寄一册给她。哦！她母亲什么时候才能读到它呢？那是一本很贵的书吗？她们母女俩想买它，那是连想都不必想！她们俩凑一块儿，也凑不出那么多先令。等到时机成熟，他自会寄来的。那时候，它就比她自己掏钱买的还要加倍地属于她了。

===Original===
The next day she met Mr. and Mrs. Macintosh, and the former actually congratulated her on what Hector had done and what people thought of him for it; but the latter only gave a sniff. And the next post brought the book itself, and with it a petition from Hector that she would fix the day to join him in London.

===Chinese===
第二天她遇见了麦金托什先生和太太，前者竟就赫克托所成之事、众人对他的称道向她道了贺；后者却只哼了一声鼻子。而下一班邮便把书本身送了来，随书附上赫克托的恳求：请她定下日子，到伦敦与他团聚。

===Original===
Annie made haste, therefore, to get ready the dress of white linen in which she meant to be married, and a lady, the sister of Hector’s friend, meeting her in London, they were married the next day, and went together to Hector’s humble lodgings in a northern suburb.

===Chinese===
于是安妮赶紧把她打算成亲时穿的那件白夏布衣裙准备停当，由赫克托那位朋友的妹妹做女傧相，在伦敦与她相会，第二天他们便成了亲，一同住进赫克托在北郊一所简朴的寓所。

===Original===
Hector’s new volume, larger somewhat, but made up of smaller poems, did not attract the same amount of attention as the former, and the result gave no encouragement to the publisher to make a third venture. One reason possibly was that the subjects of most of the poems, even the gayest of them, were serious, and another may have been that the common tribe of reviewers, searching like other parasites, discovered in them material for ridicule—which to them meant food, and as such they made use of it. At the same time he was not left without friends: certain of his readers, who saw what he meant and cared to understand it, continued his readers; and his influence on such was slowly growing, while those that admired, feeling the power of his work, held by him the more when the scoffers at him grew insolent. Still, few copies were sold, and Hector found it well that he had other work and was not altogether dependent on his pen, which would have been simple starvation. And, from the first, Annie was most careful in her expenditure.

===Chinese===
赫克托的新诗集比头一部稍厚一些，却都是由较短的诗作缀成，未能像前一部那样引人注目，这结果也令出版商无意作第三度尝试。原因之一，也许是大多数诗作——哪怕其中最轻快的——题材都偏严肃；另一个原因，也许是那群与别的寄生物一般四处搜猎的平庸书评人，从中找到了可以讥嘲的由头——于他们而言那便是食粮，他们便如此这般地把它利用了。同时他身边仍有朋友：某些看出他的用心、也有意去领会的读者，继续做他的读者；他在这些人中的影响正缓慢地增长，而那些因感到他作品的力量而钦慕他的人，在讥嘲他的人愈发无礼之际，反而愈发坚定地与他站在一起。不过，售出的册数仍寥寥无几，赫克托庆幸自己另有差事，并不全靠一支笔，否则那简直就是自寻饿死。而且，从一开始，安妮在用度上便极其俭省。

===Original===
Among the simple people whom her husband brought her to know, she speedily became a great favorite, and this circle widened more rapidly after she joined it. For her simple truth, which even to Hector had occasionally seemed some what overdriven, now revealed itself as the ground of her growing popularity. She welcomed all, was faithful to all, and sympathetic with all. Nor was it longer before her husband began to study her in order to understand her—and that the more that he could find in her neither plan nor system, nothing but straightforward, foldless simplicity. Nor did she ever come to believe less in the foreseeing care of God. She ceased perhaps to attribute so much to the ministry of the angels as when she took the fiercer blast that rescued from the flames the greasy note and blew it uncharred up the roaring chimney for the sudden waft of an angel’s wing; but she came to meet them oftener in daily life, clothed in human form, though still they were rare indeed, and often, like the angel that revealed himself to Manoah, disappeared upon recognition.

===Chinese===
在她丈夫引她结识的那些朴实人之中，她很快成了众人的宠儿，而她加入之后，这个圈子扩展得更快了。因为她那份朴素的真诚——连赫克托偶尔都觉得有点过头——如今反倒显出是她日益得人心的根基。她待谁都欢迎，对谁都忠心，对谁都体贴。不久，她丈夫也开始琢磨她、想弄懂她——尤其因为他在她身上既找不出什么计谋，也找不出什么章法，唯有坦直无折的单纯。她也从未减少对神那未雨绸缪之眷顾的信赖。她也许不再像从前那样，把那张油腻腻的钞票从火里救出、又把它毫发无损地吹上轰鸣的烟囱的那阵最猛的狂风，当作天使翅膀的一阵骤然鼓动，归功于天使的服役；可她倒反而在日常生活中更频繁地与他们相遇——他们披着人形显现，虽然仍旧稀罕得很，而且常常像向玛挪瓦（Manoah）显现的那位天使一样，一经认出便消失不见。

===Original===
By-and-by it seemed certain that, if ever Hector had had anything of what the world counts success, it had now come to a pause. For a long time he wrote nothing that, had it been published, could have produced any impression like that of his first book; it seemed as if the first had forestalled the success of those that should follow. That had been of a new sort, and the so-called Public, innocent little personification, was not yet grown ready for anything more of a similar kind, which, indeed, seemed to lack elements of attraction and interest; and the readers to whom the same man will tell even new things are apt to grow weary of his mode of saying, even though that mode have improved in directness and force; the tide of his small repute had already begun to take the other direction. Those who understood and prized his work, still holding by him, and declaring that they found in him what they found in no other writer, remained stanch in their friendship, and among them the little old lady who had at once welcomed his first poem to her heart and whose name and position were now well known to Hector. But the reviewers, seeming to have forgotten their first favorable reception of him, now began to find nothing but faults in his work, pointing out only what they judged ill contrived and worse executed in his conceptions, and that in a tone to convey the impression that he had somehow wheedled certain of them into their former friendly utterances concerning him.

===Chinese===
渐渐地，似乎可以断定：赫克托纵然有过几分世所谓"成功"的东西，如今也已停顿下来。有很长一段时间，他写出的东西，即便付梓，也再不能像头一部书那样造成什么声势；仿佛第一部已把后续之作的成功都抢先占尽了。那是新颖的一类，而那位被称作"公众"的、天真的小拟人化形象，尚未准备好接纳任何更多同类的东西——而那些东西仿佛也确乎缺乏引人入胜、令人关注的要素；再者，同一位作者纵然讲述新的内容，读他的人也容易厌倦他讲述的方式，哪怕这方式在直接与有力上已有所长进；他那点小小名气的潮水，已经开始朝相反的方向退去。那些懂得他、珍视他作品的人仍守着他，宣称在他身上找着了在别的作家身上找不到的东西，他们对他的友谊始终不渝；其中便有那位第一时间把他的头一部诗集迎进心头的小老太太——她的姓名和身份如今赫克托已十分清楚。可那些书评人却像忘了他头一次受到的有礼遇，如今开始在他的作品里只挑毛病，专指出他们所认为构思拙劣、执行更糟之处，而且语调之间仿佛暗示：他不知怎地哄骗过他们之中某些人，才换来了先前那些友好的话。

===Original===
And about the same time it so happened that business began to fall away rapidly from the bank of which his father held the chief country agency, so that he was no longer able to continue to Hector his former subsidy, the announcement of which discouraging fact was accompanied by a lecture on the desirableness of a change in his choice of subject as well as in his style; if he continued to write as he had been doing of late, no one would be left, his father said, to read what he wrote!

===Chinese===
大约也就在这时，他父亲任首席乡间代理的那家银行的生意开始急剧下滑，于是他再也无法像从前那样资助赫克托了；这一令人沮丧的消息传来，还附了一通训诫，说他在题材和风格上都该有所改变才好；他父亲说，他要是照近来这么写下去，留下来读他东西的人，可就一个都不剩了！

===Original===
And now it began to be evident what a happy thing it was for Hector that Annie was now at his side to help him. For, as his courage sank, and he saw Annie began to feel straitened in her housekeeping, he saw also how her courage arose and shone. But he grew more and more discouraged, until it was all that Annie could do to hold him back from despair. At length, however, she began to feel that possibly there might be some truth in what his father had written to him, and a new departure ought to be attempted. She could not herself believe that her husband was limited to any style or subject for the embodiment of his thoughts; he who had written so well in one fashion might write at least well, if not as well, in another! Had she not heard him say that verse was the best practice for writing prose?

===Chinese===
如今事情开始显明：赫克托幸而有安妮在身边扶持，是何等幸运的一件事。因为他的勇气一落千丈，眼见安妮开始为家用捉襟见肘而发愁，他也同时看到她的勇气如何昂然升起、熠熠生辉。可他还是愈发消沉，直到安妮竭尽全力才把他从绝望的边缘拉回来。终于，她开始觉得，他父亲写给他的那些话也许有几分道理，该尝试一次新的起步了。她自己断然不信丈夫会受限于某一种风格或题材才能表达他的思想；既曾在一种写法上写得那样好，换一种至少也能写得好——纵然不能同样好！她岂不是听他说过，写诗正是写散文最好的练习？

===Original===
Gently, therefore, and cautiously she approached the matter with him, only to find at first, as she had expected, that he but recoiled from the suggestion with increase of discouragement. Still, taking no delight in obstinacy, and feeling the necessity of some fresh attempt grow daily more pressing, he turned his brains about, and sending them foraging, at length bethought him of a certain old Highland legend with which at one time he had been a good deal taken, from the discovery in it of certain symbolical possibilities. This legend he proceeded to rewrite and remodel, doing his best endeavor to preserve in it the old Celtic aroma and aerial suggestion, while taking care neither to lose nor reproduce too manifestly its half-apparent, still evanishing symbolism. Urged by fear and enfeebled by doubt, he wrote feverously, and, after three days of laborious and unnatural toil, submitted the result to Annie, who was now his only representative of the outer world, and the only person for whose criticism he seemed now to care. She, greatly in doubt of her own judgment, submitted it to his friend; and together they agreed on this verdict: That, while it certainly proved he could write as well in prose as in verse, people would not be attracted by it, and that it would be found lacking in human interest. His friend saw in it also too much of the Celtic tendency to the mystical and allegorical, as distinguished from the factual and storial.

===Chinese===
于是她温和而谨慎地同他提起这事，起初果然如她所料，他不过是从这建议跟前退缩，愈发沮丧。不过，他既不以固执为乐，又感到作一番新尝试的必要已日渐迫切，便绞尽脑汁，让它们四出觅食，末了想起一段苏格兰高地的古老传说——他一度曾对它颇为着迷，因为从中发现了某些象征性的可能。他便着手重写、重塑这段传说，竭力保留住其中那份古老的凯尔特情调和空灵的暗示，同时又留心既不丢失、也不致过于显白地复现它那半隐半现、转瞬即逝的象征。他在恐惧的催迫下、在疑虑的消磨中，发着烧似的写着，经过三日艰辛而不自然的劳作，把成果交给了安妮——她如今是他对外界唯一的代表，也是他似乎唯一在乎其评判的人。她对自己的判断毫无把握，便把它转给了他的朋友；他们两个一同得出这样的评语：它诚然证明了他写散文能写得与写诗一样好，但人们不会被它吸引，会嫌它缺乏人情味。他朋友还从中看出了太多的凯尔特人那种倾向神秘、倾向寓言的气质——这是与重事实、重故事的倾向相对而言的。

===Original===
Upon learning this their decision, poor Hector fell once more into a state of great discouragement, not feeling in him the least power of adopting another way; there seemed to him but one mode, the way things came to him. And in this surely he was right—only might not things come, or be sent to him in some other way? His friend suggested that he might, changing the outward occurrences, and the description of the persons to whom they happened, in such fashion that there could be no identification of them, tell the very tale of how Annie and he came to know and love each other, taking especial care to muffle up to shapelessness, or at least featurelessness, the part his mother had taken in their story. This seeming to Hector a thing possible, he took courage, and set about it at once, gathering interest as he proceeded, and writing faster and faster as he grew in hope of success. At the same time it was not favorable to the result that he felt constantly behind him, the darkly lowering necessity that, urging him on, yet debilitated every motion of the generating spirit.

===Chinese===
得知他们这个定论，可怜的赫克托又陷入了极度消沉，觉得自己身上没有半点能力去改走另一条路；在他看来，似乎只有一种方式，那便是事物降临到他身上的那种方式。在这点上他无疑是对的——只是，事物难道不能以别的方式降临到他身上、或被送到他身上吗？他朋友提议，他不妨把外在的事件、把落到事件中的人物描绘都改头换面一番，改得叫人无从辨认，然后把安妮与他相知相爱的真情实节写出来，只须格外小心地把他们故事里他母亲所扮演的那一部分遮掩得不成形状、或至少模糊其面。这在赫克托看来是可行的事，便鼓起勇气，立刻动手，越写越入佳境，随着成功有望而越写越快。与此同时，对结果并非有利的是：他始终感到身后那阴沉压顶的必要——它一边催他向前，一边却削弱了他那生发之心的每一丝动弹。

===Original===
It took him a long time to get the story into a condition that he dared to consider even passable; and the longer that he had not the delight that verse would have brought with it in the process of its production. Nevertheless he would now and then come to a passage in writing which the old emotion would seem to revive; but in reading these, Annie, modest and doubtful as she always was of her own judgment, especially where her husband’s work was concerned, seemed to recognize a certain element of excitement that gave it a glow, or rather, glamour of unreality, or rather, unnaturalness, which affected her as inharmonious, therefore unfit, or out of place. She thought it better, however, to say little or nothing of any such paragraph, and tried to regard it as of small significance, and probably carrying little influence in respect of the final judgment.

===Chinese===
他花了很长时间，才把故事写到他自己敢认为尚可的地步；何况他还少了诗在写作过程中所会带来的那份愉悦，时间便显得愈发漫长。尽管如此，写作中他偶尔也会来到这样一段：旧日的情绪似乎又苏醒了过来；但在读这些段落时，安妮——她对自己的判断一向谦逊而存疑，尤其在丈夫的作品上——似乎从中察觉到一种兴奋的成分，赋予它一种光，或者毋宁说一种不真实的光彩，或者毋宁说不自然的色彩，使她感到不和谐，因此不宜、不合其位。可她觉得，对这样的段落还是少说或不说为好，便试着把它看作无关紧要，大概对最终的评判也起不了多少影响。

===Original===
The narrative, such as it might prove, was at length finished, and had been read, at least with pleasure and hope, by his friend, who was still the only critic on whose judgment he dared depend, for he could not help regarding Annie as prejudiced in his favor, although her approval continued for him absolutely essential. The sole portions to which his friend took any exception were the same concerning which Annie had already doubted, and which he found too poetical in their tone—not, he took care to say, in their meaning, for that could not be too poetical, but in their expression, which must impinge too sharply upon prosaic ears that cared only for the narrative, and would recoil from any reflection, however just in itself, that might be woven into it.

===Chinese===
这部叙事作品——无论它最终如何——到底写完了，并已由他的朋友——仍是他唯一敢仰仗其判断的批评者——至少怀着愉悦和希望读过；因为他总免不了把安妮看作偏袒他的人，尽管她的首肯对他始终是绝对必要的。他朋友唯一略有保留的几处，正是安妮早先也起过疑的几处，他觉得那几处的调子太诗化了——他特意说明，不是指意思，意思再诗化也不过分，而是指表达；这种表达必定会过于尖锐地刺到那些只关心故事、对任何织入其中的反思——无论其本身如何公允——都要退缩的平庸耳朵。

===Original===
But, alas, now came what Hector felt the last and final blow to the possibility of farther endeavor in the way of literature!

===Chinese===
可是，唉！如今赫克托所感到的、对他文学这条路上进一步努力的最后、最致命的一击，到来了！

===Original===
The bank to which Hector had been introduced by his father, and in which he had been employed ever since, had of late found it necessary to look more closely to its outlay and reduce its expenses; therefore, believing that Hector had abundance of other resources, its managers decided on giving him notice first of all that they must in future deprive themselves of the pleasure of his services. And this announcement came at a time when Annie was already in no small difficulty to make the ends of her expenditure meet those of her income. In fact, she had no longer any income. For a considerable time she had, by the stinting of what had before that seemed necessities, been making a shilling do the work of eighteenpence, and now she knew nothing beyond, except to go without. But how allow Hector to go without? He must die if she did! Already he had begun to shrink in his clothes from lack of proper nourishment.

===Chinese===
赫克托由父亲引荐进去、自那以后一直受雇其中的那家银行，近来觉得有此必要，得更紧地盯着开支、削减费用；于是，相信赫克托另有丰厚的资源，那帮管事的决定头一个给他递话：今后他们只得割爱，不再劳他大驾。这消息传来之时，安妮正为使支出的尽头够得上收入的尽头而大费周章。事实上，她已然没有任何收入了。相当一段时间以来，她靠着克扣从前被认作必需品的种种，把一先令当成一先令六便士使；如今除了干脆不用，她也别无他法了。可她怎能让赫克托也不用？那他便只有死路一条！因营养不良，他已经渐渐在衣裳里缩下去一圈了。

===Original===
A rumor reaching him of a certain post as librarian, in the gift of an old corporation, being vacant, Hector at once made application for it, but only to receive the answer that Pegasus must not be put in harness: poor Pegasus, on a false pretense of respect, must be kept out of the shafts! His fat friends would not permit him to degrade himself earning his bread by work he could have done very well; he must rather starve! He tried for many posts, one after the other. Heavier and heavier fell upon him each following disappointment. Annie had in her heart been greatly disappointed that no prospect appeared of a child to sanctify their union; but for that she had learned more than to console herself with the reflection that at least there was no such heavenly visitor for whose earthly sojourn to provide; and now how gladly would she have labored for the child in the hope that such a joy and companionship might lift him up out of his despondency! Then he would be able to enjoy and assimilate the poor food she was able to get for him. It is true he always seemed quite content; but, then, he would often, she believed, pretend not to be hungry, and certainly ate less and less. Hitherto she had fought with all her might against running in debt to the tradespeople, for, more than all else, she feared debt. Now, at last, however, her resolution was in danger of giving way, when, happily, Hector bethought himself of his precious books; to what better use could he put them than sell them to buy food—wherein the books he had written had failed him? Parcel by parcel in a leather strap, he carried them to the nearest secondhand bookseller, where he had so often bought; now he wanted to sell, but, unhappily, he soon found that books, like many other things, are worth much less to the seller than to the buyer, and where Hector had calculated on pounds, only shillings were forthcoming. Yet by their sale, notwithstanding, they managed to keep a little longer out of debt.

===Chinese===
有一则风声传到他耳里，说某古老公司名下有一个图书馆员的职位空缺，赫克托立刻去求职，结果得到的回答却是：不可把珀伽索斯（Pegasus）套进辕里！可怜的飞马珀伽索斯，被人以一份虚情假意的礼遇，给挡在了辕外！他那些脑满肠肥的朋友不许他自贬身份、去挣一份他完全能胜任的口粮；他宁可饿着吧！他一个接一个地谋过许多职位。每一次失望都愈发沉重地落到他身上。安妮心里也曾大失所望，因为迟迟不见生个孩子来圣化他们的结合的指望；可这一点她已学会不再以此自慰，至少不必为一位天上的来客预备尘世的寄居；可如今她又多么甘愿为孩子操劳，盼着这样一份欢乐与陪伴能把他从萎靡中提振起来！那样，他也就能享用并消化她所能为他张罗的粗粝饮食了。他似乎倒总是一副心满意足的样子；可她相信，他时常是装作不饿，而且无疑吃得越来越少。迄今为止，她拼尽全力抗拒向店家赊账，因为她最怕的便是欠债。可如今她的决心眼看就要动摇了；所幸赫克托这时想起了他那几箱珍爱的书；除了拿它们换吃的，还能派什么更好的用场？——他自己写的那些书可没能帮上他！他一包一包地用皮带捆着，搬到最近的那家旧书铺——他从前常在那里买书；如今却要卖，不幸他很快便发现，书与许多别的东西一样，对卖主的价钱远比对买主为低；赫克托盘算着能得几镑的地方，到手的却只有几个先令。然而靠卖书，他们毕竟又多撑了一段时日，没有赊账。

===Original===
And in these days Annie had at length finished her fair copy of Hector’s last book, writing it out in her own lovelily legible hand—not such as ladies in general count legible, because they can easily read it themselves; she could do better than that, she could write so that others could not fail to read. For Hector had always believed that the acceptance of his first volume had been owing not a little to the fact that he had written it out most legibly, and he held that what reveals itself at once and without possibility of mistake may justly hope for a better reception than what from the first moment annoys the reader with a sense of ill-treatment. It is no wonder, he said, if such a manuscript be at once tossed aside with an imprecation. Legibility is the first and intelligibility the only other thing rendered due by the submission of a manuscript to any publisher.

===Chinese===
也正是在这段日子里，安妮终于把赫克托最后一本书的誊清本写完了，用她那一笔极其可人的好字——不是女士们通常所认为的那种"看得清"，因为她们自己能轻易看懂便算；她可比这强得多，她能写得让旁人也一望便知。因为赫克托一向相信，他头一部诗集之所以被接受，相当程度上是因为他抄写得十分工整易读；他主张，一望而知、绝无看错之虞的东西，理当比那种头一眼就令读者有受辱之感的东西，受到更好的礼遇。他说，这样的稿子若被人顺手带一句咒骂扔到一边，一点也不足为奇。把稿子投给任何出版商，头一样应尽的便是"看得清"，唯一另一样应尽的便是"看得懂"。

===Original===
Hector spent a day or two in remodeling and modifying the passages remarked upon by his wife and his friend, and then, with hope reviving in both their hearts, the manuscript was sent in, acknowledged, and the day appointed when an answer would be ready.

===Chinese===
赫克托花了一两天，把他妻子和朋友指出的几处加以改造、润色，然后，两颗心里都重新燃起希望，稿子投了出去，对方回函收悉，并定下了答复之日。

===Original===
Upon a certain dark morning, therefore, in November, having nothing else whatever to do, Hector set out in his much-worn Inverness cape to call upon his former publisher in the City, with whom of late he had had no communication. The weather was cold and damp, threatening rain. But Hector was too much of a Scotchman to care about weather, and too full of anxiety to mind either cold or wet. He had, indeed, almost always felt gloomy weather exciting rather than depressing. For one thing, it seemed, when he was indoors, to close him about with protection from uncongenial interruption, leaving the freer his inventive faculty; and now that he was abroad in it, and no inventive faculty left awake, it seemed to clothe him with congenial sympathy, for the weather was just the same inside him. And now, as he strode along with his eyes on the ground, he scarcely saw any of the objects about him, but sought only the heart of the City, where he hoped to find the publisher in his office, ready to print his manuscript, and advance him a small sum in anticipation of possible profit. So absorbed was he in thought undefined, and so sunk in anxiety as to the answer he was about to receive, that more than once he was nearly run over by the cart of some reckless tradesman—seeming to him, in its overtaking suddenness, the type of prophetic fate already at his heels.

===Chinese===
于是在十一月一个阴沉的早晨，因为旁的什么也无事可做，赫克托披上他那件破旧的因弗内斯式斗篷，出门去城里造访他从前的出版商——近来他已与对方断了往来。天又冷又潮，眼看要下雨。可赫克托到底是个十足的苏格兰人，不会把天气放在心上，又焦虑得太甚，冷也罢、湿也罢都不在意。说真的，他几乎一向都觉得阴沉的天气是令人振奋而非令人沮丧的。一来，当他在屋里时，这种天气仿佛把他围在一层护持之中，挡开不合时宜的打搅，使他的创造之能愈发自由；如今他置身其中，创造之能又已不再清醒，这种天气便仿佛为他披上一层相契的同情的衣裳，因为他心里头的天气，原本就是这般。此刻他大步流星地走着，两眼盯着地面，几乎看不见身边任何物件，一心只往城中心赶——他盼着能在那儿、在出版商的办事处里找到他，指望他已预备好付印他的稿子，并预支一笔小款，以押在可能的盈利上。他沉浸在不甚分明的思绪里，又为将要得到的答复忧心忡忡，竟不止一次险些被某个莽撞的货车匠的车撞倒——那车在他身后追上来的猝不及防，在他看来，正像那预言般的命运已追到了他脚后跟。

===Original===
At length, however, he arrived safe in the outer shop, where the books of the firm were exposed to sight, in process of being subscribed for by the trade. There a pert young man asked him to take a seat, while he carried his name to the publisher, and there for some time he waited, reading titles he found himself unable to lay hold of; and there, while he waited, the threatened rain began, and, ere he was admitted to the inner premises, such a black deluge came pouring down as, for blackness at least, comes down nowhere save in London. With this accompaniment, he was ushered at length into a dingy office, deep in the recesses of the house, where a young man whom he saw for the first time had evidently, while Hector waited in the shop, been glancing at the manuscript he had left. Little as he could have read, however, it had been enough, aided perhaps by the weather, to bring him to an unfavorable decision; his rejection was precise and definite, leaving no room for Hector to say anything, for he did not seem ever to have heard of him before. Hector rose at once, gathered up his papers from the table where they lay scattered, said “Good morning,” and went out into the sooty rain.

===Chinese===
不过，他终于安然到了外间铺面，那里摆着这家书商出的书，正供同业认购。一个轻佻的年轻人请他落座，自己则把他的名姓通报给出版商；他就在那里等了一阵，读着那些他抓不住要领的书名；就在他等候的时候，那预告过的雨下了起来，而他还未被让进内间之前，一场乌黑的倾盆大雨便倒了下来——这种黑，至少，是除伦敦之外哪里也下不出来的。就在这一片伴奏声里，他终于被领进一间昏暗的办公室，深藏在宅子的腹地；那里有一个他头一回见到的年轻人，显然是在赫克托于铺面等候之时，匆匆翻过他留下的稿子。然而他读过的虽不多，却已足够——也许还借了天气的一点力——使他得出了不利的结论；他的回绝干脆而明确，没有给赫克托留下半句置喙的余地，因为他似乎从未听过赫克托其人。赫克托立刻起身，把散在桌上的稿纸一一收起，说声"早安"，便走进了那乌黑的雨里。

===Original===
Not knowing whitherward to point his foot, he stopped at the corner of King William Street, close to the money-shops of the old Lombards, and there stood still, in vain endeavor to realize the blow that had stunned him. There he stood and stood, with bowed head, like an outcast beggar, watching the rain that dropped black from the rim of his saturated hat. Becoming suddenly conscious, however, that the few wayfarers glanced somewhat curiously at him as they passed, he started to walk on, not knowing whither, but trying to look as if he had a purpose somewhere inside him, whereas he had still a question to settle—whether to buy a bun, and, on the strength of that, walk home, or spend his few remaining pence on an omnibus, as far as it would take him for the money, and walk the rest of the way.

===Chinese===
不知该把脚步迈向何方，他在威廉国王街（King William Street）的街角、紧挨着老伦巴第人那几家钱铺子跟前停了下来，怔怔地立着，徒然地想弄明白那把他打懵了的一击。他就那样站着、站着，垂着头，像个被抛弃的乞丐，看着雨水从他浸透了的帽檐上一滴一滴黑黑地落下。然而他忽然察觉，路过的少数几个行人都带着几分好奇地打量他，便又迈步走起来，不知走向何处，却竭力装出肚子里怀着什么打算的样子，其实他尚有一桩疑问未决：是买一个小圆面包，仗着它走回家去，还是把剩下的几文钱花在公共马车上，能坐多远算多远，剩下的路再走回去。

===Original===
Then, suddenly, as if out of the depths of despair, arose in him an assurance of help on the way to him, and with it a strength to look in the face the worst that could befall him; he might at least starve in patience. Therewith he drew himself up, crossed the street to the corner of the Mansion House, and got into an omnibus waiting there.

===Chinese===
就在这时，仿佛从绝望的深渊里，忽然在他心头升起了一种确信：援手正在路上向他走来；伴随这确信而来的是一种力量，使他敢于直面那最坏的可能；他至少可以从容地挨饿。于是他挺直身板，穿过街，走到市长官邸（Mansion House）的拐角，登上了一辆停在那里的公共马车。

===Original===
If only he could creep into his grave and have done! Why should that hostelry of refuge stand always shut? Surely he was but walking in his own funeral! Were not the mourners already going about the street before ever the silver cord was loosed or the golden bowl broken? Might he not now at length feel at liberty to end the life he had ceased to value? But there was Annie! He would go home to her; she would comfort him—yes, she would die with him! There was no other escape; there was no sign of coming deliverance. All was black within and around them. That was the rain on the gravestones. He was in a hearse, on his way to the churchyard. There the mourners were already gathered. They were before him, waiting his arrival. No! He would go home to Annie! He would not be a coward soldier! He would not kill himself to escape the enemy! He would stand up to the Evil One, and take his blows without flinching. He and his Annie would take them together, and fight to the last. Then, if they must die, it was well, and would be better.

===Chinese===
要是他能钻进自己的坟里、一了百了就好了！为什么那座避难的客栈总是关着门？他岂不是正行在自己的葬礼之上！送葬的人岂不是早在银链还未松开、金碗还未破裂之前，便已上街走动了？他如今难道还不能算得了自由，去结束这一条他已不再看重的性命？可是还有安妮！他要回到她身边；她会安慰他——是的，她会与他同死！再无别的出路；也看不到一丝得救的迹象。里里外外都是一片黑。那落在墓碑上的，便是雨。他正坐在一辆灵车里，往教堂墓地去。送葬的人已在那里聚齐。他们抢在他前头，等着他到。不！他要回到安妮身边！他不做个怯懦的兵卒！他不为躲避敌人而自寻短见！他要挺身迎战那恶者，毫不畏缩地挨他的拳脚。他和他的安妮要一同挨着，战到最后一刻。那时，倘若他们必死，那也很好，且会更好。

===Original===
But alas! what if the obligation of a live soul went farther than this life? What if a man was bound, by the fact that he lived, to live on, and do everything possible to keep the life alive in him? There his heart sank, and the depths of the sea covered it! Did God require of him that, sooner than die, he should beg the food to keep him alive? Would he be guilty of forsaking his post, if he but refused to ask, and waited for Death? Was he bound to beg? If he was, he must begin at once by refusing to accept the smallest credit! To all they must tell the truth of their circumstances, and refuse aught but charity. But was there not something yet he could try before begging? He had had a good education, had both knowledge and the power of imparting it; this was still worth money in the world’s market. And doubtless therein his friend could do something for him.

===Chinese===
可是，唉！倘若一个活着的灵魂所负的责任，超出了这一生之外呢？倘若一个人，仅凭他活着这一事实，便有义务活下去、并尽一切可能保住他里头那点生气呢？一念及此，他的心便沉了下去，海水的深渊便把它盖住！神难道要他宁愿乞讨求食来保命、也不肯死？他若只是不肯开口、只等死亡来临，难道便是擅离职守？他是有乞讨的义务吗？若有，他便必须从拒绝哪怕是最小的赊欠开始做起！对一切人，他们都必须如实相告自己的处境，除慈善之外一毫不取。可是乞讨之前，难道再没有别的可试了吗？他受过良好的教育，既有知识，又有传授知识的能力；这在世间的市集上仍值几个钱。而在这方面，他的朋友想必也能为他出一点力。

===Original===
Therewithal his new dread was gone; one possibility was yet left him in store! To his wife he must go, and talk the thing over with her. He had still, he believed, threepence in his pocket to pay for the omnibus.

===Chinese===
如此一来，他新添的那份畏惧消散了；尚有一条后路留给他！他必得到妻子那里去，把这事同她好生商量。他想，他口袋里还剩一枚三便士，够付车钱。

===Original===
It began to move; and then first, waking up, he saw that he had seated himself between a poor woman and a little girl, evidently her daughter.

===Chinese===
马车动了起来；这时他才如梦初醒，发觉自己坐在一个穷苦妇人和一个小女孩之间，那女孩显然是她的女儿。

===Original===
“I am very sorry to incommode you, ma’am,” he said apologetically to the white-faced woman, whose little tartan shawl scarcely covered her shoulders, painfully conscious of his dripping condition, as he took off his hat, and laid it on the floor between his equally soaking feet. But, instead of moving away from him to a drier position beyond, the woman, with a feeble smile, moved closer up to him, saying to her daughter on his other side:

===Chinese===
"很抱歉挤着您了，太太，"他满怀歉意地对那面色苍白的妇人说——她那条小小的格子呢披肩勉强盖住肩膀——他一边痛苦地意识到自己浑身滴水的狼狈，一边摘下帽子，搁在他那同样湿透的两脚之间的地板上。可那妇人不但没有躲到更干爽的位置上去，反倒带着一丝虚弱的笑，更挨近了他一些，对坐在他另一边的女儿说：

===Original===
“Sit closer to the gentleman, Jessie, and help to keep him warm. She’s quite clean, sir,” she added. “We have plenty of water in our place, and I gave her a bath myself this morning, because we were going to the hospital to see my husband. He had a bad accident yesterday, but thank God! not so bad as it might have been. I’m afraid you’re feeling very cold, sir,” she added, for Hector had just given an involuntary shiver.

===Chinese===
"挨这位先生坐近些，杰西（Jessie），帮着给他暖暖。她挺干净的，先生，"她又道，"我们那儿水多得很，今儿早上还是我亲手给她洗了个澡，因为我们要去医院看我丈夫。他昨天出了桩倒霉的事故，可感谢神！还不算顶糟。我看您挺冷的吧，先生，"她又补了一句，因为赫克托刚不由自主地打了个寒战。

===Original===
“My husband he’s a bricklayer,” she went on; “he has been in good work, and I have a few shillings in hand, thank God! Times are sure to mend, for they seldom turns out so bad as they looks.”

===Chinese===
"我丈夫是个砌砖的，"她接着说，"他一直活计不错，我手里头还攒着几个先令，感谢神！世道总会好起来的，因为事情难得有看上去那么糟。"

===Original===
Involuntarily Hector’s hand moved to his trouser pocket, but dropped by his side as he remembered the fare. She saw his movement, and broke into a sad little laugh.

===Chinese===
赫克托的手不由自主地伸向裤袋，可一记起车钱，便又垂在身侧。她看见他这个动作，发出一声小小的、带着几分辛酸的笑。

===Original===
“Don’t mistake me, sir,” she resumed. “I told you true when I said I wasn’t without money; and, before the pinch comes, wages, I dare say, will show their color again. Besides, our week’s rent is paid. And he’s in good quarters, poor fellow, though with a bad pain to keep him company, I’m afraid.”

===Chinese===
"别误会我，先生，"她又说，"我说不缺钱是真话；等到紧日子来之前，工钱我想必又会露脸的。再说，我们这周的房租也已经付了。他住的地方也不错，可怜的人，只是怕有一份痛处陪着他罢了。"

===Original===
“Where do you live?” asked Hector. “But,” he went on, “why should I ask? I am as poor as you—poorer, perhaps, for I have no trade to fall back upon. But I have a good wife like you, and I don’t doubt she’ll think of something.”

===Chinese===
"你们住在哪儿？"赫克托问。"可是，"他又说，"我何必问呢？我和你一样穷——也许更穷，因为我连一门手艺都无从依托。不过我也有一位像你这样的好妻子，我相信她总会想出点什么来。"

===Original===
“Trust to that, sir! A good woman like I’m sure she is’ll be sure to think of many a thing before she’ll give in. My husband, he was brought up to religion, and he always says there’s one as know’s and don’t forget.” But now the omnibus had reached the spot where Hector must leave it. He got up, fumbling for his threepenny-piece, but failed to find it.

===Chinese===
"那您就放心吧，先生！像您太太这么一位好女人，在认输之前，准能想出许多许多来。我丈夫他自小受教信教，他总说有这么一位，是知道、也不忘事的。"可这时马车已到了赫克托该下车的地方。他站起身，摸那枚三便士，却摸不着。

===Original===
“Don’t forget your hat, sir; it’ll come all right when it’s dry,” said the woman, as she handed it to him. But he stood, the conductor waiting, and seemed unable to take it from her: he could not find the little coin!

===Chinese===
"别忘了您的帽子，先生；等干了就没事了。"妇人把帽子递给他时说。可他站着，售票员在一旁等着，他似乎接不过那帽子：他找不到那枚小铜子儿！

===Original===
“There, there, sir!” interposed the woman, as she made haste and handed him three coppers; “I have plenty for both of us, and wish for your sake it was a hundred times as much. Take it, sir,” she insisted, while Hector yet hesitated and fumbled; “you won’t refuse such a small service from another of God’s creatures! I mean it well.”

===Chinese===
"喏，喏，先生！"那妇人插嘴道，赶忙递给他三个铜板，"我们俩的我这儿多着呢，为您的缘故，我倒巴不得这是一百倍才好。拿着吧，先生，"赫克托还在犹豫、还在摸，她却执意道，"同是神的造物，这么一点小小的相帮，您总不至于推辞吧！我是一片真心。"

===Original===
But the conductor, apparently affected with the same generosity, pushed back the woman’s hand, saying, “No, no, ma’am, thank you! The gentleman’ll pay me another day.”

===Chinese===
可那售票员显然受了同一份慷慨的感动，把妇人的手推回去，说："不，不，太太，谢谢您！这位先生改日再付我吧。"

===Original===
Hector pulled out an old silver watch, and offered it.

===Chinese===
赫克托掏出一只旧银表，递过去。

===Original===
“I cannot be so sure about that,” he said. “Better take this: it’s of little use to me now.”

===Chinese===
"这个我可说不准，"他说，"还是拿这个去吧：它现在对我没多大用了。"

===Original===
“I’ll be damned if I do!” cried the conductor fiercely, and down he jumped and stood ready to help Hector from the omnibus.

===Chinese===
"我才不要呢！"售票员厉声喊道，跳下车，站好，准备扶赫克托下车。

===Original===
But his kindness was more than Hector could stand; he walked away, unable to thank him.

===Chinese===
可这份好意赫克托实在承受不住；他走开了，连谢也谢不出来。

===Original===
“I wonder now,” muttered the conductor to himself when Hector was gone, “if that was a put-up job between him and the woman? I don’t think so. Anyhow, it’s no great loss to anybody. I won’t put it down; the company’ll have to cover that.”

===Chinese===
"我倒要琢磨琢磨，"赫克托走后，售票员自言自语地咕哝，"那是不是他和那妇人串通好了的一出？我看不像。管它呢，反正谁也亏不了多少。这笔我不记上；公司只好认了。"

===Original===
Hector turned down a street that led westward, drying his eyes, and winking hard to make them swallow the tears which sought to hide from him a spectacle that was calling aloud to be seen. For lo! the street-end was filled with the glory of a magnificent rainbow. All across its opening stretched and stood the wide arch of a wonderful rainbow. Hector could not see the sun; he saw only what it was making; and the old story came back to him, how the men of ancient time took the heavenly bow for a promise that there should no more be such a flood as again to destroy the world. And therefore even now the poets called the rainbow the bow of hope.

===Chinese===
赫克托拐进一条朝西的街，擦干眼睛，使劲眨巴着，好叫它们把泪水咽回去——那泪水正要遮住他眼前一幕大声呼唤着要人去看的景象。原来，看哪！街道那一头正满溢着一道壮丽彩虹的荣光。一道奇妙的彩虹，它宽阔的拱身横跨、伫立在那街口的开敞处。赫克托看不见太阳；他只看见太阳所正在造就的东西；那古老的故事便又回到他心头：古时的人曾把天上这道弓视作一个应许——再不会有那样一场洪水来毁灭世界了。所以即便到了今天，诗人们仍把彩虹唤作希望之弓。

===Original===
Nor, even in these days of question and unbelief, is it matter of wonder that, at sight of the harmony of blended and mingling, yet always individual, and never confused colors, and notwithstanding his knowledge of optics, and of how the supreme unity of the light was secerned into its decreed chord, the imaginative faith of the troubled poet should so work in him as to lift his head for a moment above the waters of that other flood that threatened to overwhelm his microcosm, and the bow should seem to him a new promise, given to him then and individually, of the faithfulness of an unseen Power of whom he had been assured, by one whom he dared not doubt, that He numbered the very hairs of his head. Once more his spirit rose upon the wave of a hope which he could neither logically justify nor dare to refuse; for hope is hope whencesoever it spring, and needs no justification of its self-existence or of its sudden marvelous birth. The very hope was in itself enough for itself. And now he was near his home; his Annie was waiting for him; and in another instant his misery would be shared and comforted by her! He was walking toward the wonder-sign in the heavens. But even as he walked with it full in view, he saw it gradually fade and dissolve into the sky, until not a thread of its loveliness remained to show where it had spanned the infinite with its promise of good. And yet, was not the sky itself a better thing, and the promise of a yet greater good? He must walk onward yet, in tireless hope! And the resolve itself endured—or fading, revived, and came again, and ever yet again.

===Chinese===
即便在这个怀疑与不信的时代，也不难理解：当他看见那彼此交融、却始终各各有别、从不混淆的色彩所构成的和谐，尽管他懂得光学，懂得光那至高的统一如何被分拨成它命定的和弦，这位烦乱诗人的想象之信，仍能在他身上如此作工，以至在一瞬间把他的头颅托出那另一场要吞没他这小天地的洪水之上，让那道弓在他看来成了一道新的应许——一道此时此地、单单赐给他个人的应许——出自一位看不见的大能者的信实；而这位大能者，他曾由一位他不敢置疑者处得到保证，是连他的头发都一一数过的。他的心再一次乘着希望的浪头升起，这希望他既不能从道理上为之辩白，也不敢加以拒斥；因为希望无论从何处涌出都是希望，无须为其自存、为其奇妙的骤然降生辩解。这希望本身，于其自身便已足够。而如今他已离家不远；他的安妮正在等他；再过片刻，他的苦楚便要由她分担、由她抚慰！他正朝着天上那奇妙的记号走去。可就在他走着、那记号尽收眼底之际，他看着它渐渐淡去，消融在天空中，直到它那一缕可爱的踪迹一丝不剩，再无从指认它曾在何处以其美好之应许横跨过无穷。然而，那片天空本身岂不是更美的东西，所应许的岂不是更大的善？他还得走下去，怀着不倦的希望！而这决心本身便耐住了——纵或淡去，又会复兴，再度归来，再再度地归来。

===Original===
For ere he had passed the few yards that lay between him and Annie yet another wonder befell: as if the rainbow had condensed, and taken shape as it melted away, there on the pathway, in the thickening twilight of the swift-descending November night, stood a creature, surely not of the night, but rather of the early morn, a lovely little child—whether wandered from the open door of some neighboring house, or left by the vanished rainbow, how was he to tell? Endeavoring afterward to recall every point of her appearance, he could remember nothing of her feet, or even of the frock she wore. Only her face remained to him, with its cerulean eyes—the eyes of Annie, looking up from under the cloud of her dark hair, which also was Annie’s. She looked then as she stood, in his memory of her, as if she were saying, “I trust in you; will you not trust in Him who made the rainbow?” For a moment he seemed to stand regarding her, but even while he looked he must have forgotten that she was there before him, for when again he knew that he saw her, though he did not seem ever to have looked away from her, she had changed in the gathering darkness to the phantasm of a daisy, which still gazed up in his face trustingly, and, indeed, went with him to his own door, seeming all the time to say, “It was no child; it was me you saw, and nothing but me; only I saw the sun—I mean, the man that was making the rainbow.” And never more could he in his mind separate the child, whom I cannot but think he had verily seen, from the daisy which certainly he had not seen, except in the atmosphere of his troubled and confused soul.

===Chinese===
因为就在他走完横在他与安妮之间那几码路之前，又一桩奇事降临了：仿佛那彩虹凝缩了，在消融之际化作了人形——在那十一月夜急急降临、愈来愈浓的暮色里，小径上站着一个小生灵，她绝非属这夜的，倒属那清晨，一个可爱的小孩——是从近处哪户人家敞开的门里溜出来的，还是由那消隐的彩虹遗下的，他又从何得知呢？日后他竭力回想她模样的每一处细节，却连她的脚、连她穿的衣裳都记不起来。留在他记忆里的，只有她的脸，和她那双碧蓝的眼睛——那是安妮的眼睛，从她那一片乌云般的黑发下仰望上来，那头发也是安妮的。在他对她的记忆里，她那样站在那里，仿佛在说："我信你；你难道不愿信那造彩虹的祂吗？"有那么一刻，他似乎站着端详她，可就在他看着的当儿，他一定已经忘了她就在他眼前，因为等他再度意识到自己看见了她时——虽然他似乎从未把目光从她身上移开——她在渐浓的夜色里已化作一株雏菊的幻影，那雏菊仍信赖地仰脸凝视着他，并且当真伴他走到自家门前，一路上仿佛都在说："那不是孩子；你看见的是我，除了我别无其它；只是我看见了太阳——我的意思是，那正在造彩虹的人。"而从此，在他心里，他再也不能把那个孩子——我不得不相信他确实看见了那个孩子——同那株雏菊分开，那雏菊他当然是未曾见过的，只不过是在他那烦乱而迷惘的灵魂的氛围里见过罢了。

===Original===
It may help my reader to understand its confusion if I recall to him the fact that Hector had that day eaten nothing. Nor must my wife reader think hardly of Annie for having let him leave the house without any food, for he had stolen softly away, and closed the door as softly behind him, thinking how merrily they would eat together when he came back with his good news. And now he was bringing nothing to her but the story of a poor woman and her child who had warmed him, and of an omnibus-conductor who had trusted him for his fare, and of a rainbow and a child and a daisy.

===Chinese===
如果我提醒读者一句：赫克托那天粒米未进，或有助于读者体谅他这份迷惘。也请我那位女读者莫要苛责安妮竟让他空着肚子出门，因为他是悄悄溜出去的，门也是轻轻带上的，心里盘算着等他带着好消息回来，他俩要怎样欢欢喜喜地一道吃饭。而如今他带给她的，不过是一个穷苦妇人和她孩子给他取暖的故事，一个公共马车售票员信他、让他赊车钱的故事，一道彩虹、一个孩子和一株雏菊的故事。

===Original===
“Oh, you naughty, naughty dear!” cried Annie, as she threw herself into his arms, rejoicing. But at sight of his worn and pallid face the smile faded from hers, and she thought, “What can have befallen him?”

===Chinese===
"哦，你这个淘气、淘气的宝贝！"安妮喊着，欢欢喜喜地扑进他怀里。可一见他那憔悴苍白的脸，她脸上的笑便褪了去，心想："他遭了什么事？"

===Original===
His lip quivered, and, seeking with a watery smile to reassure her, he gave way and burst into tears. Unmanly of him, no doubt, but what is a man to do when he cannot help it? And where is a man to weep if not on his wife’s bosom? Call this behavior un-English, if you will; for, indeed, Hector was in many ways other than English, and, I protest, English ways are not all human. But I will not allow that it manifested any weakness, or necessarily involved shame to him; the best of men, and the strongest—yea, the one Man whose soul harbored not an atom of self-pity—upon one occasion wept, I think because he could not persuade the women whom he loved and would fain console to take comfort in his Father. Annie, for one reverent moment, turned her head aside, then threw her arms about him, and hid her glowing face in his bosom.

===Chinese===
他的嘴唇颤了一下，想含着泪笑一笑来安抚她，却终于撑不住，泪如雨下。这未免有失男子气概，可一个人撑不住时又能如何？一个男人除了妻子的胸怀，又能在哪儿哭呢？你尽可说这举动不合英国人的做派；因为赫克托在许多方面本就与英国人不同，而我要声言，英国人的做派并非都合乎人情。但我不容许说这显出了什么软弱，或必然牵连到他的羞耻；那最好的、最强的人——是的，那独一的、灵魂里没有半星自怜的人——也曾有一次哭了，我想，是因为他不能说服他所爱、又愿意安慰的妇女们从他父那里得安慰。安妮恭敬地把头偏过一边，片刻之后，便张开双臂搂住他，把发烫的脸埋进他怀里。

===Original===
“There’s only me in the house, dear,” she said, and led the way to their room.

===Chinese===
"家里只有我一个，亲爱的，"她说，领着他往他们屋里去。

===Original===
When they reached it, she closed the door, and turned to him.

===Chinese===
进了屋，她关上门，转身向他。

===Original===
“So they won’t take your story?” she said, assuming the fact, with a sad, sunny smile.

===Chinese===
"那么他们不肯收你的故事了？"她说，径自把这事实当作既定，带着一个忧伤却明媚的微笑。

===Original===
“They refused it absolutely.”

===Chinese===
"他们一口回绝了。"

===Original===
“Well, never mind! I shall go out charing tomorrow. You have no notion how strong I am. It is well for you I have never wanted to beat you. Seriously, I believe I am much stronger than you have the least notion of. There! Feel that arm—I should let you feel it another way, only I am afraid of hurting you.”

===Chinese===
"好啦，别放在心上！我明儿出去给人打杂做短工。你不知道我多壮实。算你走运，我从没想过要揍你。说真的，我相信我比你所以为的要壮实得多。来！摸摸这条胳膊——我本该让你换种法子摸摸的，只怕弄疼了你。"

===Original===
She had turned up the sleeve of her dress, and uncovered a grandly developed arm, white as milk, and blossoming in a large, splendidly formed hand. Then playfully, but oh! so tenderly, with the under and softest part of her arm she fondled his face, rubbing it over first one, then the other cheek, and ended with both arms round his neck, her hands folding his head to her bosom.

===Chinese===
她已经卷起衣袖，露出一条发育得极好的胳膊，白如牛乳，末端开出一朵大大的、形态极佳的手。然后，她带着玩闹、可又是何等温柔！用胳膊底下最柔软的那一处抚弄他的脸，先擦过这一边脸颊、再擦过那一边，末了双臂环住他的脖子，两手把他的头按到自己怀里。

===Original===
“Wife! wife!” faltered Hector, with difficulty controlling himself; “my strong, beautiful wife! To think of your marrying me for this!”

===Chinese===
"妻子！妻子！"赫克托颤声说，好不容易才按捺住自己，"我这个壮实、美丽的妻子！想想你竟是为这才嫁我的！"

===Original===
“Hector,” answered Annie, drawing herself back with dignity, “do you dare to pity me? That would be to insult me! As if I was not fit to be your wife when doing *everything* for my mother! There are thousands of Scotch girls that would only be proud to take my place, poor as you are—and you couldn’t be much poorer—and serve you, without being your wife, as I have the honor and pride to be! But, my blessed man, I do believe you have eaten nothing today; and here am I fancying myself your wife, and letting you stand there empty, instead of bestirring myself to get you some supper! What a shame! Why, you are actually dying with hunger!” she cried, searching his face with pitiful eyes.

===Chinese===
"赫克托，"安妮答道，端庄地往后一撤身，"你竟敢可怜我？那便是侮辱我！仿佛我做*一切*为我母亲的时候，就不配做你的妻子似的！便在你这般穷——你也穷不到哪儿去——的境地里，成千上万的苏格兰姑娘都只以顶替我的位置、做你的仆役为荣，还不必像我这样，以做你的妻子为荣为傲呢！可是，我这个人中之福的人，我相信你今天八成什么也没吃；而我却在这里自命为你的妻子，让你空着肚子站在那儿，不赶紧给自己张罗点晚饭！真是不像话！怎么，你简直是快饿死了！"她喊着，用怜惜的目光细细地察看他的脸。

===Original===
“On the contrary, I am not in the least hungry,” protested Hector.

===Chinese===
"恰恰相反，我一点也不饿。"赫克托辩道。

===Original===
“Then you must be hungry at once, sir. I will go and bring you something the very sight of which will make you hungry.”

===Chinese===
"那么你必须马上饿起来，先生。我去给你拿点东西来，叫你一瞧见就饿。"

===Original===
“But you have no money, Annie; and, not being able to pay, we must go without. Come, we will go to bed.”

===Chinese===
"可你没有钱啊，安妮；既然付不起，我们就只好不吃。来，我们睡吧。"

===Original===
“Yes, I am ready; I had a good breakfast. But you have had nothing all day. And for money, do you know Miss Hamper, the dressmaker, actually offered to lend me a shilling, and I took it. Here it is. You see, I was so sure you would bring money home that I thought we *might* run that much farther into debt. So I got you two fresh eggs and such a lovely little white loaf. Besides, I have just thought of something else we could get a little money for—that dainty chemise my mother made for me with her own hands when we were going to be married. I will take it to the pawnbroker tomorrow.”

===Chinese===
"好，我愿意；我好好吃了一顿早饭。可你一整天什么也没吃。至于钱，你知道那个做女红的汉珀小姐（Miss Hamper），竟主动要借我一先令，我就接了。在这儿呢。你瞧，我原本那么笃定你会带钱回来，便想着我们不妨再多欠这一点。所以我给你买了两个鲜鸡蛋，还有那么可爱的一个小白面包。再说，我刚刚还想到一桩别的东西可以换几个钱——我母亲在我们快成亲时亲手给我做的那件精致的女衣。我明儿拿它上当铺去。"

===Original===
“I was never in a pawnshop, Annie. I don’t think I should know how to set about it.”

===Chinese===
"我从没进过当铺，安妮。我怕是连怎么着手都不知道。"

===Original===
“*You!*” cried Annie, with a touch of scorn. “Do you think I would trust a man with it? No; that’s a woman’s work. Why, you would let the fellow offer you half it was worth—and you would take it too. I shall show it to Mrs. Whitmore: *she* will know what I ought to get for it. She’s had to do the thing herself—too often, poor thing!”

===Chinese===
"*你*！"安妮喊道，带着一丝轻蔑，"你以为我会把这事交给一个男人？不；那是女人的活儿。怎么，你会让人家给你半价——你还会接呢。我要先拿给惠特莫尔太太（Mrs. Whitmore）看看：*她*才知道我该当多少钱。她自己干这档子事——次数太多了，可怜的人！"

===Original===
“It would be like tearing my heart out.”

===Chinese===
"那简直是把我的心挖出来。"

===Original===
“What! to part with my pretty chemise. Hector, dear, you must not be foolish! What does it matter, so long as we are not cheating anybody? The pawnshop is a most honorable and useful institution. No one is the worse for it, and many a one the better. Even the tradespeople will be a trifle the better. I shall be quite proud to know that I have a pawn-ticket in my pocket to fall back upon. Oh, there’s that old silk dress your mother sent me—I do believe that would bring more. It is in good condition, and looks quite respectable. If Eve had got into a scrape like ours, she would have been helpless, poor thing, not having anything *to put away*—that is the right word, I believe. There is really nothing disgraceful about it. Come now, dear, and eat your eggs—I’m afraid you must do without butter. I always preferred a piece of dry bread with an egg—you get the true taste of the egg so much better. One day or another we must part with everything. It is sure to come. Sooner or later, what does that matter? ‘The readiness is all,’ as Hamlet says. Death, or the pawnshop, signifies nothing. ‘Since no man has aught of what he leaves, what is it to leave betimes?’ We do but forestall the grave for one brief hour with the pawnshop.”

===Chinese===
"什么！心疼我那件漂亮的女衣。赫克托，亲爱的，你可不许犯傻！只要我们没坑骗任何人，那又有什么要紧？当铺是个极其体面、极其有用的去处。谁也不会因为它而更糟，许多人倒是因它而更好。便连店家也会因此略略好过一点。我口袋里揣着一张当票做后盾，我还颇感自豪呢。哦，还有你母亲送我的那件旧绸裙——我相信那个当得更多。它成色不错，看着也体面。要是夏娃（Eve）遭了我们这样的难，她可就束手无策了，可怜的人，因为她根本没有什么东西*可以拿去当*——我想这是正确的说法。这事其实毫不丢人。来，亲爱的，吃你的鸡蛋吧——只怕你得将就着没黄油。我向来喜欢就着干面包吃鸡蛋——这样能更好地尝到鸡蛋的真味。迟早有一天，我们总得把样样东西都脱手。那一天是一定会来的。早晚又有什么关系？'准备好，便是一切'，哈姆雷特（Hamlet）这么说。死也好，当铺也好，都算不得什么。'既然人离开时一无所有，早走一步又何妨？'我们不过是借当铺把坟茔提前了一小会儿罢了。"

===Original===
“You deserve to have married Epictetus, Annie, you brave woman, instead of Xantippe!”

===Chinese===
"安妮，你这个勇敢的女人，你配嫁给爱比克泰德（Epictetus），而不是珊提珀（Xantippe）！"

===Original===
“I prefer you, Hector.”

===Chinese===
"我宁愿要你，赫克托。"

===Original===
“But what might you have said if he had asked you, and you had heard me bemoaning the pawnshop?”

===Chinese===
"可要是当初是他来求你，又听见我在这儿哀叹当铺，你会怎么说？"

===Original===
“Ah, then, indeed! But, in the meantime, we will go to bed and wait there for tomorrow. Is it not a lovely thing to know that God is thinking about you? He will bring us to *our desired haven*, Hector, dearest!”

===Chinese===
"啊，那可就当真另说了！不过眼下，我们且去睡，躺在床上等明天。知道神正惦念着你，这岂不是一桩美事？他会把我们领到*我们所愿去的港湾*的，最亲爱的赫克托！"

===Original===
So in their sadness they laid them down. Annie opened her arms and took Hector to her bosom. There he sighed himself to sleep; and God put His arms about them both, and kept them asleep until the morning.

===Chinese===
于是他们在忧愁中躺下了。安妮张开双臂，把赫克托搂进怀里。他在那里叹着气，睡了过去；神便伸出双臂把他们俩一同环绕，护着他们安睡直到天明。

===Original===
> And in this love, more than in bed, I rest.

===Chinese===
> 我安息于这份爱里，胜于安息于卧榻之上。

===Original===
Annie was the first to spring up and begin to dress herself, pondering in her mind as she did so whether to go first to the pawnbroker’s or to the baker, to ask him to recommend her as a charwoman. She would tell him just the truth—that she must in future work for her daily bread. Then Hector rose and dressed himself.

===Chinese===
安妮先一跃而起，开始穿衣，一边穿一边在心里盘算：是先去当铺，还是先去面包师傅那儿，请他把自己推荐出去做短工。她要把实情原原本本告诉他——今后她得凭自己的气力挣饭吃了。随后赫克托也起来穿好衣裳。

===Original===
“Oh, Annie!” he said, as he did so, “is it gone, that awful misery of last night in the omnibus? It seemed, as I jolted along, as if God had forgotten one of the creatures he had made, and that one was me; or, worse, that he thought of me, and would not move to help me! And why do I feel now as if He had help for me somewhere near waiting for me? I think I will go and see a man who lives somewhere close by, and find out if he is the same I used to know at St. Andrews; if he be the same, he may know of something I could try for.”

===Chinese===
"哦，安妮！"他边穿边说，"昨晚在公共马车里那种可怕的苦楚，过去了么？我一路颠簸着，仿佛神已经忘了他所造的众生中的一个，那一个便是我；或者更糟，他惦着我，却不肯动一动来帮我！可为什么我现在又觉得，他正把一份帮助预备在什么地方、就在近处等着我呢？我想去看看一个住得不远的人，看看他是不是我从前在圣安德鲁斯（St. Andrews）认识的那个；要是同一个人，他也许知道有什么我可以一试的事。"

===Original===
“Do,” replied Annie. “I will go with you, and on the way call at the grocer’s—I think he will be the best to ask if he knows of any family that wants a charwoman or could give me any sort of work. There’s more than one kind of thing I could turn my hand to—needlework, for instance. I could make a child’s frock as well, I believe, as a second-rate dressmaker. Can you tell me who was the first tailor, Hector? It was God himself. He made coats of skins for Adam and his wife.”

===Chinese===
"去吧，"安妮应道，"我跟你一道，路上再拐去杂货铺——我想他那里最该问问，他知不知道有哪户人家要个打杂的女佣、或能给我什么活计干。能让我上手的事不止一桩——比如针线活。我相信我做起一件孩子的小罩衫来，不比二流的裁缝差。你能不能告诉我，赫克托，头一个裁缝是谁？是神自己。他为亚当和他妻子做了皮衣。"

===Original===
“Quite right, dear. You may well try your hand—as I know you have done many a time already. And, if I can get hold of ever so young a pupil, I shall be glad even to teach him his letters. We must try anything and everything. We are long past being fastidious, I hope.”

===Chinese===
"一点不错，亲爱的。你大可一试身手——我知道你已经试过许多回了。再说，我只要能抓到一个学生，哪怕年纪再小，我就是教他认字也甘心。什么我们都要试一试。我想，我们早过了挑三拣四的份儿了。"

===Original===
He turned and went on with his toilet.

===Chinese===
他转过身，继续梳洗穿戴。

===Original===
“Oh, Hector,” said Annie suddenly, and walked to the mantelpiece, “I am so sorry! Here is a letter that came for you yesterday. I did not care to open it, though you have often told me to open any letters I pleased. The fact is, I forgot all about it, I believe, because I was so unhappy at your going away without breakfast. Or perhaps it was that I was frightened at its black border. I really can’t tell now why I did not open it.”

===Chinese===
"哦，赫克托，"安妮忽然说，走到壁炉台跟前，"真对不起！这儿有一封昨天送来给你的信。我不想拆，虽说你常跟我说，我看哪封顺眼就拆哪封。说实在的，我想我是把它全然忘了，因为你空着肚子出门，我心里太难受了。也许是那信的黑边把我吓着了。我现在真说不上当时为什么没拆它。"

===Original===
With little interest and less hope, Hector took the letter—black-bordered and black-sealed—opened it, and glanced carelessly at the signature, while Annie stood looking at him, in the hope merely that he would find in it no fresh trouble—some forgotten bill perhaps!

===Chinese===
赫克托提不起多大兴致，更无多少指望，接过那封信——黑边、黑火漆封口——拆开，心不在焉地扫了一眼署名，安妮则站在一旁望着他，只盼他别从中再读出什么新的烦恼——也许是一笔忘掉的账单！

===Original===
She saw his face change, and his eyes grow fixed. A moment more and the letter dropped in the fender. He stood an instant, then fell on his knees, and threw up his hands.

===Chinese===
她看见他的脸色变了，眼神定了下来。再一刻，那封信落进了炉挡里。他站了一瞬，然后跪倒在地，双手高举。

===Original===
“What is it, darling?” she cried, beginning to tremble.

===Chinese===
"怎么了，亲爱的？"她喊道，开始颤抖起来。

===Original===
“Only five hundred pounds!” he answered, and burst into an hysterical laugh.

===Chinese===
"不过是五百镑罢了！"他答道，随即发出一阵狂笑。

===Original===
“Impossible!” cried Annie.

===Chinese===
"不可能！"安妮喊道。

===Original===
“Who *can* have played us such a cruel trick?” said Hector feebly.

===Chinese===
"到底是谁跟我们开了这么个残忍的玩笑？"赫克托有气无力地说。

===Original===
“It’s no trick, Hector!” exclaimed Annie. “There’s nobody would have the heart to do it. Let *me* see the letter.”

===Chinese===
"这不是玩笑，赫克托！"安妮喊道，"谁也不会有这份心。让我看看这封信。"

===Original===
She almost caught it from his hands as he picked it from the fender, and looked at the signature.

===Chinese===
他从炉挡里把信捡起来时，她几乎是一把从他手中夺了过来，去看那署名。

===Original===
“Hale & Hale?” she read. “I never heard of them!”

===Chinese===
"黑尔父子（Hale & Hale）？"她念道，"我从没听过这名字！"

===Original===
“No, nor anyone else, I dare say,” answered Hector.

===Chinese===
"没有，我想别人也没听过。"赫克托答道。

===Original===
“Let us see the address at the top,” said Annie.

===Chinese===
"看看信头上那地址。"安妮说。

===Original===
“There it is—Philpot Lane.”

===Chinese===
"在这儿呢——菲尔波特巷（Philpot Lane）。"

===Original===
“Where is that? I don’t believe there is such a place!”

===Chinese===
"那在哪儿？我不信有这么个地方！"

===Original===
“Oh, yes, there is; I’ve seen it—somewhere in the City, I believe. But let us read the letter. I saw only the figures. I confess I was foolish enough at first to fancy somebody had sent us five hundred pounds!”

===Chinese===
"哦，有，有的；我见过——好像就在城里的什么地方。可我们还是读读这信吧。我刚才只看见了那笔数目。我承认，我起先竟傻到以为有人给我们送来了五百镑！"

===Original===
“And why not?” cried Annie. “I am sure there’s no one more in need of it.”

===Chinese===
"为什么不能？"安妮喊道，"我相信再没有谁比我们更需要它了。"

===Original===
“That’s just why not,” answered Hector. “Did you ever know a rich man leave his money to a poor relation? Oh, I hope it does not mean that my father is gone. He may have left us a trifle. Only he could not have had so much to leave to anybody. I know he loved you, Annie.”

===Chinese===
"正因为如此才不能。"赫克托答道，"你可曾见哪个有钱人把他的钱留给一个穷亲戚？哦，我希望这并不意味着我父亲走了。他也许给我们留了一点小意思。只是他不可能有那么多留给谁。我知道他是爱你的，安妮。"

===Original===
In the meantime Annie had been doing the one sensible thing—reading the letter, and now she stood pondering it.

===Chinese===
这时安妮在做着唯一明智的事——读信，现在她站着，细细地琢磨它。

===Original===
“I have it, Hector. He always uses good people to do his kindnesses. Don’t you remember me telling you about the little old lady in Graham’s shop the time your book came out?”

===Chinese===
"我明白了，赫克托。他总是用好人来行他的善事。你不记得我跟你说过，你的书出来那阵子，格雷厄姆书店里那个小老太太吗？"

===Original===
“Yes, Annie; I wasn’t likely to forget that; it was my love for you that made me able to write the poem. Ah, but how soon was the twenty pounds I got for it spent, though I thought it riches then!”

===Chinese===
"记得，安妮；那我不容易忘的；正是对你的爱，才使我得以写出那首诗。唉，可我因此到手的二十镑又花得多快啊，尽管那时我还当它是笔财富呢！"

===Original===
“So it was—and so it is!” cried Annie, half laughing, but crying outright. “It’s just that same little old lady. She was so delighted with the book, and with you for writing it, that she put you down at once in her will for five hundred pounds, believing it would help people to trust in God.”

===Chinese===
"正是如此——如今也是！"安妮喊着，半哭半笑，"就是那同一个小老太太。她是那样喜欢那本书，喜欢你写了它，便当即在遗嘱里给你记下了五百镑，相信这会帮助人信靠神。"

===Original===
“And here was I distrusting so much that I was nearly ready to kill myself. Only I thought it would be such a terrible shock to you, my precious! It would have been to tell God to his face that I knew he would not help me. I am sure now that he is never forgetting, though he seems to have forgotten. There was that letter lying in the dark through all the hours of the long night, while we slept in the weariness of sorrow and fear, not knowing what the light was bringing us. God is good!”

===Chinese===
"而我却怀疑到那般地步，几乎要自寻短见。只是想到那对你将是一记多么可怕的打击，我的宝贝！那样做，便是当面告诉神，我知道他不会帮我。如今我确知他是永不忘记的，纵使他似乎忘记了。那封信就在那黑地里躺了整整一个长夜，我们在悲伤与恐惧的疲倦中沉睡，全然不知晨光将给我们带来什么。神是良善的！"

===Original===
“Let us go and see these people and make sure,” said Annie. “ ‘Hale and Hearty,’ do they call themselves? But I’m going with you myself this time! I’m not going to have such another day as I had yesterday—waiting for you till the sun was down, and all was dark, you bad man!—and fancying all manner of terrible things! I wonder—I wonder, if—”

===Chinese===
"我们去找这些人，把事情弄个明白，"安妮说，"他们自称'黑尔父子公司'，是吧？这一回我可要亲自跟你一道去！我可不要再过昨天那样的一天——直等你到太阳落山，天全黑了，你这个坏人！——还想象出各式各样可怕的事！我不知道——我不知道，要是——"

===Original===
“Well, what do you wonder, Annie?”

===Chinese===
"你不知道什么，安妮？"

===Original===
“Only whether, if now we were to find out it was indeed all a mistake, I should yet be able to hope on through all the rest. I doubt it; I doubt it! Oh, Hector, you have taught me everything!”

===Chinese===
"只是不知道，要是我们此刻发现这一切果然是个误会，我是否还能在往后的全部日子里这样地盼望下去。我怀疑；我怀疑！哦，赫克托，你教会了我一切！"

===Original===
“More, it seems, than I have myself learned. Your mother had already taught you far more than ever I had to give you!”

===Chinese===
"看来比我自己所学的还多。你母亲早已教会了你远过于我所能给你的！"

===Original===
“But it is much too early yet, I fear, to call in the City,” said Annie. “Don’t you think we should have time first to find out whether the gentleman we were thinking of inquiring after today be your old college friend or not? And I will call at the grocer’s, and tell him we hope to settle his bill in a few days. Then you can come to me, and I will go to you, and we shall meet somewhere between.”

===Chinese===
"可现在进城还太早了，只怕。"安妮说，"你想，我们是不是先有空去弄清楚，今天打算去打听的那位先生，究竟是不是你当年的大学同窗？我还要去一趟杂货铺，告诉他我们希望过几天就能把账结清。然后你来我这儿，我去你那儿，我们便会在半路上碰头。"

===Original===
They did as Annie proposed; and before they met, Hector had found his friend, and been heartily received both by him and by his young wife.

===Chinese===
他们照安妮说的做了；还没等他们碰头，赫克托便已找着了他的朋友，并受到了他和他的年轻妻子的热忱款待。

===Original===
When at length they reached Philpot Lane, and were seated in an outer room waiting for admission, Annie said: “Surely, if rich people knew how some they do not know need their help, they would be a little more eager to feather their wings ere they fly aloft by making friends with the Mammon of unrighteousness. Don’t you think it may be sometimes that they are afraid of doing harm with their money?”

===Chinese===
等他们终于到了菲尔波特巷，坐在外间一间屋里候见时，安妮说："要是富人们知道，有些他们素不相识的人正需要他们的帮助，他们该会更急切地趁着高飞之前，借着那不义之财（Mammon of unrighteousness）结交朋友、为自己添置羽翼吧。你说，有时候他们会不会是怕自己的钱用了反而害人？"

===Original===
“I’m afraid it is more that they never think what our Lord meant when he said the words. But oh, Annie! is it a bad sign of me that the very possibility of this money could make me so happy?”

===Chinese===
"我怕更多的是他们压根儿不去想主说那些话时是何用意。可是，哦，安妮！这钱的一线可能就让我这样快活，这对我来说是不是个坏兆头？"

===Original===
They were admitted at length, and kindly received by a gray-haired old man, who warned them not to fancy so much money would last them very long.

===Chinese===
他们终于被请了进去，一位灰白头发的老人和蔼地接待了他们，并提醒他们别以为这么一笔钱够他们花多久。

===Original===
“Indeed, sir,” answered Annie, “the best thing we expect from it is that it will put my husband in good heart to begin another book.”

===Chinese===
"说真的，先生，"安妮答道，"我们指望从这笔钱上得到的最大的好处，便是它能让我丈夫打起精神，着手再写一本书。"

===Original===
“Oh! your husband writes books, does he? Then I begin to understand my late client’s will. It is just like her,” said the old gentleman. “Had you known her long?”

===Chinese===
"哦！你丈夫是写书的，是吗？那我开始明白我这位已故当事人的遗嘱了。这正像她的为人。"老先生说，"你们认识她很久了吗？"

===Original===
“I never once saw her,” said Hector.

===Chinese===
"我从未见过她一面。"赫克托说。

===Original===
“But I did,” said Annie, “and I heard her say how delighted she was with his first book. Please, sir,” she added, “will it be long before you can let us have the money?”

===Chinese===
"可我见过，"安妮说，"我还亲耳听她说，她多喜欢他的头一本书。求您了，先生，"她又道，"还要多久您才能让我们拿到这笔钱？"

===Original===
“You shall have it by-and-by,” answered the lawyer; “all in good time.”

===Chinese===
"你们日后会拿到的，"律师答道，"一切自有它的时节。"

===Original===
And now first they learned that not a penny of the money would they receive before the end of a twelvemonth.

===Chinese===
到这时他们才头一回知道，这笔钱一便士也得等到满一年之后才能到手。

===Original===
“Well, that will give us plenty of time to die first,” thought Hector, “which I am sure the kind lady did not intend when she left us the money.”

===Chinese===
"好啦，那我们便有充裕的时间先死上一回了，"赫克托心想，"我相信这位好心的太太留下这笔钱给我们时，可绝不是这个意思。"

===Original===
Another thing they learned was that, even then, they would not receive the whole of the money left them, for seeing they could claim no relation to the legator, ten percent must be deducted from their legacy. If they came to him in a year from the date of her death, he told them he would have much pleasure in handing them the sum of four hundred and fifty pounds.

===Chinese===
他们还知道了另一件事：即便到那时，他们也不能全数领到遗赠给他们的那笔钱，因为他们不能以任何亲属关系自居于这位遗赠人，须从遗产里扣去一成。他告诉他们，如果他们从死者去世之日起满一年来找他，他将很乐意把四百五十镑这一笔款项交到他们手上。

===Original===
So they left the office—not very exultant, for they were both rather hungry, and had to go at once in search of work—with but a poor chance of borrowing upon it.

===Chinese===
于是他们离开了那间事务所——并不十分欢欣，因为他们俩都颇饿了，得立刻去找活计——而想拿这笔钱去借钱，希望也很渺茫。

===Original===
Nevertheless, Hector broke the silence by saying:

===Chinese===
尽管如此，赫克托却打破沉默说道：

===Original===
“I declare, Annie, I feel so light and free already that I could invent anything, even a fairy tale, and I feel as if it would be a lovely one. I hope you have a penny left to buy a new bottle of ink. The ink at home is so thick it takes three strokes to one mark.”

===Chinese===
"我敢说，安妮，我已经觉得一身轻松、一身自在，简直什么都能创造出来了，连一篇仙灵故事（fairy tale）也行，我觉得它一定会很美。我希望你还剩一个便士，能买一瓶新墨水。家里的墨水稠得一笔下去得分三下才成一个印子。"

===Original===
“Yes, dear, I have a penny; I have two, indeed—just twopence left. We shall buy a bottle of ink with one, and—shall it be a bun with the other? I think one penny bun will divide better than two halfpenny ones.”

===Chinese===
"有的，亲爱的，我还有一个便士；其实我有两个——刚好剩两便士。我们用一个买一瓶墨水，另一个——买个圆面包好不好？我想买一个一便士的，比买两个半便士的更好分。"

===Original===
“Very well. Only, mind, *I’m* to divide it. But, do you know, I’ve been thinking,” said Hector, “whether we might not take a holiday on the strength of our expectations, for we shall have so long to wait for the money that I think we may truly say we have *great* expectations.”

===Chinese===
"很好。不过记住，*由我*来分。可是你知道，我一直在想，"赫克托说，"我们能不能凭着这份指望先放一天假，因为我们要等这笔钱等上这么久，我想我们大可以说，我们有*远大的前程*。"

===Original===
“I think we should do better,” answered Annie, “to go back to your old friend, Mr. Gillespie, and tell him of our good-fortune, and see whether he can suggest anything for us to do in the meantime.”

===Chinese===
"我想，我们不如回到你那位老朋友吉莱斯皮先生（Mr. Gillespie）那里去，"安妮答道，"把我们的好消息告诉他，看看他能不能给我们出点什么主意，趁这段日子做点什么。"

===Original===
Hector agreed, and together they sought the terrace where Mr. and Mrs. Gillespie lived, who were much interested in their story; and then first they learned that the lady was at least well enough off to be able to help them, and, when they left, she would have Annie take with her a dozen of her handkerchiefs, to embroider with her initials and crest; but Annie begged to be allowed to take only one, that Mrs. Gillespie might first see how she liked her work.

===Chinese===
赫克托同意了，他们一同去到吉莱斯皮夫妇住的那排楼房，夫妇俩对他们的故事深表关切；到这时他们才头一回知道，这位夫人至少家境殷实、能周济他们；临走时，她非要安妮带走一打她的手帕，绣上她姓名首字母和家徽；可安妮只恳请允她拿走一块，让吉莱斯皮太太先看看是否中意她的活计。

===Original===
“For, then, you see,” she said to her husband, as they went home, “I shall be able to take it back to her this very evening and ask her for the half-crown she offered me for doing it, which I should not have had the face to do with eleven more of them still in my possession. I have no doubt of her being satisfied with my work; and in a week I shall have finished the half of them, and we shall be getting on swimmingly.”

===Chinese===
"因为这样一来，你瞧，"回家的路上她对丈夫说，"我今天晚上就能把它送回去给她，并向她要她许给我的那半克朗工钱——要是手里还攥着另外十一块，我可没脸去要。我相信她一定会满意我的活计；一周之内我就能做完其中一半，我们的日子就好过得风生水起了。"

===Original===
Throughout the winter Hector wrote steadily every night, and every night Annie sat by his side and embroidered—though her embroidery was not *all* for other people. Many a time in after years did their thoughts go back to that period as the type of the happy life they were having together.

===Chinese===
整个冬天，赫克托每晚稳稳地写着，安妮则每晚坐在他身畔刺绣——虽然她的刺绣并不*全*是为别人。多年以后，他们的心思还常常回到那段时光，把它视作他们共同度过的幸福生活的典范。

===Original===
The next time Hector went to see Mr. Gillespie, that gentleman suggested that he should give a course of lectures to ladies upon English Poetry, beginning with the Anglo-Saxon poets, of whom Gillespie said he knew nothing, but would be glad to learn a great deal. He knew also, he said, some ladies in the neighborhood willing to pay a guinea each for a course of, say, half-a-dozen such lectures. They would not cost Hector much time to prepare, and would at once bring in a little money. Coleridge himself, he suggested, had done that kind of thing.

===Chinese===
赫克托下一次去看吉莱斯皮先生时，那位先生提议他给女士们开一系列英国诗的讲座，从盎格鲁-撒克逊诗人讲起——吉莱斯皮说自己对这些诗人一无所知，倒很乐意多学一点。他还说，他认识附近几位女士，肯每人出一几尼听这样一系列讲座，比方说六讲。这些讲义不会花赫克托太多时间去准备，还能马上带来一点进项。他甚至提议说，柯尔律治（Coleridge）本人就做过这种事。

===Original===
“Yes,” said Hector, “but he was Coleridge. I have nothing to say worth saying.”

===Chinese===
"是的，"赫克托说，"可他是柯尔律治。我没什么值得一说的东西可说。"

===Original===
“Leave your hearers to judge of that,” returned Gillespie. “Do your best, and take your chance. I promise you two pupils at least not overcritical—my wife and myself. It is amazing how little those even who imagine they love it know about English poetry.”

===Chinese===
"说得好不好，留给听众去评判，"吉莱斯皮应道，"尽你所能，碰你的运气。我保你至少有两个不算苛求的听众——我太太和我。那些自以为爱诗的人，对英国诗所知竟如此之少，真是惊人。"

===Original===
“But where should I find a room?” Hector still objected.

===Chinese===
"可我到哪儿找一间屋子呢？"赫克托仍反对。

===Original===
“Would not this drawing room do?” asked his friend.

===Chinese===
"这间客厅不行吗？"他朋友问。

===Original===
“Splendidly!” answered Hector. “But what will Mrs. Gillespie say to it?”

===Chinese===
"再好不过！"赫克托答道，"可吉莱斯皮太太会怎么说？"

===Original===
“She and I are generally of one mind—about people, at least.”

===Chinese===
"她和我通常是一条心的——至少在人事上。"

===Original===
“Then I will go home at once and set about finding what to say.”

===Chinese===
"那我立刻回家，着手找该说什么。"

===Original===
“And I will go out at once and begin hunting you up an audience.”

===Chinese===
"我也立刻出去，开始给你搜罗听众。"

===Original===
Gillespie succeeded even better than he had anticipated; and there was at the first lecture a very fair gathering indeed. When it was over, the one that knew most of the subject was the young lecturer’s wife. The first course was followed by two more, the third at the request of almost all his hearers. And the result was that, before the legacy fell due, Annie had paid all their debts and had not contracted a single new one.

===Chinese===
吉莱斯皮办得比他预想的还要好；头一讲便有相当可观的一群人到场。讲完了，对这个题目所知最多的那一位，是这位年轻讲师的妻子。第一轮讲罢，又连开了两轮，第三轮几乎是在全体听众的请求下开的。结果是，在那笔遗产到期之前，安妮已还清了所有的债，且未添一笔新债。

===Original===
But when the happy day dawned Annie was not able to go with her husband to receive the money; neither did Hector wish that she had been able, for he was glad to go alone. By her side lay a lovely woman-child peacefully asleep. Hector declared her the very image of the child the rainbow left behind as it vanished.

===Chinese===
然而那可喜的一日到来时，安妮却不能随丈夫同去领那笔钱；赫克托也不希望她能去，因为他倒乐意独自前往。她身旁躺着一个可爱的女婴，安详地睡着。赫克托宣称，这孩子正是那道彩虹消失时所遗下的孩子的活脱脱的模样。

===Original===
One day, when the mother was a little stronger, she called Hector to her bedside, and playfully claimed the right to be the child’s godmother, and to give it her name.

===Chinese===
有一天，母亲稍稍硬朗了些，她把赫克托叫到床前，半开玩笑地声称自己有权做这孩子的教母，并用自己的名字给孩子命名。

===Original===
“And who else can have so good a right?” answered Hector. Yet he wondered just a little that Annie should want the child named after herself, and not after her mother.

===Chinese===
"还有谁能有更好的权利呢？"赫克托答道。可他多少有些纳闷，安妮竟想让孩子随自己的名字，而不是随她母亲。

===Original===
But when the time for the child’s baptism came, Annie, who would hold the little one herself, whispered in the ear of the clergyman:

===Chinese===
可到了孩子受洗之时，安妮执意要自己抱着这小家伙，在牧师耳畔低声说：

===Original===
“The child’s name is Iris.”

===Chinese===
"这孩子叫艾丽丝（Iris）。"

===Original===
I have told my little story. But perhaps my readers will have patience with me while I add just one little inch to the tail of the mouse my mountain has borne.

===Chinese===
我的小故事讲完了。不过，也许读者们还能耐着性子，容我把我这大山所生的小老鼠的尾巴，再添上一寸。

===Original===
Hector’s next book, although never so popular as in any outward sense to be called a success, yet was not quite a failure even in regard to the money it brought him, and even at the present day has not ceased to bring in something. Doubtless it has faults not a few, but, happily, the man who knows them best is he who wrote it, and he has never had to repent that he did write it. And now he has an audience on which he can depend to welcome whatever he writes. That he has enemies as well goes without saying, but they are rather scorners than revilers, and they have not yet caused him to retaliate once by criticising any work of theirs. Neither, I believe, has he ever failed to recognize what of genuine and good work most of them have produced. One of the best results to himself of his constant endeavor to avoid jealousy is that he is still able to write verse, and continues to take more pleasure in it than in telling his tales. And still his own test of the success of any of his books is the degree to which he enjoyed it himself while writing it.

===Chinese===
赫克托的下一本书，虽然从任何外在的意义上说都从未流行到可称作成功的地步，可就其所带给他的金钱而言也算不得失败，甚至直到今日也仍未断绝带给他一点进项。它无疑有不少毛病，但所幸最清楚这些毛病的人，正是写它的人，而且他从不曾为写了它而后悔。如今他已有一群可以仰仗的读者，欢迎他所写的一切。他自然也有敌人，那是不消说的，可他们更多是讥嘲者而非毁谤者，他们也还从未逼得他哪怕一次以批评他们的作品来还击。我相信，他也不曾因此而无视他们大多数人所作的真正优秀的作品。他不断努力避免嫉妒，其最好的结果之一便是：他至今仍能写诗，并继续从写诗中得到比讲故事更大的乐趣。而他自己衡量任何一本书是否成功的标尺，仍然是他自己写作过程中所享受到乐趣的程度。

===Original===
His legacy has long been spent, and he has often been in straits since; but he has always gathered good from those straits, and has never again felt as if slow walls were closing in upon him to crush him. And he has hopes by God’s help, and with Annie’s, of getting through at last, without ever having dishonored his high calling.

===Chinese===
他那笔遗产早已花光，此后他也时常陷入窘境；可他总能从那些窘境中得到益处，也再不曾有过那种仿佛有缓缓合拢的墙朝他逼近要把他压碎的感觉。他靠神的帮助，也靠安妮的帮助，满怀希望终能安然度过，而从不曾羞辱过他那崇高的职分。

===Original===
The last time I saw him, he introduced his wife to me—having just been telling me his and her story—with the rather enigmatical words:

===Chinese===
我最后一次见他时，他把我俩方才所讲的他和她的故事讲完，便这样把妻子介绍给我，用了几句颇为费解的话：

===Original===
“This is my wife. You cannot see her very well, for, like Hamlet, I wear her ‘in my heart’s core, aye, in my heart of hearts!’ ”

===Chinese===
"这是我的妻子。你看不太清她，因为我和哈姆雷特一样，把她'藏在我心坎里，是的，藏在我内心的最深处！'"

## Port in a Storm / 风暴港

===Original===
“Papa,” said my sister Effie, one evening as we all sat about the drawing-room fire. One after another, as nothing followed, we turned our eyes upon her. There she sat, still silent, embroidering the corner of a cambric handkerchief, apparently unaware that she had spoken.
It was a very cold night in the beginning of winter. My father had come home early, and we had dined early that we might have a long evening together, for it was my father’s and mother’s wedding-day, and we always kept it as the homeliest of holidays. My father was seated in an easy-chair by the chimney corner, with a jug of Burgundy near him, and my mother sat by his side, now and then taking a sip out of his glass.
Effie was now nearly nineteen; the rest of us were younger. What she was thinking about we did not know then, though we could all guess now. Suddenly she looked up, and seeing all eyes fixed upon her, became either aware or suspicious, and blushed rosy red.

===Chinese===
"爸爸，"妹妹艾菲（Effie）说道——那是一个夜晚，我们一家人正围坐在起居室的炉火旁。话音落下，却再没有下文，我们便一个接一个地把目光投向了她。只见她坐在那里，依旧默不作声，正绣着一方细麻布手帕的一角，仿佛全然不知自己方才开了口。
那是初冬里一个极冷的夜晚。父亲早早地回了家，我们也早早用了晚餐，好一同度过一个漫长的黄昏；因为那天是父亲和母亲的结婚纪念日，我们一向把它当作最温馨家常的节日来过。父亲坐在烟囱角落的一张安乐椅里，身旁摆着一壶勃艮第酒（Burgundy），母亲则坐在他边上，不时从他杯中呷上一口。
艾菲那时快十九岁了；我们其余几个比她更小。她那时心里想些什么，我们当时并不知道，尽管如今都能猜到。忽然间她抬起头来，见所有的目光都定定地落在自己身上，便似是察觉、又似是起了疑，双颊飞上一抹玫瑰般的红晕。

===Original===
“You spoke to me, Effie. What was it, my dear?”
“O yes, papa. I wanted to ask you whether you wouldn’t tell us, tonight, the story about how you﻿—”
“Well, my love?”
“﻿—About how you﻿—”
“I am listening, my dear.”
“I mean, about mamma and you.”
“Yes, yes. About how I got your mamma for a mother to you. Yes. I paid a dozen of port for her.”
We all and each exclaimed “Papa!” and my mother laughed.
“Tell us all about it,” was the general cry.
“Well, I will,” answered my father. “I must begin at the beginning, though.”
And, filling his glass with Burgundy, he began.

===Chinese===
"你方才叫我了，艾菲。是什么事呀，亲爱的？"
"哦，是的，爸爸。我想问您，今晚您能不能给我们讲讲，那件事——您是怎么——"
"怎么呀，我的乖？"
"——讲讲您是怎么——"
"我听着呢，亲爱的。"
"我是说，关于妈妈和您的事。"
"是，是。关于我是怎么给你们把妈妈娶来做你们母亲的。是啊。我为她花了十二瓶波特酒（port）。"
我们几个不约而同、一个不落地齐声惊呼"爸爸！"，母亲也笑了起来。
"快把来龙去脉讲给我们听。"这是齐声的请求。
"好吧，我就讲。"父亲答道，"不过，我得从头说起。"
说着，他给自己的杯子斟满勃艮第，便开了口。

===Original===
“As far back as I can remember, I lived with my father in an old manor-house in the country. It did not belong to my father, but to an elder brother of his, who at that time was captain of a seventy-four. He loved the sea more than his life; and, as yet apparently, had loved his ship better than any woman. At least he was not married.
“My mother had been dead for some years, and my father was now in very delicate health. He had never been strong, and since my mother’s death, I believe, though I was too young to notice it, he had pined away. I am not going to tell you anything about him just now, because it does not belong to my story. When I was about five years old, as nearly as I can judge, the doctors advised him to leave England. The house was put into the hands of an agent to let﻿—at least, so I suppose; and he took me with him to Madeira, where he died. I was brought home by his servant, and by my uncle’s directions, sent to a boarding-school; from there to Eton, and from there to Oxford.

===Chinese===
"在我记忆所能及的最早年月里，我与父亲一同住在乡间一座古老的庄园宅邸里。那宅子并非父亲所有，而是归他一位长兄，那位兄长当时是一艘七十四炮战舰的舰长。他爱海胜过爱自己的命；而且看样子，至今他爱自己的船胜过爱任何女子。至少他并未娶亲。
