Else, there was calm everywhere, rendered yet deeper and more intense by the dusky sorrow that filled their hearts. For, far away, hundreds of miles beyond the hearing of their ears, roared the great war-guns; next week their brother must sail with his regiment to join the army; and tomorrow he must leave his home.
The sisters looked on him tenderly, with vague fears about his fate. Yet little they divined it. That the face they loved might lie pale and bloody, in a heap of slain, was the worst image of it that arose before them; but this, had they seen the future, they would, in ignorance of the further future, have infinitely preferred to that which awaited him. And even while they looked on him, a dim feeling of the unsuitableness of his lot filled their minds. For, indeed, to all judgments it must have seemed unsuitable that the homeboy, the loved of his mother, the pet of his sisters, who was happy womanlike (as Coleridge says), if he possessed the signs of love, having never yet sought for its proofs—that he should be sent amongst soldiers, to command and be commanded; to kill, or perhaps to be himself crushed out of the fair earth in the uproar that brings back for the moment the reign of Night and Chaos. No wonder that to his sisters it seemed strange and sad. Yet such was their own position in the battle of life, in which their father had died with doubtful conquest, that when their old military uncle sent the boy an ensign’s commission, they did not dream of refusing the only path open, as they thought, to an honourable profession, even though it might lead to the trench-grave. They heard it as the voice of destiny, wept, and yielded.

===Chinese===
三双眼睛——姐妹俩和她们的兄弟——最后一次凝望着一颗巨大的淡金星子，它正追随太阳沉入陡峭的西天。它落下是为了再度升起；那即将远行的兄弟也许还会归来，然而比寻常更深的疑虑却笼罩着他的前路。因为，在姐妹俩洁白的衣裙之间，闪耀着他那件猩红的军装和金色的剑绶——这是他头一次穿戴它们，与其说是为了满足他自己的虚荣，不如说是为了成全她们的骄傲。渐渐明亮的月亮，仿佛预示着将来的一段记忆，已经开始冲淡那猩红与金黄，给它们染上一层苍白的、幽灵般的色泽。在她那沉思的光辉里，这整群人看来倒更像是影之国里的一场重逢，而非坚实大地上的一次分别。可是，究竟哪一片才该被称为真实的国土呢？——是那表象、空间与时间横插其间，阻断了灵魂言语奔流的疆域？还是那心与心相会、表象已沦为倾诉的奴仆、空间与时间尽皆被人遗忘的疆域？
穿过静谧的空气，传来远方水流奔涌之声，近处长脚秧鸡（land-rail）的啼鸣。时有一阵寒风掠过常春藤座椅上方的荫蔽，惊得叶片相挤相挨，簌簌作响，却无人留意。除此之外，到处一片宁静，因他们心头沉郁的哀愁而愈发深沉、愈发凝重。因为，在数百里之外，远在他们耳力所不能及的地方，巨大的战争炮火正在轰鸣；下周，她们的兄弟便要随他的团启航去与大军会合；而明天，他就要离开他的家了。
姐妹俩温柔地望着他，心中对他命运怀着模糊的恐惧。然而她们对那命运却几乎毫无窥见。她们所爱的这张脸庞，或许会苍白带血地躺卧在尸堆之中——这便是她们所能想到的最坏的景象；可是，倘若她们当真看见了未来，那么在对更远的未来一无所知的情形下，她们倒会宁愿这景象成真，而无限地拒绝那真正等在前头的事。甚至在她们凝视他的当儿，一种隐隐约约的感觉便已充塞她们的心——觉得他的命数是何等地不相宜。因为，诚然，无论何人判断，此事都必显得不相宜：这个家中长大的孩子，母亲所疼爱的，姐妹所宠溺的，他像女人那样地幸福（正如柯尔律治（Coleridge）所言）——只要拥有爱的凭证便已足矣，从来不曾去追寻爱的考验——如今竟要被送去军人之中，去发号施令，也受人驱使；去杀人，或也许在那短暂带回黑夜与混沌之治的喧嚣之中，被从这美好的大地上碾碎除灭。这在姐妹们看来奇特而可悲，自不待言。然而她们自己在人生的战场上也正处在同样的境地——她们的父亲便是在那战场上怀着胜负未明的结果死去的——所以，当年迈的军人伯父给这孩子送来一份少尉（ensign）的委任状时，她们做梦也没想到要拒绝这唯一一条（在她们看来）通往体面职业的道路，哪怕它可能通向壕沟般的坟墓。她们把这件事当作命运的声音，哭了，然后顺从了。

===Original===
If they had possessed a deeper insight into his character, they would have discovered yet further reason to doubt the fitness of the profession chosen for him; and if they had ever seen him at school, it is possible the doubt of fitness might have strengthened into a certainty of incongruity. His comparative inactivity amongst his schoolfellows, though occasioned by no dullness of intellect, might have suggested the necessity of a quiet life, if inclination and liking had been the arbiters in the choice. Nor was this inactivity the result of defective animal spirits either, for sometimes his mirth and boyish frolic were unbounded; but it seemed to proceed from an over-activity of the inward life, absorbing, and in some measure checking, the outward manifestation. He had so much to do in his own hidden kingdom, that he had not time to take his place in the polity and strife of the commonwealth around him. Hence, while other boys were acting, he was thinking. In this point of difference, he felt keenly the superiority of many of his companions; for another boy would have the obstacle overcome, or the adversary subdued, while he was meditating on the propriety, or on the means, of effecting the desired end. He envied their promptitude, while they never saw reason to envy his wisdom; for his conscience, tender and not strong, frequently transformed slowness of determination into irresolution: while a delicacy of the sympathetic nerves tended to distract him from any predetermined course, by the diversity of their vibrations, responsive to influences from all quarters, and destructive to unity of purpose.

===Chinese===
倘若她们对他的性灵洞察得更深一些，她们本会进一步发现更多理由，去怀疑为他所选的这门职业是否相宜；倘若她们曾见过他在学堂里的样子，那么这相宜之疑便有可能强化为相悖之确。他在同窗之间相较沉默寡言，这虽非源于智力的迟钝，却足以让人想到：假如好恶与倾向是选业时的裁决者，他所需要的其实是一种恬静的生活。这种沉默也并非源于天生的血气之弱，因为有时他的欢笑与孩童般的顽皮可谓毫无节制；它似乎倒源于内在生命的过度活跃，将外在的表现既吸纳了，又在某种程度上抑制了。他在自己那个隐秘的王国里有那么多事要做，以至于无暇在身旁这个共和国的政事与纷争中占一席之地。于是，当别的男孩在行事之时，他却在沉思。正是在这一点差异上，他痛切地感到许多同伴胜过自己；因为另一个男孩早已越过了障碍、制服了对手，而他却还在思索这件事该不该做，或用什么方法去达成所愿。他羡慕他们的果决，而他们却从不觉得有理由羡慕他的明智；因为他的良心虽然敏感却不坚强，常常把决断的迟缓变成了优柔寡断；而他同情神经的过于纤敏，又因应四方影响而生的种种震荡，往往令他从既定的方向上分心，毁坏目标的专一。

===Original===
Of such a one, the *a priori* judgment would be, that he ought to be left to meditate and grow for some time, before being called upon to produce the fruits of action. But add to these mental conditions a vivid imagination, and a high sense of honour, nourished in childhood by the reading of the old knightly romances, and then put the youth in a position in which action is imperative, and you have elements of strife sufficient to reduce that fair kingdom of his to utter anarchy and madness. Yet so little, do we know ourselves, and so different are the symbols with which the imagination works its algebra, from the realities which those symbols represent, that as yet the youth felt no uneasiness, but contemplated his new calling with a glad enthusiasm and some vanity; for all his prospect lay in the glow of the scarlet and the gold. Nor did this excitement receive any check till the day before his departure, on which day I have introduced him to my readers, when, accidently taking up a newspaper of a week old, his eye fell on these words—“*Already crying women are to be met in the streets.*” With this cloud afar on his horizon, which, though no bigger than a man’s hand, yet cast a perceptible shadow over his mind, he departed next morning. The coach carried him beyond the consecrated circle of home laws and impulses, out into the great tumult, above which rises ever and anon the cry of Cain, “Am I my brother’s keeper?”

===Chinese===
对于这样一个人，先验的判断本会是：他应当被留下来，沉思、成长一段时间，然后才能被召唤去结出行动的果实。但是，若在这般心性之上，再添上一份鲜活的想象力和一种高度的荣誉感——这二者在童年时代因阅读古老的骑士传奇而得到滋养——而后把这青年放在一个非行动不可的位置上，你便有了足够的纷争元素，足以将他那个美好的王国搅成彻底的混乱与疯狂。然而，我们对自己竟是如此无知，而想象力用以演算其代数的符号，与这些符号所代表的真实，竟相去如此之远，以至于这青年至今未感到一丝不安，反而怀着欣然的热情和些许虚荣来打量他的新使命；因为他眼前的一切前景，都笼罩在猩红与金黄的光辉之中。这股兴奋直到他启程的前一日才受到一点遏制——也就是我把他介绍给读者们的这一日——当时他偶然拿起一张一周前的报纸，目光落到了这样一句话上——“*如今街上已能遇见啼哭的妇人。*”带着天边这一朵云——它虽不过巴掌大小，却在他心头投下了清晰可辨的阴影——他在次日清晨启程了。驿车把他载过了家法与家情的圣洁疆界，载入了那场大动荡之中，在其上空，该隐的呼喊不时升起：“我岂是看守我兄弟的吗？”

===Original===
Every tragedy of higher order, constructed in Christian times, will correspond more or less to the grand drama of the Bible; wherein the first act opens with a brilliant sunset vision of Paradise, in which childish sense and need are served with all the profusion of the indulgent nurse. But the glory fades off into grey and black, and night settles down upon the heart which, rightly uncontent with the childish, and not having yet learned the childlike, seeks knowledge and manhood as a thing denied by the Maker, and yet to be gained by the creature; so sets forth alone to climb the heavens, and instead of climbing, falls into the abyss. Then follows the long dismal night of feverish efforts and delirious visions, or, it may be, helpless despair; till at length a deeper stratum of the soul is heaved to the surface; and amid the first dawn of morning, the youth says within him, “I have sinned against my *Maker*—I will arise and go to my *Father*.” More or less, I say, will Christian tragedy correspond to this—a fall and a rising again; not a rising only, but a victory; not a victory merely, but a triumph. Such, in its way and degree, is my story. I have shown, in one passing scene, the home paradise; now I have to show a scene of a far differing nature.

===Chinese===
在基督教时代所建构的每一出更高层次的悲剧，都或多或少与《圣经》那部伟大的戏剧相呼应；其中第一幕以伊甸园辉煌的日落景象开场，孩童般的感官与需求在那里被一位纵容的保姆所无限丰盛地侍奉着。然而荣耀褪为灰黑，夜色降落在那一颗心上——它理应不满足于稚气，却尚未学会孩童般的信靠，于是把知识与成年当作造物主所不肯赐予、而受造之物却仍要凭自己取得的东西去追寻；它独自启程要攀登诸天，结果非但没能攀升，反倒坠入了深渊。接踵而来的便是那漫长阴郁的黑夜——狂热的挣扎与谵妄的幻象，又或也许是全然无力的绝望；直到灵魂更深的一层终于被推到了表面；在黎明的第一缕曙光之中，那青年在内心说：“我得罪了我的*造物主*——我要起来，到我*父*那里去。”我说，基督教的悲剧或多或少都会与这相呼应——一次堕落，一次再起；不只是再起，而是一场胜利；不只是胜利，而是一场凯旋。我的故事，以它自己的方式与程度，也是如此。我已经在一个转瞬即逝的场景中，展示了家的乐园；如今我要展示一个性质截然不同的场景。

===Original===
The young ensign was lying in his tent, weary, but wakeful. All day long the cannon had been bellowing against the walls of the city, which now lay with wide, gaping breach, ready for the morrow’s storm, but covered yet with the friendly darkness. His regiment was ordered to be ready with the earliest dawn to march up to the breach. That day, for the first time, there had been blood on his sword—there the sword lay, a spot on the chased hilt still. He had cut down one of the enemy in a skirmish with a sally party of the besieged and the look of the man as he fell, haunted him. He felt, for the time, that he dared not pray to the Father, for the blood of a brother had rushed forth at the stroke of his arm, and there was one fewer of living souls on the earth because he lived thereon. And tomorrow he must lead a troop of men up to that poor disabled town, and turn them loose upon it, not knowing what might follow in the triumph of enraged and victorious foes, who for weeks had been subjected, by the constancy of the place, to the greatest privations. It was true the general had issued his commands against all disorder and pillage; but if the soldiers once yielded to temptation, what might not be done before the officers could reclaim them! All the wretched tales he had read of the sack of cities rushed back on his memory. He shuddered as he lay. Then his conscience began to speak, and to ask what right he had to be there.—Was the war a just one?—He could not tell; for this was a bad time for settling nice questions. But there he was, right or wrong, fighting and shedding blood on God’s earth, beneath God’s heaven.

===Chinese===
年轻的少尉躺在帐篷里，疲惫却毫无睡意。整整一天，大炮都在向着城墙怒吼；那城墙如今已被轰开一道宽阔豁裂的缺口，只待明日强攻，此刻仍为友善的夜色所遮蔽。他的团奉命须在最早的晨光中整装待发，向那缺口进发。那一天，他的剑上头一次沾了血——那把剑此刻就放在那里，雕花的剑柄上还残留着一处斑痕。他在一次与守军出击小队的遭遇战中砍倒了一个敌人，那人倒下时的神情，萦绕在他心头挥之不去。一时间，他觉得自己不敢向父祈祷，因为一个兄弟的血已经从他臂膀的一击之中涌出，又因为他在世上活着，世上活着的灵魂便少了一个。而明天，他还得率领一队人马开上那座可怜的、已无还手之力的城镇，把他们放手投入其中，却不知那些被此地的坚守困顿了数周、被激怒而获胜的敌军，在胜利之中会做出什么事来。将军确实已颁布了严禁一切骚乱与劫掠的命令；可士兵们一旦屈服于诱惑，在军官们能把他们重新收束住之前，什么事做不出来呢！他所读过的关于城市遭劫的种种惨事，一齐涌回了他的记忆。他躺着，不禁一阵阵战栗。接着他的良心开始发声，问他究竟有什么权利置身于此。——这场战争是正义的吗？——他无从回答；因为眼下并非解决这类微妙问题的好时候。但他就在那里，对也好错也好，在上帝的大地上厮杀、流血，在上帝的苍穹之下。

===Original===
Over and over he turned the question in his mind; again and again the spouting blood of his foe, and the death-look in his eye, rose before him; and the youth who at school could never fight with a companion because he was not sure that he was in the right, was alone in the midst of undoubting men of war, amongst whom he was driven helplessly along, upon the waves of a terrible necessity. What wonder that in the midst of these perplexities his courage should fail him! What wonder that the consciousness of fainting should increase the faintness! or that the dread of fear and its consequences should hasten and invigorate its attacks! To crown all, when he dropped into a troubled slumber at length, he found himself hurried, as on a storm of fire, through the streets of the captured town, from all the windows of which looked forth familiar faces, old and young, but distorted from the memory of his boyhood by fear and wild despair. On one spot lay the body of his father, with his face to the earth; and he woke at the cry of horror and rage that burst from his own lips, as he saw the rough, bloody hand of a soldier twisted in the loose hair of his elder sister, and the younger fainting in the arms of a scoundrel belonging to his own regiment. He slept no more. As the grey morning broke, the troops appointed for the attack assembled without sound of trumpet or drum, and were silently formed in fitting order. The young ensign was in his place, weary and wretched after his miserable night. Before him he saw a great, broad-shouldered lieutenant, whose brawny hand seemed almost too large for his sword-hilt, and in any one of whose limbs played more animal life than in the whole body of the pale youth. The firm-set lips of this officer, and the fire of his eye, showed a concentrated resolution, which, by the contrast, increased the misery of the ensign, and seemed, as if the stronger absorbed the weaker, to draw out from him the last fibres of self-possession: the sight of unattainable determination, while it increased the feeling of the arduousness of that which required such determination, threw him into the great gulf which lay between him and it. In this disorder of his nervous and mental condition, with a doubting conscience and a shrinking heart, is it any wonder that the terrors which lay before him at the gap in those bristling walls, should draw near, and, making sudden inroad upon his soul, overwhelm the government of a will worn out by the tortures of an unassured spirit? What share fear contributed to unman him, it was impossible for him, in the dark, confused conflict of differing emotions, to determine; but doubtless a natural shrinking from danger, there being no excitement to deaden its influence, and no hope of victory to encourage to the struggle, seeing victory was dreadful to him as defeat, had its part in the sad result. Many men who have courage, are dependent on ignorance and a low state of the moral feeling for that courage; and a further progress towards the development of the higher nature would, for a time at least, entirely overthrow it. Nor could such loss of courage be rightly designated by the name of cowardice. But, alas! the colonel happened to fix his eyes upon him as he passed along the file; and this completed his confusion. He betrayed such evident symptoms of perturbation, that that officer ordered him under arrest; and the result was, that, chiefly for the sake of example to the army, he was, upon trial by court-martial, expelled from the service, and had his sword broken over his head. Alas for the delicate minded youth! Alas for the home-darling!

===Chinese===
他在心中把这问题翻来覆去；那仇敌喷涌而出的鲜血、他眼中的死亡神情，又一再在他眼前浮现；这青年当年在学堂里从不曾与同伴动手相争，因为他总不能确信自己是有理的一方——如今却独自置身于一群毫不犹豫的战士之间，在他们当中被一股可怕必然性的浪潮无助地裹挟而去。处在这种种困惑之中，他的勇气竟然弃他而去，又有什么可怪的呢！意识到自己正在晕厥，这意识本身又加重了晕厥，又有什么可怪的呢！对恐惧及其后果的忧惧，反过来催迫并加重了恐惧的攻势，又有什么可怪的呢！更糟的是，当他终于跌入一场烦躁的浅睡之时，他发觉自己仿佛被一场烈火风暴裹挟着，飞驰过那座已被攻陷的城镇的街巷，所有窗子里都探出熟悉的脸庞，有老有少，却因恐惧与绝望而扭曲得全然不像他童年记忆里的模样。在一处地方，他父亲的尸首面朝大地躺着；而当他看见一只粗暴带血的手攥住他姐姐散乱的头发、他妹妹昏厥在他自己团里一个无赖的臂弯里时，他猛然惊醒——他自己唇间正迸发出一声惊惧与愤怒的呼喊。他再也睡不着了。灰蒙蒙的晨光初破，受命进攻的部队没有号角、没有鼓声地集合起来，悄然地按恰当的队形排好。年轻的少尉在自己的位置上，熬过了可悲的一夜之后既疲惫又痛苦。在他前面，是一位身材高大、肩膀宽阔的中尉，那只筋骨粗大的手握住剑柄几乎嫌太大，他随便哪一条肢干里所涌动的血气，都比这苍白青年整个身躯里的还要多。这位军官紧抿的双唇与眼中燃烧的光芒，显出一种凝定的决心；这决心因对照之故，反倒加重了少尉的痛苦，仿佛强者吞并了弱者一般，把他自持的最后一丝纤维也抽了出去：望着那高不可攀的决断，他既更深刻地感到需要这种决断之事是何等艰巨，整个人便被掷进了他自身与那决断之间横亘的深渊。处在这样神经与心智俱乱的状态下，良心犹疑、心灵畏缩，那些埋伏在他前方、在那矛戟森然的城墙缺口处等着他的恐惧，竟走近前来，骤然侵入他的灵魂，压垮了一个已被犹疑之灵的折磨所耗尽的意志之统御——这又有什么可怪的呢？在这一片纷杂情绪的晦暗混乱冲突之中，他无从判断恐惧究竟占了多少成分，使他失了男儿气概；但无疑地，对危险一种本能的畏缩——既无兴奋以麻痹其影响，又无胜利的希望以鼓舞他去搏斗，因为胜利对他而言与失败同样可怖——也在这可悲的结局中占了一份。许多有勇气的人，他们的勇气实则有赖于无知与道德感的低下；而一旦向更高本性的发育再推进一步，这种勇气至少在一段时间内便会彻底崩塌。这样的勇气丧失，也不能恰当地被冠以懦夫之名。然而，可叹哪！上校沿队列走过时，目光恰好落在了他身上；这一下便彻底击溃了他。他表现出如此明显的慌乱征候，那位军官当即下令将他拘押；其结果便是，主要是为了在全军之前立个榜样，他在军事法庭受审之后被开除军籍，他的剑被当众在他头上折断。可叹这心思细密的青年！可叹这家中的宠儿！

===Original===
Long after, he found at the bottom of his chest the pieces of the broken sword, and remembered that, at the time, he had lifted them from the ground and carried them away. But he could not recall under what impulse he had done so. Perhaps the agony he suffered, passing the bounds of mortal endurance, had opened for him a vista into the eternal, and had shown him, if not the injustice of the sentence passed upon him, yet his freedom from blame, or, endowing him with dim prophetic vision, had given him the assurance that some day the stain would be wiped from his soul, and leave him standing clear before the tribunal of his own honour. Some feeling like this, I say, may have caused him, with a passing gleam of indignant protest, to lift the fragments from the earth, and carry them away; even as the friends of a so-called traitor may bear away his mutilated body from the wheel. But if such was the case, the vision was soon overwhelmed and forgotten in the succeeding anguish. He could not see that, in mercy to his doubting spirit, the question which had agitated his mind almost to madness, and which no results of the impending conflict could have settled for him, was thus quietly set aside for the time; nor that, painful as was the dark, dreadful existence that he was now to pass in self-torment and moaning, it would go by, and leave his spirit clearer far, than if, in his apprehension, it had been stained with further blood-guiltiness, instead of the loss of honour. Years after, when he accidentally learned that on that very morning the whole of his company, with parts of several more, had, or ever they began to mount the breach, been blown to pieces by the explosion of a mine, he cried aloud in bitterness, “Would God that my fear had not been discovered before I reached that spot!” But surely it is better to pass into the next region of life having reaped some assurance, some firmness of character, determination of effort, and consciousness of the worth of life, in the present world; so approaching the future steadily and faithfully, and if in much darkness and ignorance, yet not in the oscillations of moral uncertainty.

===Chinese===
许久之后，他在箱底找到了那把折断的剑的碎片，才回想起当时自己曾把它们从地上拾起，带走了。但他却记不清自己是在怎样的冲动之下做下此事的。也许，他所受的苦痛既已逾越了凡人所能承受的界限，便为他洞开了一道通往永恒的远景，向他显明了——即便不是那判决本身的不公，至少也是他自己的清白无愧；又或者，赋予了他一种隐约的先知之见，让他确信有朝一日那污点终将从他灵魂上拭去，使他清清白白地站在自己良心的法庭之前。我说，正是某种与此相近的感受，使他在一闪而过的愤然抗议之中，从地上拾起了那些碎片，把它们带走了；正如一个所谓叛徒的友人，也许会把他那残破的尸首从车轮上抬走一般。但倘若果真如此，那异象也很快就在随之而来的痛苦之中被淹没、被遗忘了。他看不见——原来这乃是对他那犹疑之灵的一种怜悯：那个几乎把他逼疯、而即将到来的战斗的任何结局都无法为他解开的问题，就这样被悄然搁置一旁；他也看不见——尽管他此后将在自责与呻吟中度过一段黑暗可怖的时光，但这时光终将过去，并使他的灵魂远比另一种情形更为清明——倘若照他所忧虑的那样，他灵魂上沾染的是更深的血罪，而不是荣誉的丧失。多年以后，当他偶然得知，就在那个清晨，他那一整连人——外加其他几个连的部分人马——在尚未开始攀登缺口之前，便已被一颗地雷的爆炸炸得粉身碎骨，他痛苦地高呼：“愿上帝让我的恐惧，不至于在我到达那地方之前便被人发觉！”然而，在进入人生的下一个境域之前，先在现世收获某种把握、某种性格的坚毅、某种努力的决心、对生命价值的某种自觉，无疑才是更好的事；如此方能沉稳而忠实地走向未来，纵然仍处在许多黑暗与无知之中，却不再摇摆于道德的犹疑不定。

===Original===
Close upon the catastrophe followed a torpor, which lasted he did not know how long, and which wrapped in a thick fog all the succeeding events. For some time he can hardly be said to have had any conscious history. He awoke to life and torture when halfway across the sea towards his native country, where was no home any longer for him. To this point, and no farther, could his thoughts return in after years. But the misery which he then endured is hardly to be understood, save by those of like delicate temperament with himself. All day long he sat silent in his cabin; nor could any effort of the captain, or others on board, induce him to go on deck till night came on, when, under the starlight, he ventured into the open air. The sky soothed him then, he knew not how. For the face of nature is the face of God, and must bear expressions that can influence, though unconsciously to them, the most ignorant and hopeless of His children. Often did he watch the clouds in hope of a storm, his spirit rising and falling as the sky darkened or cleared; he longed, in the necessary selfishness of such suffering, for a tumult of waters to swallow the vessel; and only the recollection of how many lives were involved in its safety besides his own, prevented him from praying to God for lightning and tempest, borne on which he might dash into the haven of the other world. One night, following a sultry calm day, he thought that Mercy had heard his unuttered prayer. The air and sea were intense darkness, till a light as intense for one moment annihilated it, and the succeeding darkness seemed shattered with the sharp reports of the thunder that cracked without reverberation. He who had shrunk from battle with his fellow-men, rushed to the mainmast, threw himself on his knees, and stretched forth his arms in speechless energy of supplication; but the storm passed away overhead, and left him kneeling still by the uninjured mast. At length the vessel reached her port. He hurried on shore to bury himself in the most secret place he could find. Out of sight was his first, his only thought. Return to his mother he would not, he could not; and, indeed, his friends never learned his fate, until it had carried him far beyond their reach.

===Chinese===
紧随着那场灾祸而来的，是一种麻木；这种麻木持续了多久他无从知晓，它把此后发生的一切都裹在了一层浓雾之中。有一段时间，简直不能说他有过任何清醒的人生。他在朝着故国渡海的中途才重新苏醒过来，回到生命与折磨之中——那里早已不再有他的家。在往后的岁月里，他的思绪也只能回溯到这里，再无法向前。但他那时所承受的苦痛，除了与他性情同样细腻的人之外，旁人几乎无从体会。他整日默然坐在舱中；无论是船长还是船上其他人的好意努力，都无法引他上甲板，直到夜幕降临，他才在星光下胆怯地走进露天之处。那时，天空抚慰了他，他自己也不知是怎么抚慰的。因为自然的面貌便是上帝的面貌，它必有种种神情，能够影响——尽管他们自己并不觉察——上帝那些最无知、最绝望的儿女。他常常凝望云气，巴望一场暴风雨；他的心绪随天色的阴沉或清明而起落；他在那种痛苦所必然带有的自私之中，渴望一阵滔天大水将船吞没；只是他想到这艘船的安全还牵连着除他之外的那么多条性命，这才阻止了他向上帝祈求闪电与狂风——他好乘风冲入另一个世界的港湾。某个夜晚，在一个闷热无风的白日之后，他想，仁慈已听见了他那未曾说出口的祷告。空气与海都是一片深沉的黑暗，直到一道同样强烈的光在一刹那间将它吞噬，而随后的黑暗似乎被一声声清脆而不带回响的雷霆劈裂开来。这个曾退缩而不肯与同类交锋的人，此刻却冲向主桅，双膝跪地，在无言的热切恳求之中张开双臂；然而风暴从头顶掠过，留下他依旧跪在那根未受损伤的桅杆旁。船终于抵港。他急急上岸，想把自己埋进他能找到的最隐秘的角落。从世人眼前消失，是他头一个、也是唯一的念头。回到母亲身边，他不愿，也不能；而事实上，他的亲友们也始终未能得知他的下落，直到命运已把他远远带离了他们所能触及的范围。

===Original===
For several weeks he lurked about like a malefactor, in low lodging-houses in narrow streets of the seaport to which the vessel had borne him, heeding no one, and but little shocked at the strange society and conversation with which, though only in bodily presence, he had to mingle. These formed the subjects of reflection in after times; and he came to the conclusion that, though much evil and much misery exist, sufficient to move prayers and tears in those who love their kind, yet there is less of both than those looking down from a more elevated social position upon the weltering heap of humanity, are ready to imagine; especially if they regard it likewise from the pedestal of self-congratulation on which a meagre type of religion has elevated them. But at length his little stock of money was nearly expended, and there was nothing that he could do, or learn to do, in this seaport. He felt impelled to seek manual labour, partly because he thought it more likely he could obtain that sort of employment, without a request for reference as to his character, which would lead to inquiry about his previous history; and partly, perhaps, from an instinctive feeling that hard bodily labour would tend to lessen his inward suffering.

===Chinese===
有好几个星期，他像个罪犯似的潜行躲藏，寄居在那艘船所载他到达的海港城中那些狭窄街巷里的下等客栈；他不去理会任何人，对于自己虽只身在肉体上却也不得不与之混处的古怪人群与古怪言谈，也几乎不大感到震惊。这些人情物态日后便成了他沉思的材料；他得出的结论是：世间诚然存在许多邪恶与苦难，足以使爱人如己者为之流泪祷告；然而这两者其实并不像某些人所以为的那么多——那些人从一个更高的社会地位俯瞰那翻腾的人类群堆，尤其是当他们同时还站在某种贫瘠的宗教所为他们垫高的自庆自矜的台座上俯瞰之时。但他的那点微薄积蓄终于将近告罄，而在这海港城里，他既没有什么事可做，也学不会做什么。他感到自己非得去寻找体力劳动不可，一方面是因为他觉得自己更容易得到这一类活计，而不至于被索要品行的担保，由此引来对他过往经历的查问；另一方面，也许也是出于一种本能的预感——繁重的体力劳动或许能减轻他内心的痛苦。

===Original===
He left the town, therefore, at nightfall of a July day, carrying a little bundle of linen, and the remains of his money, somewhat augmented by the sale of various articles of clothing and convenience, which his change of life rendered superfluous and unsuitable. He directed his course northwards, travelling principally by night—so painfully did he shrink from the gaze even of foot-farers like himself; and sleeping during the day in some hidden nook of wood or thicket, or under the shadow of a great tree in a solitary field. So fine was the season, that for three successive weeks he was able to travel thus without inconvenience, lying down when the sun grew hot in the forenoon, and generally waking when the first faint stars were hesitating in the great darkening heavens that covered and shielded him. For above every cloud, above every storm, rise up, calm, clear, divine, the deep infinite skies; they embrace the tempest even as the sunshine; by their permission it exists within their boundless peace: therefore it cannot hurt, and must pass away, while there they stand as ever, domed up eternally, lasting, strong, and pure.

===Chinese===
于是，在七月某日的傍晚，他离开了那座城，随身带着一小包衣物和剩下的钱——这点钱因卖掉了一些因他生活的变故而变得多余、不相宜的衣饰什物，略略增加了些。他朝北而行，主要在夜间赶路——他甚至对与他一样的步行旅人的目光都畏缩得那样痛苦；白天则在树林或灌木丛的某个隐秘角落里，或是在孤零零田野上一棵大树的浓荫下睡觉。时节正美，一连三个星期他都能这样旅行而不觉不便：上午太阳转烈时便躺下歇息，通常在第一批隐约的星辰在那覆盖他、庇护他的、渐渐昏暗的辽阔苍穹中踌躇着亮起来时醒来。因为在每一片云、每一场风暴之上，都升起那平静、清明、神圣而深邃无限的诸天；它们拥抱风暴，一如拥抱阳光；风暴是因它们的允准才存在于它们无尽的安宁之内：因此它无法伤人，终将消逝，而它们却一如既往地矗立在那里，永远穹隆般高拱，永恒、坚强而纯净。

===Original===
Several times he attempted to get agricultural employment; but the whiteness of his hands and the tone of his voice not merely suggested unfitness for labour, but generated suspicion as to the character of one who had evidently dropped from a rank so much higher, and was seeking admittance within the natural masonic boundaries and secrets and privileges of another. Disheartened somewhat, but hopeful, he journeyed on. I say hopeful; for the blessed power of life in the universe in fresh air and sunshine absorbed by active exercise, in winds, yea in rain, though it fell but seldom, had begun to work its natural healing, soothing effect, upon his perturbed spirit. And there was room for hope in his new endeavour. As his bodily strength increased, and his health, considerably impaired by inward suffering, improved, the trouble of his soul became more endurable—and in some measure to endure is to conquer and destroy. In proportion as the mind grows in the strength of patience, the disturber of its peace sickens and fades away. At length, one day, a widow lady in a village through which his road led him, gave him a day’s work in her garden. He laboured hard and well, notwithstanding his soon-blistered hands, received his wages thankfully, and found a resting-place for the night on the low part of a haystack from which the upper portion had been cut away. Here he ate his supper of bread and cheese, pleased to have found such comfortable quarters, and soon fell fast asleep.

===Chinese===
他好几次想去寻一份农活；但他那双手的白皙、他那说话的声调，不仅显得他不堪劳作，更招来了疑心——这样一个人显然是从一个高出许多的身份上跌落下来，如今却想挤进另一个阶层那天然的、如共济会（masonic）般的边界、隐秘与特权之中。他多少有些气馁，却仍怀着希望，继续前行。我说“怀着希望”；因为那宇宙间赐福的生命之力——借由活跃的运动所吸收的新鲜空气与阳光，借由风，甚至借由少得可怜的雨——已经开始在他那纷扰的心上发挥它天然的疗愈、安抚之效。在他这一番新的努力之中，确实有希望的余地。随着他体力的增长，随着他那被内心痛苦大大损伤的健康逐渐恢复，他灵魂上的烦恼变得更容易忍受了——而在某种程度上，能够忍受，便是征服，便是消灭。心灵随忍耐之力一同增长，那搅扰它安宁的祸首便会衰弱、凋零。终于有一日，在他所经过的一个村子里，一位寡居的夫人给了他一天的园子活计。尽管双手不久便磨起了水泡，他依旧干得卖力而出色，感激地领了工钱，在割去了上半截的一个干草堆的低矮处寻得一处过夜之所。他在那里吃了面包和奶酪作晚餐，满心欢喜地寻到这么一处舒适的栖身地，很快便沉沉睡去。

===Original===
When he awoke, the whole heavens and earth seemed to give a full denial to sin and sorrow. The sun was just mounting over the horizon, looking up the clear cloud-mottled sky. From millions of water-drops hanging on the bending stalks of grass, sparkled his rays in varied refraction, transformed here to a gorgeous burning ruby, there to an emerald, green as the grass, and yonder to a flashing, sunny topaz. The chanting priest-lark had gone up from the low earth, as soon as the heavenly light had begun to enwrap and illumine the folds of its tabernacle; and had entered the high heavens with his offering, whence, unseen, he now dropped on the earth the sprinkled sounds of his overflowing blessedness. The poor youth rose but to kneel, and cry, from a bursting heart, “Hast Thou not, O Father, some care for me? Canst Thou not restore my lost honour? Can anything befall Thy children for which Thou hast no help? Surely, if the face of Thy world lie not, joy and not grief is at the heart of the universe. Is there none for me?”

===Chinese===
当他醒来时，整个天地仿佛都在断然否定罪与忧愁的存在。太阳正从地平线上升起，仰望着那澄澈而云迹斑驳的天空。千万颗水珠挂在弯弯的草茎上，阳光折射出多彩的光芒——在这里化作一颗华美炽燃的红宝石，在那里化作一颗与草色同样碧绿的翡翠，再远处又化作一颗闪烁的、阳光般的黄玉。那吟诵祭祷的云雀，一等天上的光开始笼罩并照亮它那会幕的层层帷幔，便从低处的大地腾起；它带着自己的供物进入了高天，从那里，隐而不见的它，将满溢的福乐化作洒落的声音，撒在大地之上。这可怜的青年起来，却只为跪下，从一颗几欲破裂的心中呼喊：“父啊，你岂不也看顾我吗？你难道不能归还我失去的荣誉吗？你的儿女会遭遇什么，是你所无力相助的吗？若你这世界的面容不曾说谎，那么宇宙的心必定是喜乐，而不是忧愁。难道竟没有为我预备的一份吗？”

===Original===
The highest poetic feeling of which we are now conscious, springs not from the beholding of perfected beauty, but from the mute sympathy which the creation with all its children manifests with us in the groaning and travailing which look for the sonship. Because of our need and aspiration, the snowdrop gives birth in our hearts to a loftier spiritual and poetic feeling, than the rose most complete in form, colour, and odour. The rose is of Paradise—the snowdrop is of the striving, hoping, longing Earth. Perhaps our highest poetry is the expression of our aspirations in the sympathetic forms of visible nature. Nor is this merely a longing for a restored Paradise; for even in the ordinary history of men, no man or woman that has fallen, can be restored to the position formerly held. Such must rise to a yet higher place, whence they can behold their former standing far beneath their feet. They must be restored by the attainment of something better than they ever possessed before, or not at all. If the law be a weariness, we must escape it by taking refuge with the spirit, for not otherwise can we fulfil the law than by being above the law. To escape the overhanging rocks of Sinai, we must climb to its secret top.

===Chinese===
我们今日所能体味到的最高的诗意感受，并非源于对完美之美的凝望，而是源于受造之物连同它一切儿女，在我们这等候得着儿子名分的叹息与劳苦之中，向我们默默流露的同情。正因为我们的匮乏与渴慕，雪花莲在我们心中所催生的那份灵性与诗意，比那形态、色彩、芬芳都臻于圆满的玫瑰所催生的还要崇高。玫瑰属于乐园——雪花莲属于那挣扎、盼望、渴慕的大地。或许我们最高的诗，便是借可见自然的同情之形，来抒发我们的渴慕。这也并不仅仅是对失而复得的乐园的渴望；因为即便在人世间寻常的历史里，凡堕落过的人，都无法再被恢复到原先所处的地位。这样的人必须升到一个更高的位置，从那里俯瞰自己曾经站立之处已远远地在脚下。他们唯有借着获得某种前所未有过的、更好的东西，才能被挽回；否则便无可挽回。律法若成了一种重负，我们唯有遁入那灵之中才能脱身，因为除了站在律法之上，我们再无别法可以成全律法。要躲避西奈山（Sinai）那悬于头顶的巉岩，唯有攀登到它隐秘的峰顶。

> “Is thy strait horizon dreary?
>
> Is thy foolish fancy chill?
>
> Change the feet that have grown weary
>
> For the wings that never will.”
>
> “你这逼仄的天际可觉凄凉？
>
> 你这痴妄的念想可觉寒凉？
>
> 那双走累了的脚啊，且换作
>
> 那永不疲倦的翅膀。”

===Original===
Thus, like one of the wandering knights searching the wide earth for the Sangreal, did he wander on, searching for his lost honour, or rather (for that he counted gone forever) seeking unconsciously for the peace of mind which had departed from him, and taken with it, not the joy merely, but almost the possibility, of existence.
At last, when his little store was all but exhausted, he was employed by a market gardener, in the neighbourhood of a large country town, to work in his garden, and sometimes take his vegetables to market. With him he continued for a few weeks, and wished for no change; until, one day driving his cart through the town, he saw approaching him an elderly gentleman, whom he knew at once, by his gait and carriage, to be a military man. Now he had never seen his uncle the retired officer, but it struck him that this might be he; and under the tyranny of his passion for concealment, he fancied that, if it were he, he might recognise him by some family likeness—not considering the improbability of his looking at him. This fancy, with the painful effect which the sight of an officer, even in plain clothes, had upon him, recalling the torture of that frightful day, so overcame him, that he found himself at the other end of an alley before he recollected that he had the horse and cart in charge. This increased his difficulty; for now he dared not return, lest his inquiries after the vehicle, if the horse had strayed from the direct line, should attract attention, and cause interrogations which he would be unable to answer. The fatal want of self-possession seemed again to ruin him. He forsook the town by the nearest way, struck across the country to another line of road, and before he was missed, was miles away, still in a northerly direction.

===Chinese===
于是，他便像一个四方漂泊、踏遍天涯寻找圣杯（Sangreal）的游侠骑士那样，一路流浪下去，寻找他失去的荣誉；更确切地说——因为他认定那荣誉已永远失去——他是在无意识地寻找那一度离开他的心境的安宁，而那份安宁带走的，不仅是人生的喜乐，更是人生存下去的几乎全部可能。
终于，当他那点微薄的积蓄几近耗尽之时，他在一座乡间大城附近被一位菜园主雇下，到他的园子里干活，间或也替他把菜运到市场上去。他在那里做了几个星期，并不想另寻去处；直到有一天，他赶着大车穿过城里的街道，看见迎面走来一位上了年纪的绅士，他从那人的步态与举止一眼便认出，这是个行伍中人。他其实从未见过他那退役军官的伯父，但忽然心生一念，觉得这人说不定就是他；在他那种一心想隐姓埋名的痴念的逼迫下，他又暗想，若是伯父，说不定能凭某种家族的相像认出他来——却没顾上去想伯父根本不会朝他看上一眼。这念头，加上即便是一个着便装的军官映入眼帘也会在他心头唤起的痛楚——那痛楚把那个可怕日子里的折磨一并带了回来——竟把他整个压倒，以致他发觉自己已跑到一条小巷的另一头，才猛然想起自己还赶着一匹马、一辆车。这下他的处境更难了；因为如今他不敢回头去找，生怕他打听车辆下落——万一那马已偏离了原路——会引来注意，招来他无从作答的盘问。那种要命的失于自持，似乎又一次要毁了他。他从最近的路逃出了那座城，斜穿过乡野奔向另一条大道，还没等人发觉他不见了，他已在几英里之外，仍朝着北方而去。

===Original===
But although he thus shunned the face of man, especially of anyone who reminded him of the past, the loss of his reputation in their eyes was not the cause of his inward grief. That would have been comparatively powerless to disturb him, had he not lost his own respect. He quailed before his own thoughts; he was dishonoured in his own eyes. His perplexity had not yet sufficiently cleared away to allow him to see the extenuating circumstances of the case; not to say the fact that the peculiar mental condition in which he was at the time, removed the case quite out of the class of ordinary instances of cowardice. He condemned himself more severely than any of his judges would have dared; remembering that portion of his mental sensations which had savoured of fear, and forgetting the causes which had produced it. He judged himself a man stained with the foulest blot that could cleave to a soldier’s name, a blot which nothing but death, not even death, could efface. But, inwardly condemned and outwardly degraded, his dread of recognition was intense; and feeling that he was in more danger of being discovered where the population was sparser, he resolved to hide himself once more in the midst of poverty; and, with this view, found his way to one of the largest of the manufacturing towns.

===Chinese===
然而，他虽这样躲避世人的目光，尤其躲避任何能令他想起往事的人，但他在他人眼中的名誉丧失，并非他内心悲伤的根源。倘若他不是连对自己的尊重也一并失去，那一点挫损本来是相对无力搅扰他的。他在自己的念头面前发抖；他在自己的眼中已是蒙羞之人。他的迷惘尚未散去到足以让他看清案情的种种从轻情节；更不必说，他当时所处的特殊精神状态，已使这件事完全不属于寻常懦夫之例。他责罚自己，比任何一位审判他的人都更敢下重手；他记得自己心绪中那种带有恐惧味道的部分，却忘记了酿成那恐惧的种种缘由。他判定自己是一个沾着军人之名所能沾上的最污秽污点的人，一种除死之外——不，甚至死也不能——抹去的污点。但既然内受自责、外遭贬黜，他怕被人认出来的恐惧便无比强烈；他觉得自己在人烟稀少之处反倒更易被发觉，于是决心再次藏身于贫苦之中；怀着这一念头，他来到了那些最大的制造业城市之一。

===Original===
He reached it during the strike of a great part of the workmen; so that, though he found some difficulty in procuring employment, as might be expected from his ignorance of machine-labour, he yet was sooner successful than he would otherwise have been. Possessed of a natural aptitude for mechanical operations, he soon became a tolerable workman; and he found that his previous education assisted to the fitting execution of those operations even which were most purely mechanical.

===Chinese===
他到达那城时，正值大部分工人罢工；因此，尽管因不谙机器活计，不出所料地在谋职上颇费了些周折，他还是比较快地如愿以偿，比寻常时候要快得多。他天生对机械操作颇有悟性，不久便成为一名过得去的工人；他发现，自己先前所受的教育，对于恰如其分地完成那些操作——哪怕是最纯粹的机械活——也大有助益。

===Original===
He found also, at first, that the unrelaxing attention requisite for the mastering of the many niceties of his work, of necessity drew his mind somewhat from its brooding over his misfortune, hitherto almost ceaseless. Every now and then, however, a pang would shoot suddenly to his heart, and turn his face pale, even before his consciousness had time to inquire what was the matter. So by degrees, as attention became less necessary, and the nervo-mechanical action of his system increased with use, his thoughts again returned to their old misery. He would wake at night in his poor room, with the feeling that a ghostly nightmare sat on his soul; that a want—a loss—miserable, fearful—was present; that something of his heart was gone from him; and through the darkness he would hear the snap of the breaking sword, and lie for a moment overwhelmed beneath the assurance of the incredible fact. Could it be true that *he* was a coward? that *his* honour was gone, and in its place a stain? that *he* was a thing for men—and worse, for women—to point the finger at, laughing bitter laughter? Never lover or husband could have mourned with the same desolation over the departure of the loved; the girl alone, weeping scorching tears over *her* degradation, could resemble him in his agony, as he lay on his bed, and wept and moaned.

===Chinese===
起先他还发觉，掌握工作中许多精微之处所必需的那种一刻不懈的专注，多少把他的心思从他几乎无休无止地反复咀嚼的不幸之中拉开了些。然而，时不时地，一阵剧痛会猝然射入他的心，把他的脸变得惨白——甚至在他的意识还来不及追问出了什么事之前。于是渐渐地，随着专注变得越来越不必要，随着他那近乎机械的神经性反应因反复使用而增强，他的心思又回到了旧日的痛苦之上。夜里，他会在那间破旧的屋子里惊醒，只觉有个鬼魅般的梦魇坐在他灵魂上；只觉一种匮乏——一种失去——可悲可怖——就在眼前；只觉心头的某样东西从他身上失落了；透过黑暗，他会听见那把剑折断时的脆响，有那么一刻被这一不可思议的事实压得透不过气来。难道这是真的吗——*他*竟是个懦夫？*他的*荣誉竟已荡然无存，取而代之的是一道污痕？*他*竟成了男人——更糟的是，女人——用手指指着、发出尖刻嘲笑的对象？再没有哪个恋人或丈夫，能在所爱之人逝去之时，怀着与他同样的凄凉哀恸；唯有那为*自己*的堕落而流下灼热眼泪的女子，才能与他那躺在床上一面哭泣一面呻吟的剧痛相比。

===Original===
His sufferings had returned with the greater weight, that he was no longer upheld by the “divine air” and the open heavens, whose sunlight now only reached him late in an afternoon, as he stood at his loom, through windows so coated with dust that they looked like frosted glass; showing, as it passed through the air to fall on the dirty floor, how the breath of life was thick with dust of iron and wood, and films of cotton; amidst which his senses were now too much dulled by custom to detect the exhalations from greasy wheels and overtasked humankind. Nor could he find comfort in the society of his fellow-labourers. True, it was a kind of comfort to have those near him who could not know of his grief; but there was so little in common between them, that any interchange of thought was impossible. At least, so it seemed to him. Yet sometimes his longing for human companionship would drive him out of his dreary room at night, and send him wandering through the lower part of the town, where he would gaze wistfully on the miserable faces that passed him, as if looking for someone—some angel, even there—to speak goodwill to his hungry heart.

===Chinese===
他的苦痛之所以卷土重来，分量更重，是因为他再也无从仰赖那“神一般的空气”和那敞开的天宇；如今那阳光只能在午后晚些时候，透过他织机旁那些蒙尘厚得几如磨砂玻璃的窗子照到他；阳光穿过空气落在肮脏的地板上，显出那生命之息里竟饱含着铁屑、木屑和棉花飞絮；置身其间，他的感官如今已被习惯磨得过于迟钝，竟嗅不出油腻的齿轮和过度操劳的人身上发出的浊气。他在同工之中也找不到慰藉。诚然，身边有些不知道他悲伤的人，这本身算是一种慰藉；但他们彼此之间实在少有共通之处，任何思想的交流都不可能。至少，在他看来是如此。然而有时，他对人际陪伴的渴望会把他从那阴郁的屋子里赶出去，驱使他在夜里踯躅于城的下层街区，他热切地凝望那些与他擦肩而过的愁苦面孔，仿佛在寻找某个人——某位天使，哪怕就在那里——好对他的饥渴之心说一句善意的话。

===Original===
Once he entered one of those gin-palaces, which, like the golden gates of hell, entice the miserable to worse misery, and seated himself close to a half-tipsy, good-natured wretch, who made room for him on a bench by the wall. He was comforted even by this proximity to one who would not repel him. But soon the paintings of warlike action—of knights, and horses, and mighty deeds done with battle-axe, and broadsword, which adorned the—panels all round, drove him forth even from this heaven of the damned; yet not before the impious thought had arisen in his heart, that the brilliantly painted and sculptural ceiling, with the gilded vine-leaves and bunches of grapes trained up the windows, all lighted with the great shining chandeliers, was only a microcosmic repetition of the bright heavens and the glowing earth, that overhung and surrounded the misery of man. But the memory of how kindly they had comforted and elevated him, at one period of his painful history, not only banished the wicked thought, but brought him more quiet, in the resurrection of a past blessing, than he had known for some time. The period, however, was now at hand when a new grief, followed by a new and more elevated activity, was to do its part towards the closing up of the fountain of bitterness.

===Chinese===
有一次，他走进了那样一家金酒馆（gin-palace）——这类酒馆仿佛地狱的金门，引诱不幸的人陷入更深的不幸——他在墙边一条长凳上，紧挨着一个半醉、好脾气的可怜虫坐下，那人给他让出了位置。身边有这么一个不会排斥他的人，他竟从中得了些许安慰。但不久，四周壁板上装饰的那些战斗题材的画作——骑士、战马，以及用战斧和阔剑完成的赫赫武功——便把他连这等该死之人的天堂也驱了出去；可在离去之前，他心中已生出了一个不敬的念头：那绘制得辉煌夺目、带有雕塑感的穹顶，那攀附在窗上的镀金葡萄叶与一串串葡萄，再加上那些巨大的、光芒四射的枝形吊灯，都不过是那笼罩、环绕着人类苦难的明丽苍穹与炽燃大地的一次微缩再现。但是，回想起在他那段痛苦历史中的某个时期，那一切曾如何善意地安慰过他、提升过他，这回忆不仅驱走了那恶念，更在一段往日祝福的复活之中，给他带来了许久未有的宁静。然而，一个新的悲哀、随之而来的一种更高尚的活动，即将来临，要在封住那苦泉之事上尽它的一份力。

===Original===
Amongst his fellow-labourers, he had for a short time taken some interest in observing a young woman, who had lately joined them. There was nothing remarkable about her, except what at first sight seemed a remarkable plainness. A slight scar over one of her rather prominent eyebrows, increased this impression of plainness. But the first day had not passed, before he began to see that there was something not altogether common in those deep eyes; and the plain look vanished before a closer observation, which also discovered, in the forehead and the lines of the mouth, traces of sorrow or other suffering. There was an expression, too, in the whole face, of fixedness of purpose, without any hardness of determination. Her countenance altogether seemed the index to an interesting mental history. Signs of mental trouble were always an attraction to him; in this case so great, that he overcame his shyness, and spoke to her one evening as they left the works. He often walked home with her after that; as, indeed, was natural, seeing that she occupied an attic in the same poor lodging-house in which he lived himself. The street did not bear the best character; nor, indeed, would the occupations of all the inmates of the house have stood investigation; but so retiring and quiet was this girl, and so seldom did she go abroad after work hours, that he had not discovered till then that she lived in the same street, not to say the same house with himself.

===Chinese===
在他的同工之中，他曾有一阵子对观察一位年轻女子生出了几分兴趣，她是新近才加入他们中间的。她身上本没什么惹眼之处，除了一眼看去那种颇为显眼的平庸。她一道略显突出的眉毛上方有一道浅浅的疤痕，更强化了这种平庸的印象。可是头一天还没过完，他便开始看出，那双深邃的眼睛里有些并不寻常的东西；那张平庸的脸在更近的端详之下消失了，这端详还顺带在她额头和嘴角线条中发现了忧愁或其他苦痛的痕迹。整张脸还有一种神情：一种目标的凝定，却无丝毫决心的生硬。她的容貌整体看来，仿佛是一段耐人寻味的心史的索引。心绪受苦的痕迹，在他眼中向来是一种吸引；这一次的吸引如此之强，以至于他克服了自己的羞怯，在某个傍晚他们一同离开工场时开口与她说话。自那以后他常常陪她走回家；其实这也自然而然，因为她正住在他自己所住的那间破旧客栈的同一间顶层小阁楼里。那条街名声并不算好；事实上，这屋里所有住户的营生经不起细细追究；但这姑娘是那样沉默寡言、安安静静，下了工之后又极少出门，以至于他直到那时才发觉，她不仅与他同住一屋，还与他同住一条街。

===Original===
He soon learned her history—a very common one as outward events, but not surely insignificant because common. Her father and mother were both dead, and hence she had to find her livelihood alone, and amidst associations which were always disagreeable, and sometimes painful. Her quick womanly instinct must have discovered that he too had a history; for though, his mental prostration favouring the operation of outward influences, he had greatly approximated in appearance to those amongst whom he laboured, there were yet signs, besides the educated accent of his speech, which would have distinguished him to an observer; but she put no questions to him, nor made any approach towards seeking a return of the confidence she reposed in him. It was a sensible alleviation to his sufferings to hear her kind voice, and look in her gentle face, as they walked home together; and at length the expectation of this pleasure began to present itself, in the midst of the busy, dreary work-hours, as the shadow of a heaven to close up the dismal, uninteresting day.

===Chinese===
他不久便得知了她的身世——就外在事件而言，是再寻常不过的一段；可正因为寻常，却未必就是无足轻重的。她的父母都已过世，因此她不得不独自谋生，身处一群始终令她不快、有时令她痛苦的伴侣之中。她那敏捷的女子直觉，必定早已察觉他也有过一段经历；因为虽说他的精神颓丧有利于外界影响的渗透，使他在外表上已大大接近与他一同劳作的人，但除却他言谈中那种受过教育的口音之外，仍有种种迹象足以把他在一位观察者眼中区分出来；可她从不向他追问，也从不试图去寻求他回报她所给予他的那份信任。一同回家的路上，听见她温厚的声音，看着她温柔的脸庞，这对他是一种切实可感的痛苦减轻；终于，对这份欢愉的盼望，开始在那些忙碌、枯燥的工作时辰之中浮现，仿佛一道天堂的影子，来收束那阴沉乏味的一天。

===Original===
But one morning he missed her from her place, and a keener pain passed through him than he had felt of late; for he knew that the Plague was abroad, feeding in the low stagnant places of human abode; and he had but too much reason to dread that she might be now struggling in its grasp. He seized the first opportunity of slipping out and hurrying home. He sprang upstairs to her room. He found the door locked, but heard a faint moaning within. To avoid disturbing her, while determined to gain an entrance, he went down for the key of his own door, with which he succeeded in unlocking hers, and so crossed her threshold for the first time. There she lay on her bed, tossing in pain, and beginning to be delirious. Careless of his own life, and feeling that he could not die better than in helping the only friend he had; certain, likewise, of the difficulty of finding a nurse for one in this disease and of her station in life; and sure, likewise, that there could be no question of propriety, either in the circumstances with which they were surrounded, nor in this case of terrible fever almost as hopeless for her as dangerous to him, he instantly began the duties of a nurse, and returned no more to his employment. He had a little money in his possession, for he could not, in the way in which he lived, spend all his wages; so he proceeded to make her as comfortable as he could, with all the pent-up tenderness of a loving heart finding an outlet at length. When a boy at home, he had often taken the place of nurse, and he felt quite capable of performing its duties. Nor was his boyhood far behind yet, although the trials he had come through made it appear an age since he had lost his light heart. So he never left her bedside, except to procure what was necessary for her. She was too ill to oppose any of his measures, or to seek to prohibit his presence. Indeed, by the time he had returned with the first medicine, she was insensible; and she continued so through the whole of the following week, during which time he was constantly with her.

===Chinese===
可是一天早晨，他发觉她的位置空着，一阵比近来所感都更尖锐的痛楚掠过他心头；因为他知道瘟疫正在外头肆虐，在人居之中那些低洼滞闷的角落里觅食；他实在太有理由担心，她此刻也许正挣扎在那瘟疫的掌中。他抓住第一个溜出去的机会，匆匆赶回家。他三步并作两步冲上楼到她的房间。他发觉门锁着，却听见里面传出一声声微弱的呻吟。他决心要进去，却又怕惊扰她，便下楼取来自己房门的钥匙，竟用它打开了她的门，就这样头一次跨过了她家的门槛。她躺在床上，痛得辗转反侧，已开始发起谵妄。他把自家性命置之度外，只觉得能死在帮助他唯一朋友的这件事上，便是死得其所；他也确知，要为这样一个身患此病、处于她那般境地的人找到一位看护，是何等不易；他更确信，无论从他们所处境况来看，还是从这一场对她几乎无望、对他又极度凶险的可怖热病来看，都没有什么礼数可言——于是他立刻担起看护的职责，再也不回工场去了。他手头还有一点钱，因为按他那般活法，工钱是花不完的；他便倾其所有，让她尽可能地舒服些，一颗久被囚禁的爱心所蓄满的温柔，终于找到了出口。他还是个在家孩童之时，便常常充当看护的角色，他自觉完全能够胜任这份差事。虽说自从失去了那颗轻松的心之后，他所经历的磨难使那段童年显得已隔了一世之遥，可那孩童气质其实离他并不远。于是，除开为她置办必需品之外，他寸步不离她的床前。她病得太重，既无力反对他任何一项安排，也无力拒绝他在场。事实上，等他取来第一批药回到她身边时，她已全然不省人事；此后整整一个星期她都如此，他则一直陪在她身旁。

===Original===
That action produces feeling is as often true as its converse; and it is not surprising that, while he smoothed the pillow for her head, he should have made a nest in his heart for the helpless girl. Slowly and unconsciously he learned to love her. The chasm between his early associations and the circumstances in which he found her, vanished as he drew near to the simple, essential womanhood. His heart saw hers and loved it; and he knew that, the centre once gained, he could, as from the fountain of life, as from the innermost secret of the holy place, the hidden germ of power and possibility, transform the outer intellect and outermost manners as he pleased. With what a thrill of joy, a feeling for a long time unknown to him, and till now never known in this form or with this intensity, the thought arose in his heart that here lay one who some day would love him; that he should have a place of refuge and rest; one to lie in his bosom and not despise him! “For,” said he to himself, “I will call forth her soul from where it sleeps, like an unawakened echo, in an unknown cave; and like a child, of whom I once dreamed, that was mine, and to my delight turned in fear from all besides, and clung to me, this soul of hers will run with bewildered, half-sleeping eyes, and tottering steps, but with a cry of joy on its lips, to me as the life-giver. She will cling to me and worship me. Then will I tell her, for she must know all, that I am low and contemptible; that I am an outcast from the world, and that if she receive me, she will be to me as God. And I will fall down at her feet and pray her for comfort, for life, for restoration to myself; and she will throw herself beside me, and weep and love me, I know. And we will go through life together, working hard, but for each other; and when we die, she shall lead me into paradise as the prize her angel-hand found cast on a desert shore, from the storm of winds and waves which I was too weak to resist—and raised, and tended, and saved.” Often did such thoughts as these pass through his mind while watching by her bed; alternated, checked, and sometimes destroyed, by the fears which attended her precarious condition, but returning with every apparent betterment or hopeful symptom.

===Chinese===
行动能生出感情，正如感情能生出行动，二者同样常见地真实；因此，当他在为她抚平头下的枕头之时，竟为这无助的姑娘在自己心里筑了一个窠巢，也就不足为奇了。他慢慢地、不知不觉地学会了她。在他早年的牵念与她所处境遇之间的那道鸿沟，随着他走近那素朴而本质的女性气质而消融了。他的心看见了她的心，便爱上了它；他知道，一旦赢得了这中心，他便能从这生命之泉，从这至圣所最内在的奥秘，从这藏匿的力量与可能性的胚芽之中，随心所欲地改造她外在的才智与最外层的举止。怀着怎样一阵久未体验、迄今从未以此种形式、此种强度出现过的喜悦之颤动，这样一个念头在他心头升起：这里躺着一个人，有朝一日会爱他；他将有一处避难与安息之所；有一个人会卧在他怀中而不轻看他！“因为，”他对自己说，“我要唤醒她的灵魂，让它从它沉睡之处——像一个未醒的回声，在一座无名的洞穴里——走出来；就像我曾梦见的一个孩子，那孩子是我的，满心欢喜地，他惧怕一切旁人，唯独依恋我；她的灵魂也会这样，带着迷惘半睡的眼睛，蹒跚的脚步，唇上却挂着一声明亮的欢呼，向我这位赐生命者奔来。她会依恋我，敬拜我。那时我便告诉她——因为她必须知道一切——我卑微可鄙；我是被这世界放逐的人；如果她肯接纳我，她于我便如同上帝。我会伏在她脚前，求她给我安慰，给我生命，把我归还给我自己；她会倒在我身旁，哭着爱我，我知道。我们要一同走完这一生，辛辛苦苦，却为着彼此；当我们死去之时，她要领我进乐园，当作她那天使之手在荒凉海岸上捡到的战利品——那是从一场我无力抵抗的风浪风暴之中被抛上岸的——她把它扶起，照料，救活。”守在她床边之时，这类念头常常掠过他心间；它们被她危殆病情所伴生的种种恐惧所交替、所遏制，有时被击碎，却又随着每一个看似好转的迹象或一线希望而重来。

===Original===
I will not stop to decide the nice question, how far the intention was right, of causing her to love him before she knew his story. If in the whole matter there was too much thought of self, my only apology is the sequel. One day, the ninth from the commencement of her illness, a letter arrived, addressed to her; which he, thinking he might prevent some inconvenience thereby, opened and read, in the confidence of that love which already made her and all belonging to her appear his own. It was from a soldier—*her lover*. It was plain that they had been betrothed before he left for the continent a year ago; but this was the first letter which he had written to her. It breathed changeless love, and hope, and confidence in her. He was so fascinated that he read it through without pause.

===Chinese===
我不愿停下来裁定这个微妙的问题：在她还不知道他的过往之前，便使她爱上他，这一意图究竟有几分正当。若在这整件事里，他对自我的考量太多，那我唯一的辩白便是后文。一日，自她发病算起的第九天，一封寄给她的信送到了；他想着或许能借此避免某种不便，便凭着那份已使她和属于她的一切在他眼中都显得是他自己一般的爱，拆开读了。信是一个士兵——*她的情人*——写来的。看得出，一年前他动身去大陆之前，他们就已订下婚约；但这却是他写给她的第一封信。字里行间吐露着不变的爱、希望和对她的信赖。他看得入了迷，竟一口气读到底。

===Original===
Laying it down, he sat pale, motionless, almost inanimate. From the hard-won sunny heights, he was once more cast down into the shadow of death. The second storm of his life began, howling and raging, with yet more awful lulls between. “Is she not *mine*?” he said, in agony. “Do I not feel that she is mine? Who will watch over her as I? Who will kiss her soul to life as I? Shall she be torn away from me, when my soul seems to have dwelt with hers forever in an eternal house? But have I not a right to her? Have I not given my life for hers? Is he not a soldier, and are there not many chances that he may never return? And it may be that, although they were engaged in word, soul has never touched soul with them; their love has never reached that point where it passes from the mortal to the immortal, the indissoluble: and so, in a sense, she may be yet free. Will he do for her what I will do? Shall this precious heart of hers, in which I see the buds of so many beauties, be left to wither and die?”

===Chinese===
他把信放下，坐着，苍白、一动不动，几乎没了生气。从好不容易赢得的阳光高处，他再一次被掷入了死亡的阴影之中。他人生的第二场风暴开始了，呼啸、肆虐，其间夹着更为骇人的间歇。“她难道不是*我的*？”他痛苦地说，“我难道不感到她是我的吗？谁会像我这样看护她？谁会像我这样亲吻她的灵魂使它苏醒？当我的灵魂仿佛已与她的同住在一座永恒之屋里，难道她要被从我身边夺走吗？可我对她难道没有权利吗？我难道没有以我的命换她的命吗？他难道不也是个士兵，难道没有许多种可能使他永不归来吗？而且，也许，尽管他们在言语上订了婚，灵魂却从未与他们彼此相触；他们的爱从未达到那从必死通向不朽、不可拆解的一点：所以，在某种意义上，她也许仍是自由的。他会像我这样待她吗？她这颗珍贵的心——我在其中看见那么多美的蓓蕾——难道要被留下枯萎、死去吗？”

===Original===
But here the voice within him cried out, “Art thou the disposer of destinies? Wilt thou, in a universe where the visible God hath died for the Truth’s sake, do evil that a good, which He might neglect or overlook, may be gained? Leave thou her to Him, and do thou right.” And he said within himself, “Now is the real trial for my life! Shall I conquer or no?” And his heart awoke and cried, “I will. God forgive me for wronging the poor soldier! A brave man, brave at least, is better for her than I.”

===Chinese===
但此刻他心里的声音呼喊起来：“你岂是命运的支配者？在这位可见的上帝已为真理而死的宇宙之中，你竟要为着一个他也许不曾留意、不曾看顾的善，而去做恶吗？你把她交托给他，你自己只管行正道。”他在自己心里说：“现在才是我一生真正的试炼！我究竟胜得过，还是胜不过？”他的心苏醒过来，呼喊道：“我胜得过。愿上帝宽恕我对那可怜士兵所行的不义！一个勇敢的人——至少是勇敢的人——比我更适合她。”

===Original===
A great strength arose within him, and lifted him up to depart. “Surely I may kiss her once,” he said. For the crisis was over, and she slept. He stooped towards her face, but before he had reached her lips he saw her eyelids tremble; and he who had longed for the opening of those eyes, as of the gates of heaven, that she might love him, stricken now with fear lest she should love him, fled from her, before the eyelids that hid such strife and such victory from the unconscious maiden had time to unclose. But it was agony—quietly to pack up his bundle of linen in the room below, when he knew she was lying awake above, with her dear, pale face, and living eyes! What remained of his money, except a few shillings, he put up in a scrap of paper, and went out with his bundle in his hand, first to seek a nurse for his friend, and then to go he knew not whither. He met the factory people with whom he had worked, going to dinner, and amongst them a girl who had herself but lately recovered from the fever, and was yet hardly able for work. She was the only friend the sick girl had seemed to have amongst the women at the factory, and she was easily persuaded to go and take charge of her. He put the money in her hand, begging her to use it for the invalid, and promising to send the equivalent of her wages for the time he thought she would have to wait on her. This he easily did by the sale of a ring, which, besides his mother’s watch, was the only article of value he had retained. He begged her likewise not to mention his name in the matter; and was foolish enough to expect that she would entirely keep the promise she had made him.

===Chinese===
一股大力量在他里面升起，把他扶了起来，要他离去。“我总可以亲她一次吧，”他说。因为危机已过，她正睡着。他俯身向她的脸，但还未够到她的唇，他便看见她的眼皮颤动了一下；这个曾渴望那双眼睛睁开——仿佛渴望天堂之门大开——好让她爱上他的人，此刻却因怕她爱上他而恐惧万分，在那隐藏着这般挣扎、这般胜利、不令那毫无察觉的少女知晓的眼皮还来不及睁开之前，便从她身边逃走了。可是，这又是何等的痛苦——在楼下的房间里静静地收拾他那包衣物，而他却知道她正醒着躺在楼上，带着她那张亲爱的、苍白的脸和那一双活生生的眼睛！他除了留下几先令之外，把所余的钱都用一小片纸包好，一手提着包袱走出门去，先去为他这位朋友寻一位看护，然后再去——他也不知道去往何处。他遇见与他一同做工的工场同伴正去吃午饭，其中有一个姑娘，自己前不久才从那场热病中康复，还几乎不能胜任活计。她是那病女孩在工场女工之中唯一显得有情分的朋友，他轻易便说服了她去照看她。他把钱放到她手里，恳求她把这些钱用在病人身上，并许诺会按他估算她需要照看病人的时间，把相当于她工钱的钱寄去。这件事他轻而易举地办到了——靠卖掉一枚戒指，那是他除母亲留下的怀表之外唯一留存的一件值钱物事。他还求她在这件事上别提他的名字；他竟愚蠢到指望她会完全信守对他许下的承诺。

===Original===
Wandering along the street, purposeless now and bereft, he spied a recruiting party at the door of a public-house; and on coming nearer, found, by one of those strange coincidences which do occur in life, and which have possibly their root in a hidden and wondrous law, that it was a party, perhaps a remnant, of the very regiment in which he had himself served, and in which his misfortune had befallen him. Almost simultaneously with the shock which the sight of the well-known number on the soldiers’ knapsacks gave him, arose in his mind the romantic, ideal thought, of enlisting in the ranks of this same regiment, and recovering, as a private soldier and unknown, that honour which as officer he had lost. To this determination, the new necessity in which he now stood for action and change of life, doubtless contributed, though unconsciously. He offered himself to the sergeant; and, notwithstanding that his dress indicated a mode of life unsuitable as the antecedent to a soldier’s, his appearance, and the necessity for recruits combined, led to his easy acceptance.

===Chinese===
他在街上漫无目的地踯躅，心神俱丧，忽然瞥见一家酒馆门前有一队招募新兵的人马；走近一看，他竟凭着人生中那些确实会发生、也许根植于某条隐秘而奇妙的法则之上的奇巧因缘，发觉这正是他曾服役、也曾遭逢不幸的那个团的一队人马——也许是仅存的一股。几乎就在他因士兵背包上那熟悉的番号而感到震撼的同时，一个浪漫而理想的念头在他心头升起：他要投身到这个团的行列里去，以一个无名小卒的列兵身份，重新赢回他以军官身份所失去的荣誉。促成这一决心的，无疑还有他此刻所面临的必须有所行动、必须改变生活的全新迫切——尽管他自己并未察觉。他向那位中士毛遂自荐；虽说他的衣着所显示的生活方式并不适合作为士兵的前身，但他的仪表再加上对新兵的迫切需求，使他轻易便被接纳了。

===Original===
The English armies were employed in expelling the enemy from an invaded and helpless country. Whatever might be the political motives which had induced the Government to this measure, the young man was now able to feel that he could go and fight, individually and for his part, in the cause of liberty. He was free to possess his own motives for joining in the execution of the schemes of those who commanded his commanders.

===Chinese===
英国大军正受命驱逐敌人，使其退出一座被侵犯而无助的国度。无论政府采取此一举措的政治动机为何，这青年如今已能感到：他可以个别地、就他这一份而言，为自由的事业奔赴战场、奋勇作战。那些下令给他的上级的人，自有他们执行种种计划的缘故；而他投身其间，也大可怀抱他自己的动机。

===Original===
With a heavy heart, but with more of inward hope and strength than he had ever known before, he marched with his comrades to the seaport and embarked. It seemed to him that because he had done right in his last trial, here was a new glorious chance held out to his hand. True, it was a terrible change to pass from a woman in whom he had hoped to find healing, into the society of rough men, to march with them, “*mitgleichem Tritt und Schritt*,” up to the bristling bayonets or the horrid vacancy of the cannon mouth. But it was the only cure for the evil that consumed his life.

===Chinese===
他怀着沉重的心，却又怀着前所未有的内在的希望与力量，与他的战友们一同行军到海港，登船出发。在他看来，正因为他上一次试炼中行了正道，所以这里才有一线新的、光荣的机会伸到了他手边。诚然，从一位他曾希望从中寻得医治的女子，转而投身到一群粗鲁汉子之中，与他们一同“*迈着齐整的步调（mitgleichem Tritt und Schritt）*”开向森然的刺刀，或炮口那可怖的虚空——这是一种可怕的转变。但这是医治那吞噬他生命的恶疾的唯一良方。

===Original===
He reached the army in safety, and gave himself, with religious assiduity, to the smallest duties of his new position. No one had a brighter polish on his arms, or whiter belts than he. In the necessary movements, he soon became precise to a degree that attracted the attention of his officers; while his character was remarkable for all the virtues belonging to a perfect soldier.

===Chinese===
他平安抵达军中，以一种近乎宗教般的勤勉，投身于他新职位上最微末的职责。再没有谁比他的兵器擦得更亮，皮带比他更白。在那些必要的操演之中，他很快就以精确到令人侧目的程度引起了军官们的注意；而他的品行，则以一名完美士兵所应有的一切美德著称。

===Original===
One day, as he stood sentry, he saw the eyes of his colonel intently fixed on him. He felt his lip quiver, but he compressed and stilled it, and tried to look as unconscious as he could; which effort was assisted by the formal bearing required by his position. Now the colonel, such had been the losses of the regiment, had been promoted from a lieutenancy in the same, and had belonged to it at the time of the ensign’s degradation. Indeed, had not the changes in the regiment been so great, he could hardly have escaped so long without discovery. But the poor fellow would have felt that his name was already free of reproach, if he had seen what followed on the close inspection which had awakened his apprehensions, and which, in fact, had convinced the colonel of his identity with the disgraced ensign. With a hasty and less soldierly step than usual the colonel entered his tent, threw himself on his bed and wept like a child. When he rose he was overheard to say these words—and these only escaped his lips: “He is nobler than I.”

===Chinese===
一日，他站岗之时，发觉上校的目光正凝神落在他身上。他觉得自己的嘴唇颤了一下，但他紧紧抿住，让它平静下来，竭力装作若无其事；岗位所要求的那种规整仪态，也助了他一臂之力。原来这位上校——因团里损失惨重——正是从这个团的中尉擢升上来的，在那少尉被革职之时，他就在团里。其实，倘若团里的人事变迁没有这么大，他恐怕早就无法逃过被人发觉的命运这么久了。但是，倘若这可怜人看见了那次令他惴惴不安的仔细端详之后所发生的事——那次端详其实已使上校确信，他就是当年那个蒙羞的少尉——他便会感到自己的名字早已洗清了耻辱。只见上校迈着比平日急促、也不那么像个军人的步子走进自己的帐篷，一头扑倒在床上，像个孩子似地哭了起来。他起身之时，有人听见他说出了这样一句话——他唇间只吐出这一句：“他比我高尚。”

===Original===
But this officer showed himself worthy of commanding such men as this private; for right nobly did he understand and meet his feelings. He uttered no word of the discovery he had made, till years afterwards; but it soon began to be remarked that whenever anything arduous, or in any manner distinguished, had to be done, this man was sure to be of the party appointed. In short, as often as he could, the colonel “set him in the forefront of the battle.” Passing through all with wonderful escape, he was soon as much noticed for his reckless bravery, as hitherto for his precision in the discharge of duties bringing only commendation and not honour. But his final lustration was at hand.

===Chinese===
然而，这位军官证明了自己配得上统率像这个列兵这样的人；因为他极其体面地理解并回应了这个列兵的心情。对于自己的这一发现，他直到数年之后才吐露一字；但不久人们就注意到，每逢有什么艰巨、或在某方面不同凡响的事要做，这人必定在被挑中的那一队人马里。简而言之，上校一有机会便“把他放在战阵的最前列”。他一次次以令人惊异的侥幸安然通过，不久便以奋不顾身的英勇而引人瞩目，一如他此前以履职的精确著称——只是那精确所换来的，不过是嘉许，而非荣誉。然而，他最后的洁净之礼近在眼前了。

===Original===
A great part of the army was hastening, by forced marches, to raise the siege of a town which was already on the point of falling into the hands of the enemy. Forming one of a reconnoitring party, which preceded the main body at some considerable distance, he and his companions came suddenly upon one of the enemy’s outposts, occupying a high, and on one side precipitous rock, a short way from the town, which it commanded. Retreat was impossible, for they were already discovered, and the bullets were falling amongst them like the first of a hailstorm. The only possibility of escape remaining for them was a nearly hopeless improbability. It lay in forcing the post on this steep rock; which if they could do before assistance came to the enemy, they might, perhaps, be able to hold out, by means of its defences, till the arrival of the army. Their position was at once understood by all; and, by a sudden, simultaneous impulse, they found themselves halfway up the steep ascent, and in the struggle of a close conflict, without being aware of any order to that effect from their officer. But their courage was of no avail; the advantages of the place were too great; and in a few minutes the whole party was cut to pieces, or stretched helpless on the rock. Our youth had fallen amongst the foremost; for a musquet ball had grazed his skull, and laid him insensible.

===Chinese===
大军的一大部分正以急行军赶去解一座城之围——那城已濒临落入敌手。他作为一支侦察小队的一员，在大队人马之前相当远的地方先行；他和同伴们猝然撞上了敌方的一处前哨，那前哨占据着离城不远的一座高岩，岩的一面陡峭异常，可俯瞰全城。退却已不可能，因为他们已被发觉，子弹像冰雹初降般落下，夹杂在他们之间。他们剩下的唯一一线生机，近乎是一种无望的渺茫可能。那便是强攻这座陡岩上的据点；倘若他们能在敌人得到增援之前做到这一点，也许就能凭它的工事坚守到大军到来。众人一时都明白了彼此的处境；在一股突发的、不约而同的冲动之下，他们发觉自己已冲到了陡坡的半途，陷入了贴身搏斗的混战之中，却谁也没意识到军官下过这样的命令。但他们的勇猛无济于事；地势之利太过悬殊；几分钟之内，整队人便被砍杀殆尽，或无力地横陈在岩石之上。我们的青年倒在了最前面的人之中；一颗滑膛枪弹擦过他的颅骨，把他打得失去了知觉。

===Original===
But consciousness slowly returned, and he succeeded at last in raising himself and looking around him. The place was deserted. A few of his friends, alive, but grievously wounded, lay near him. The rest were dead. It appeared that, learning the proximity of the English forces from this rencontre with part of their advanced guard, and dreading lest the town, which was on the point of surrendering, should after all be snatched from their grasp, the commander of the enemy’s forces had ordered an immediate and general assault; and had for this purpose recalled from their outposts the whole of his troops thus stationed, that he might make the attempt with the utmost strength he could accumulate.

===Chinese===
但意识缓缓归来，他终于勉强撑起身子，四下环顾。那地方已空无一人。他身旁躺着几个同伴，还活着，却伤势惨重。其余的人俱已死去。看来，敌方主帅从这一次与他们前哨部队一部分的遭遇之中，得知英军已近在咫尺，又担心那座即将投降的城终会被从他掌中夺走，便下令立即发起总攻；为此他把驻守各前哨的全部人马都召回，好以他能集结的最大兵力发起这一击。

===Original===
As the youth’s power of vision returned, he perceived, from the height where he lay, that the town was already in the hands of the enemy. But looking down into the level space immediately below him, he started to his feet at once; for a girl, bareheaded, was fleeing towards the rock, pursued by several soldiers. “Aha!” said he, divining her purpose—the soldiers behind and the rock before her—“I will help you to die!” And he stooped and wrenched from the dead fingers of a sergeant the sword which they clenched by the bloody hilt. A new throb of life pulsed through him to his very fingertips; and on the brink of the unseen world he stood, with the blood rushing through his veins in a wild dance of excitement. One who lay near him wounded, but recovered afterwards, said that he looked like one inspired. With a keen eye he watched the chase. The girl drew nigh; and rushed up the path near which he was standing. Close on her footsteps came the soldiers, the distance gradually lessening between them.

===Chinese===
随着这青年视力的恢复，他从他所躺的高处看出，那城已落入敌手。但当他俯瞰身下那片紧贴着的平地时，他猛地一下跳了起来；因为一个未戴帽子的姑娘正朝岩石奔来，后面有几个士兵紧紧追赶。“哈！”他说，已猜出了她的心意——身后是士兵，身前是岩石——“我来助你赴死！”他俯下身，从一名中士僵硬死去的手指间，硬生生掰出了那柄被血污的剑柄所紧握的剑。一股新的生命搏动贯穿他全身，直抵指尖；他站在那不可见世界的边缘，血液在血管里狂野地奔涌起舞。一个躺在他身旁受伤、后来康复的人说，他那时看上去犹如神灵附体。他以锐利的目光注视着那场追逐。姑娘奔近了；她冲上了他身旁的那条小径。士兵紧追在她脚后，彼此间的距离一点点缩短。

===Original===
Not many paces higher up, was a narrower part of the ascent, where the path was confined by great stones, or pieces of rock. Here had been the chief defence in the preceding assault, and in it lay many bodies of his friends. Thither he went and took his stand.

===Chinese===
再往上几步，便是那条上坡路更窄的一段，小径被巨石或岩块夹束。先前那一波强攻时，这里曾是主要的防御之处，其中横陈着他许多同伴的尸首。他便走向那里，立定了脚跟。

===Original===
On the girl came, over the dead, with rigid hands and flying feet, the bloodless skin drawn tight on her features, and her eyes awfully large and wild. She did not see him though she bounded past so near that her hair flew in his eyes. “Never mind!” said he, “we shall meet soon.” And he stepped into the narrow path just in time to face her pursuers—between her and them. Like the red lightning the bloody sword fell, and a man beneath it. *Cling! clang!* went the echoes in the rocks—and another man was down; for, in his excitement, he was a destroying angel to the breathless pursuers. His stature rose, his chest dilated; and as the third foe fell dead, the girl was safe; for her body lay a broken, empty, but undesecrated temple, at the foot of the rock. That moment his sword flew in shivers from his grasp. The next instant he fell, pierced to the heart; and his spirit rose triumphant, free, strong, and calm, above the stormy world, which at length lay vanquished beneath him.

===Chinese===
姑娘奔了过来，越过死人，双手僵直，两脚如飞，毫无血色的皮肤紧绷在她五官之上，眼睛大得骇人、狂乱。她并没有看见他，尽管她从他身旁那么近的地方一跃而过，以至于她的发丝拂过了他的眼睛。“不打紧！”他说，“我们很快就会相见。”他一步踏入那条窄径，恰好迎上她的追兵——挡在她与他们之间。像一道赤色闪电，那柄血污的剑落下，一个人倒在其下。*当！啷！*岩石间回声激荡——又一个人倒下；因为在他那一腔激昂之中，他对那些气喘吁吁的追兵而言，不啻一位毁灭的天使。他的身形拔高了，胸膛扩张了；当第三个敌人倒地死去之时，姑娘已得了安全；因为她那具躯体——一座破碎、空寂、却未被亵渎的殿宇——已横陈在岩石脚下。就在那一刻，他手中的剑化作碎片飞了出去。下一瞬，他倒下了，一击透心；他的灵魂凯旋地升起，自由、坚强而宁静，凌驾于那风暴般的世界之上——那世界终于伏在他脚下，被他征服。

## I Husband and Wife / 一 夫与妻

===Original===
I am going to tell a story of married life. My title will prepare the reader for something hardly heroic; but I trust it will not be found lacking in the one genuine and worthy interest a tale ought to have—namely, that it presents a door through which we may walk into one region or another of the human heart, and there find ourselves not altogether unacquainted or from home.
===Chinese===
我要讲一个关于婚姻生活的故事。我的题目会使读者有所准备，知道这故事里没有什么英雄气概可言；但我相信，它不会缺少一个故事所该具有的那种唯一真切而值得珍视的意趣——也就是说，它呈现了一扇门，让我们可以由此走进人心的某一处或另一处，在那里发觉自己并不全然陌生，也不算身在异乡。

===Original===
There was a law among the Jews which forbade the yoking together of certain animals, either because, being unequal in size or strength, one of them must be oppressed, or for the sake of some lesson thus embodied to the Eastern mind—possibly for both reasons. Half the tragedy would be taken out of social life if this law could be applied to human beings in their various relations. I do not say that this would be well, or that we could afford to lose the result of the tragedy thus occasioned. Neither do I believe that there are so many instances of unequal yoking as the misprising judgments of men by men and women by women might lead us to imagine. Not everyone declared by the wisdom of acquaintance to have thrown himself or herself away must therefore be set down as unequally yoked. Or it may even be that the inequality is there, but the loss on the other side. How some people could ever have come together must always be a puzzle until one knows the history of the affair; but not a few whom most of us would judge quite unsuited to each other do yet get on pretty well from, the first, and better and better the longer they are together, and that with mutual advantage, improvement, and development. Essential humanity is deeper than the accidents of individuality; the common is more powerful than the peculiar; and the honest heart will always be learning to act more and more in accordance with the laws of its being. It must be of much more consequence to any lady that her husband should be a man on whose word she can depend than that he should be of a gracious presence. But if instead of coming nearer to a true understanding of each other, the two should from the first keep falling asunder, then something tragic may almost be looked for.
===Chinese===
犹太人有一条律法，禁止将某些牲畜同负一轭，或是因为它们体型、气力不均，其中之一必受压抑；或是为了借这事向东土之人喻示某种道理——也许两般缘故兼而有之。倘若这条律法也能施用于人际之间的种种关系，社交生活里的悲剧便要减去一半。我不敢说这便是好事，也不敢说我们承受得起因而失去的那些悲剧所带来的果实。我同样不信世间真有那么多不相称的同负一轭，并非男人评断男人、女人评断女人时所发出的那些鄙薄的论定会引领我们去想象的那般多。凡是被相识的"明智"判定为自暴自弃的人，未必就该记作同负了不均之轭。抑或，那不相称确乎存在，吃亏的却是在另一头。有些人究竟是怎么走到一起的，必永远是个谜，直到人晓得这段姻缘的来龙去脉方休；可是在我们大多数人看来极不相配的男女之中，也有不少从最初便相处得不坏，愈是长相厮守愈是和睦，且彼此受益、共同长进、彼此成就。本质的人性，比个体的种种偶然更深；共通之物，比特殊之物更有力；而诚实的心，总会日益学会依照自身存在的法则行事。对任何一位太太而言，丈夫是个言而有信的人，比他生就一副悦目的仪表，要紧得多。然而倘若两人不是日渐走近对彼此的真切理解，反倒从一开始就不断分崩离析，那么一场近乎悲剧的事，几乎便可以预见了。

===Original===
Duncan and Lucy Dempster were a couple the very mention of whose Christian names together would have seemed amusing to the friends who had long ceased to talk of their unfitness. Indeed, I doubt if in their innermost privacy they ever addressed each other except as Mr. and Mrs. Dempster. For the first time to see them together, no one could help wondering how the conjunction could have been effected. Dempster was of Scotch descent, but the hereditary high cheekbone seemed to have got into his nose, which was too heavy a pendant for the low forehead from which it hung. About an inch from the end it took a swift and unexpected curve downwards, and was a curious and abnormal nose, which could not properly be assorted with any known class of noses. A long upper lip, a large, firm, and not quite ugly mouth, with a chin both long and square, completed a face which, with its low forehead, being yet longer than usual, had a particularly equine look. He was rather under the middle height, slender, and well enough made—altogether an ordinary mortal, known on ’Change as an able, keen, and laborious man of business. What his special business was I do not know. He went to the city by the eight o’clock omnibus every morning, dived into a court, entered a little square, rushed up two flights of stairs to a couple of rooms, and sat down in the back one before an office table on a hair-seated chair. It was a dingy place—not so dirty as it looked, I daresay. Even the windows, being of bad glass, did, I believe, look dirtier than they were. It was a place where, so far as the eye of an outsider could tell, much or nothing might be doing. Its occupant always wore his hat in it, and his hat always looked shabby. Some people said he was rich, others that he would be one day. Some said he was a responsible man, whatever the epithet may have been intended to mean. I believe he was quite as honest as the recognized laws of his trade demanded—and for how many could I say more? Nobody said he was avaricious—but then he moved amongst men whose very notion was first to make money, after that to be religious, or to enjoy themselves, as the case might be. And no one either ever said of him that he was a good man, or a generous. He was about forty years of age, looking somehow as if he had never been younger. He had had a fair education—better than is generally considered necessary for mercantile purposes—but it would have been hard to discover any signs of it in the spending of his leisure. On Sunday mornings he went with his wife to church, and when he came home had a good dinner, of which now and then a friend took his share. If no stranger was present he took his wine by himself, and went to sleep in his easy chair of marone-coloured leather, while his wife sat on the other side of the fire if it was winter, or a little way off by the open window if it was summer, gently yawned now and then, and looked at him with eyes a little troubled. Then he went off again by the eight o’clock omnibus on Monday morning, and not an idea more or less had he in his head, not a hair’s-breadth of difference was there in his conduct or pursuits, that he had been to church and had spent the day out of business. That may, however, for anything I know, have been as much the clergyman’s fault as his. He was the sort of man you might call machine-made, one in whom humanity, if in no wise caricatured, was yet in no wise ennobled.
===Chinese===
邓肯·登普斯特（Duncan Dempster）与露西·登普斯特（Lucy Dempster）这对夫妇，单是把他们的教名一并提起，便已足以令那些早已不再议论他俩不相配的朋友们发笑。说实话，我疑心即便是在他们最私密的两人之间，相互称呼的也不过是"登普斯特先生""登普斯特太太"。初次见到他们同在一起，任谁都不免纳闷：这样的结合究竟是怎么撮合成的。登普斯特祖上是苏格兰人（of Scotch descent），不过那份世代相传的高颧骨，倒像是跑进了他的鼻子里——那鼻子从低矮的额头上垂下来，未免是个太过沉重的坠饰。离鼻尖大约一英寸处，它骤然出乎意料地向下拐了个弯，可谓一只古怪而反常的鼻子，无法妥当地归入任何已知的鼻子类别。再配上一张长上唇、一张阔大而紧抿、却不怎么丑的嘴，以及一个又长又方的下巴，便凑成了一张脸：因前额低矮、脸又比常人更长，便显出一种格外像马的神气。他身量略低于中等，瘦削，身形倒也算匀称——总而言之，是个再普通不过的凡夫，在交易所里被人称作一个干练、精明、勤勉的生意人。他究竟做的是哪一行生意，我并不知晓。每日清晨他乘八点钟的公共马车进城，钻进一条庭院小道，步入一方小广场，奔上两段楼梯，进得两间屋子，便在后一间里那张铺着马尾毛垫子的椅子坐下，面对着一张办公桌。那是个昏暗的去处——我料想其实并不像看上去那般脏。就连那几扇窗，因玻璃粗劣，我相信瞧着也比实际更脏些。那是个外人的目光所及之处，看不出大有可为还是无所事事的地方。屋子里的主人总戴着帽子，那帽子总是一副寒碜相。有人说他富有，有人说他总有一日会富。有人说他是个"靠得住"的人——随这句形容词究竟意在何处。我相信他与其行业所公认的法则所要求的一样诚实——又有多少人我能作此语呢？没人说他贪婪——可话又说回来，他所往来之人，根本的观念便是先把钱财挣到手，至于此后是否要虔诚信教、是否要自得其乐，则视情形而定。也从来没有人说过他是个好人，或是个慷慨的人。他约莫四十岁年纪，可瞧着却仿佛从未年轻过。他受过相当不错的教育——比一般认为经商所必需的还要好些——然而要在他的闲暇时光里寻出受教育的丝毫痕迹，却难如登天。星期日早晨他偕妻同往教堂，归家后吃一顿好饭，偶尔也有朋友来同享。若无外客在场，他便独自饮酒，在那张栗红色皮的安乐椅里沉沉睡去；妻子则坐在火的另一边——若值冬日——或在夏日则坐在敞开的窗前稍远处，偶尔轻轻打个哈欠，一双眸子带着些许不安望着他。到了星期一早晨，他又照旧乘八点钟的公共马车出门，而他既去了教堂、又歇了一日不在生意上，竟没有因此脑子里多出一个或半个念头，也没有因此举止和营生有了一丝一毫的改变。然而，据我所知，这恐怕与他无关，倒该怪那牧师。他是那种你尽可以称作机器模子里倒出来的人，其身上的人性虽未被扭曲丑化，却也同样未得半点提升。

===Original===
His wife was ten years younger than he—hardly less than beautiful—only that over her countenance seemed to have gathered a kind of haze of commonness. At first sight, notwithstanding, one could not help perceiving that she was china and he was delft. She was graceful as she sat, long-necked, slope-shouldered, and quite as tall as her husband, with a marked daintiness about her in the absence of the extremes of the fashion, in the quality of the lace she wore on her black silk dress, and in the wide white sleeves of fine cambric that covered her arms from the shoulder to the wrist. She had a morally delicate air, a look of scrupulous nicety and lavender-stored linen. She had long dark lashes; and when they rose, the eyelids revealed eyes of uncommon beauty. She had good features, good teeth, and a good complexion. The main feeling she produced and left was of ladyhood—little more.
===Chinese===
他的妻子比他小十岁——几乎称得上美丽——只是她的脸庞上似乎笼着一层庸常的薄雾。尽管如此，初见之下，人不由得便觉出她是细瓷，而他不过是粗陶。她落座时姿态娴雅，颈项纤长、双肩微削，身量与丈夫相齐；她周身有一种分明的精致——虽不趋极端的时新，但她那身黑绸衣裙上所缀花边的质地，以及那对从肩头覆至腕间的细白麻布宽袖，都透着这份考究。她有一种道德上纤柔的气韵，一副一丝不苟、洁净无瑕的神情，仿佛薰衣草香气里贮藏过的亚麻布。她生着长长的深色睫毛；待睫毛一扬，眼睑便露出双异常美的眸子。她五官端正，牙齿齐整，肤色亦佳。她给人的主要感受、所留下的主要印象，便是位淑女——仅此而已。

===Original===
Sunday afternoon came fifty-two times in the year. I mention this because then always, and nearly then only, could one calculate on seeing them together. It came to them in a surburb of London, and the look of it was dull. Doubtless Mr. Dempster’s dinner and his repose after it were interesting to him, but I cannot help thinking his wife found it dreary. She had, however, got used to it. The house was a good old one, of red brick, much larger than they required, but not expensive, and had a general look of the refinement of its mistress. In the summer the windows of the dining-room would generally be open, for they looked into a really lovely garden behind the house, and the scent of the jasmine that crept all around them would come in plentifully. I wonder what the scent of jasmine did in Duncan Dempster’s world. Perhaps it never got farther than the general antechamber of the sensorium. It often made his wife sad—she could not tell why. To him I daresay it smelt agreeable, but I can hardly believe it ever woke in him that dreamy sensation it gave her—of something she had not had enough of, she could not say what. When the heat was gone off a little he would walk out on the lawn, which was well kept and well watered, with many flowering shrubs about it. Why he did so, I cannot tell. He looked at nothing in particular, only walked about for a few minutes, no doubt derived some pleasure of a mild nature from something, and walked in again to tea. One might have expected he would have cultivated the acquaintance of his garden a little, if it were only for the pleasure the contrast would give him when he got back to his loved office, for a greater contrast could not well have been found than between his dingy dreary haunt on weekdays—a place which nothing but duty could have made other than repugnant to any free soul—and this nest of greenery and light and odour. Sweet scents floated in clouds invisible about the place; flower eyes and stars and bells and bunches shone and glowed and lurked all around; his very feet might have learned a lesson of that which is beyond the sense from the turf he trod; but all the time, if he were not exactly seeing in his mind’s eye the walls and tables of his office in the City square, his thoughts were not the less brooding over such business as he there transacted. For Mr. Dempster’s was not a free soul. How could it be when all his energies were given to making money? This he counted his calling—and I believe actually contrived to associate some feeling of duty with the notion of leaving behind him a plump round sum of money, as if money in accumulation and following flood, instead of money in peaceful current, were the good thing for the world! Hence the whole realm of real life, the universe of thought and growth, was a high-hedged park to him, within which he never even tried to look—not even knowing that he was shut out from it, for the hedge was of his own growing. What shall ever wake such a man to a sense of indwelling poverty, or make him begin to hunger after any lowliest expansion? Does a reader retort, “The man was comfortable, and why should he be troubled?” If the end of being, I answer, is only comfort in self, I yield. But what if there should be at the heart of the universe a Thought to which the being of such men is distasteful? What if to that Thought they look blots in light, ugly things? May there not lie in that direction some possible reason why they should bethink themselves? Dempster, however, was not yet a clinker out of which all the life was burned, however much he looked like one. There was in him that which might yet burn—and give light and heat.
===Chinese===
一年之中，星期日的下午要来上五十二回。我提这一点，是因为只有这时候——也几乎仅仅在那时候——才可以稳稳地料定看见他俩同在一处。这下午是在伦敦（London）一处近郊落到他们头上的，那景象很是乏味。登普斯特先生的午餐及其后的休憩，于他自有意趣，自不待言；可我总忍不住想，他的太太怕是觉得百无聊赖。然而她早已习以为常了。那宅子是幢不错的旧屋，红砖所砌，比他们所需的大得多，房租却不算贵，整体透着一种女主人式的雅致。夏日里，餐厅的窗户多半是敞着的，因为窗外正对着屋后一座着实可爱的花园，绕窗攀缘的茉莉，便将香气源源送入。我颇想知道，茉莉花香在邓肯·登普斯特的那个世界里究竟起了什么作用。也许它从来就没越过感官的前厅。那香气常令他的妻子黯然——她说不出缘由。至于他，我承认那气味闻着大抵是怡人的，可我很难相信它曾在他的心头唤起，它在她心头所唤起的那种梦幻之感——那是一种"有什么她未曾得够、却又说不出是什么"的感觉。待暑气稍退，他便走到草坪上去——那草坪打理得齐整、浇灌得湿润，四下里栽着许多花木灌丛。他为何要如此，我无从得知。他什么都不细看，只来回踱上几分钟，无疑从某处得着些温和的乐趣，便又踱回屋里吃茶去了。你本会以为，他多少该培养一下对自家花园的相熟之情，哪怕只是为了在回到他那心爱的办公间时，反差之感所能带给他的那份愉悦；因为再难找出比这更大的反差了——工作日里他那昏暗愁闷的巢穴，若非出于本分，对任何自由的灵魂都只会令人生厌；而这一处，却是绿意、光影与芬芳筑成的窠巢。甜香如无形的云一般在四下飘荡；花儿们的眼睛、星辰、铃铛与一簇簇花束，处处闪烁、灼灼发光、又隐伏于幽微；就连他的双脚，也几乎能从他所踏的那片草皮上学得一课，领会那超乎感官之上的物事。然而这一切之间，他纵使不曾在心眼之中确切地望见城中广场那间办公室的墙壁与案桌，他的心思却照样盘桓在他那儿所经手的那些生意上。因为登普斯特先生的灵魂并不自由。他又怎能自由，既然他全部的精力都倾注在了挣钱上？他把这当作自己的"天职"——而且我相信，他竟真的设法把某种尽忠守责之情，与"身后留下一笔圆鼓鼓的整款"这一念头挂上了钩，仿佛囤积如潮、随之奔涌的钱财，才是于这世界有益的好东西，而非平静流淌的钱财！于是乎，整个真实生活的疆域，那思想与生长的浩瀚宇宙，于他便是一座围了高高树篱的园囿，他连试着朝里张望一回都不曾——甚至不知自己已被关在它的外头，因为那树篱是他自家栽起来的。究竟什么才能唤醒这样的人，让他觉出内里栖居着的贫乏，或令他开始渴慕那最 humble 的一点点扩充呢？读者若反驳："这人不也舒舒服服的，又何必去搅扰他？"——我答曰：倘若人生的终极不过是自身的安逸，那我甘愿认输。可万一宇宙的心脏里，正有一个"意念"，对此辈之存在深感不悦呢？万一在那个"意念"看来，他们是光中的斑渍、是丑陋之物呢？难道沿这个方向，便没有一丝可能的理由，教他们好生反思一番自身么？不过，登普斯特到底还只是一块尚未把生气尽数烧尽的硬砖，尽管他看着很像那样一块。他里头尚有可燃之物——日后还能发光、发热。

===Original===
On the Sunday evenings Mrs. Dempster would have gladly gone to church again, if only—though to herself she never allowed this for one of her reasons—to slip from under the weight of her husband’s presence.
