与此同时，画师家中对沃尔肯利希特而言景况依旧——整日作画；两个年轻人之间不通一言；午饭与酒；活计做完后的默然阅读，间或越过书顶偷瞄过去几眼，却从未换来一次回眸；冷冰冰的晚安；落锁的门；无眠的夜与昏沉的清晨。可末了，一场变故来了，来得比三人中任何一人所预料的都早。因为，究竟是托伊费尔斯比尔斯特的急不可耐驱使他去做更危险的试验，还是他长久以来所用那些手段的持续，终至于压垮了沃尔肯利希特的生机——某一个下午，他正坐在活计跟前，忽地从椅子上跌了下去，他的师父慌忙赶过去一看，只见他浑身僵硬、似已没了气息。这事发生时，莉莉丝并不在画室里。为托伊费尔斯比尔斯特说句公道话，须得承认他已使出自己所精通的一切手段——虽则这些手段用于救人济世并不甚高明——想要唤回这少年；却全无效果。末了，他听见莉莉丝的脚步声，有些着慌地住了手；又为了不让她被她素常见活人的地方竟躺着一具死尸这副景象所惊吓，便把那具人体模型从卧榻上挪开，把卡尔放了上去，盖上一块黑丝绒的棺衣。他正好赶得上。她看见卡尔的位置上没人，吃了一惊，说道——
"你的学生呢，父亲？"
"回家去了。"他答道，脸上带着一种痉挛似的咧嘴笑。

===Original===
She glanced round the room, caught sight of the lay figure where it had not been before, looked at the couch, and saw the pall yet heaved up from beneath, opened her eyes till the entire white sweep around the iris suggested a new expression of consternation to Teufelsbürst, though from a quarter whence he did not desire or look for it; and then, without a word, sat down to a drawing she had been busy upon the day before. But her father, glancing at her now, as Wolkenlicht had used to do, could not help seeing that she was frightfully pale. She showed no other sign of uneasiness. As soon as he released her, she withdrew, with one more glance, as she passed, at the couch and the figure blocked out in black upon it. She hastened to her chamber, shut and locked the door, sat down on the side of the couch, and fell, not a-weeping, but a-thinking. Was he dead? What did it matter? They would all be dead soon. Her mother was dead already. It was only that the earth could not bear more children, except she devoured those to whom she had already given birth. But what if they had to come back in another form, and live another sad, hopeless, loveless life over again?﻿—And so she went on questioning, and receiving no replies; while through all her thoughts passed and repassed the eyes of Wolkenlicht, which she had often felt to be upon her when she did not see them, wild with repressed longing, the light of their love shining through the veil of diffused tears, ever gathering and never overflowing. Then came the pale face, so worshipping, so distant in its self-withdrawn devotion, slowly dawning out of the vapours of her reverie. When it vanished, she tried to see it again. It would not come when she called it; but when her thoughts left knocking at the door of the lost, and wandered away, out came the pale, troubled, silent face again, gathering itself up from some unknown nook in her world of fantasy, and once more, when she tried to steady it by the fixedness of her own regard, fading back into the mist. So the phantasm of the dead drew near and wooed, as the living had never dared.﻿—What if there were any good in loving? What if men and women did not die all out, but some dim shade of each, like that pale, mind-ghost of Wolkenlicht, floated through the eternal vapours of chaos? And what if they might sometimes cross each other’s path, meet, know that they met, love on? Would not that revive the withered memory, fix the fleeting ghost, give a new habitation, a body even, to the poor, unhoused wanderers, frozen by the eternal frosts, no longer thinking beings, but thoughts wandering through the brain of the “Melancholy Mass?” Back with the thought came the face of the dead Karl, and the maiden threw herself on her bed in a flood of bitter tears. She could have loved him if he had only lived: she did love him, for he was dead. But even in the midst of the remorse that followed﻿—for had she not killed him?﻿—life seemed a less hard and hopeless thing than before. For it is love itself and not its responses or results that is the soul of life and its pleasures.

===Chinese===
她朝室内一扫，瞥见那具人体模型立在一个从所未见的位置上，又看了看卧榻，见那棺衣仍从底下鼓起来，便把眼睛睁大到虹膜四周一整圈眼白尽现的程度——这给托伊费尔斯比尔斯特提示了一种新的惊惶神情，尽管这神情出自一个他不曾指望、也不曾期待的方面；随后，她一言不发，便坐下来，去画她前一日正画着的那幅素描。可她父亲此刻朝她望去——正如沃尔肯利希特从前常做的那样——禁不住看出她苍白得吓人。她并未显露别的不安之色。他一放她走，她便退了出来，临过榻时又朝那榻、朝榻上那一团黑色里勾勒出的人影再瞥了一眼。她奔回自己的闺房，关上门、落了闩，在卧榻边坐下，却不曾哭，只是出神地想。他死了吗？这又有什么要紧？反正他们不久都要死的。她的母亲已经死了。这无非是因为大地若不再把已经生下来的那些吞噬，便再也生不出更多的儿女。可万一他们不得不换一种形态再回来，把又一段悲哀、无望、无爱的日子从头再过一遍呢？——她就这样问下去，得不到一句回答；而穿过她一切念头来来去去的，是沃尔肯利希特的那双眼睛——她常感觉到那双眼睛正在自己身上，哪怕她看不见；那双眼蓄着压抑的渴望而狂乱，爱的光透过一层泪水织成的薄纱透出来，那泪水永远在积聚、却从未溢出。接着是那张苍白的脸，那样崇敬，那样在其自甘疏远的虔诚中显得遥远，从她遐思的水汽里缓缓显现。待它消隐，她想要再看它一眼。她唤它，它却不肯来；可当她的念头不再去敲那失落之人的门、自顾自游荡开去，那张苍白、愁苦、无言的脸又出来了，从她幻想世界中的某一处隐秘角落里把自己聚拢起来；而当她想以自己凝望的定力来稳住它，它便再度消融回那片雾里去。于是这死者的幻影挨近前来、殷勤求爱，一如生前从未敢为的那样。——倘若去爱竟有什么益处呢？倘若男人和女人并不全然死绝，而每一个都留下某种朦胧的影，像沃尔肯利希特那苍白的心念之鬼一般，飘荡在永恒的混沌水汽之中呢？又倘若他们偶尔会彼此交错路径、相逢、知晓自己相逢了、爱下去呢？那岂不会使枯萎的记忆复苏、把转瞬即逝的鬼影定住、给那些可怜的、无家可归、被永恒之霜冻透了的、已不再是能思维的生灵、而只是在"忧愁之团"（Melancholy Mass）的脑中游荡的念头，重新安一个居所、甚至赋一个身体？伴随着这个念头，死去的卡尔的那张脸又回来了，少女便扑倒在床上，泪如雨下。倘若他还活着，她本可以爱他的：她的确爱他，因为他死了。可即便是在随之而来的悔恨之中——岂不是她害死了他的吗？——生活似乎也比从前少了几分艰涩与无望。因为生命的灵魂与生命之乐，乃是爱本身，而非爱的回应或结果。

===Original===
Two hours passed ere she could again show herself to her father, from whom she seemed in some new way divided by the new feeling in which he did not, and could not share. But at last, lest he should seek her, and finding her, should suspect her thoughts, she descended and sought him.﻿—For there is a maidenliness in sorrow, that wraps her garments close around her.﻿—But he was not to be seen; the door of the study was locked. A shudder passed through her as she thought of what her father, who lost no opportunity of furthering his all but perfect acquaintance with the human form and structure, might be about with the figure which she knew lay dead beneath that velvet pall, but which had arisen to haunt the hollow caves and cells of her living brain. She rushed away, and up once more to her silent room, through the darkness which had now settled down in the house; threw herself again on her bed, and lay almost paralysed with horror and distress.
But Teufelsbürst was not about anything so frightful as she supposed, though something frightful enough. I have already implied that Wolkenlicht was, in form, as fine an embodiment of youthful manhood as any old Greek republic could have provided one of its sculptors with as model for an Apollo. It is true, that to the eye of a Greek artist he would not have been more acceptable in consequence of the regimen he had been going through for the last few weeks; but the emaciation of Wolkenlicht’s frame, and the consequent prominence of the muscles, indicating the pain he had gone through, were peculiarly attractive to Teufelsbürst.﻿—He was busy preparing to take a cast of the body of his dead pupil, that it might aid to the perfection of his future labours.

===Chinese===
两个钟头过去了，她才能再度在父亲面前露面——他既不能、也不会与她共享这份新感觉，这感觉仿佛以某种新的方式，把她与父亲隔开。可末了，她生怕他来找她、找到了便疑心她的心事，便下楼去寻他。——因为忧愁之中自有一种少女的端庄，会把衣裳紧紧裹住自己。——可他不在；画室的门上了锁。一阵战栗传遍她全身，她想到父亲从不放过任何一个增进他那几近圆满的人体形态与构造知识的机会，他会拿那具她明知躺在丝绒棺衣下、却又在她的活脑的那些空洞穴室里起身作祟的尸首，做出什么事来。她连忙奔走，再奔回自己那间寂寂的房间，穿过此时已笼罩了整座宅子的黑暗；又扑到床上，几乎被恐怖与悲愁惊骇得瘫软。
可托伊费尔斯比尔斯特手上所为，并不像她所设想的那样可怖——虽然也是够可怖的了。我已暗示过，沃尔肯利希特的体魄，是青春男子气概的精美化身，足以与任何一个古希腊城邦提供给其雕塑家作阿波罗（Apollo）模特的范本相媲美。诚然，在一个希腊艺术家眼里，由于他这几周所受的种种熬炼，他未必更合心意；可沃尔肯利希特身躯的消瘦、及由此带来的肌肉凸显，所昭示的正是他所经受的苦痛，这对托伊费尔斯比尔斯特而言格外地诱人。——他正忙着准备给这死去的学生翻一个石膏模，好叫它有助于自己将来的作品臻于完美。

===Original===
He was deep in the artistic enjoyment of a form, at the same time so beautiful and strong, yet with the lines of suffering in every limb and feature, when his daughter’s hand was laid on the latch. He started, flung the velvet drapery over the body, and went to the door. But Lilith had vanished. He returned to his labours. The operation took a long time, for he performed it very carefully. Towards midnight, he had finished encasing the body in a close-clinging shell of plaster, which, when broken off, and fitted together, would be the matrix to the form of the dead Wolkenlicht. Before leaving it to harden till the morning, he was just proceeding to strengthen it with an additional layer all over, when a flash of lightning, reflected in all its dazzle from the snow without, almost blinded him. A peal of long-drawn thunder followed; the wind rose; and just such a storm came on as had risen some time before at the death of Kuntz, whose spectre was still tormenting the city. The gnomes of terror, deep hidden in the caverns of Teufelsbürst’s nature, broke out jubilant. With trembling hands he tried to cast the pall over the awful white chrysalis﻿—failed, and fled to his chamber. And there lay the studio naked to the eyes of the lightning, with its tortured forms throbbing out of the dark, and quivering, as with life, in the almost continuous palpitations of the light; while on the couch lay the motionless mass of whiteness, gleaming blue in the lightning, almost more terrible in its crude indications of the human form, than that which it enclosed. It lay there as if dropped from some tree of chaos, haggard with the snows of eternity﻿—a huge misshapen nut, with a corpse for its kernel.

===Chinese===
他正深深沉浸在一份艺术享受之中，这份享受所对的是那样一具形体——既美且强，四肢五官又处处刻着苦痛的线条——就在这时，女儿的手搭上了门闩。他一惊，把丝绒的帷布往那尸身上一甩，便去开门。可莉莉丝已不见了。他回到他的活计上来。这道工序颇费时辰，因为他做得极其仔细。将近半夜，他已把这尸身封进一层紧贴的石膏硬壳里，待它敲下来、再拼合起来，便是那死去的沃尔肯利希特之形的模具。在他将其搁置到次晨硬化之前，正要给整个再覆上一层来加固，一道闪电从外面的积雪上一反射回来，炫目得几乎叫他睁不开眼。紧接着是一阵拖长的雷鸣；风起了；这样一场风暴骤然而至，与不久前孔茨死时那场一模一样，而孔茨的幽灵还在折磨着全城。那些蛰伏在托伊费尔斯比尔斯特天性深处洞穴里的恐惧地精，欢腾地奔窜而出。他颤着手，想把那棺衣罩到那可怖的白色虫蛹之上——却没罩成，便逃回自己房中去了。那画室就这样赤裸裸地袒露在闪电眼下，那些受难的形体从黑暗里鼓胀出来，在那几乎连绵不断的明灭之中，像有生命似地颤动；而卧榻上躺着那一团纹丝不动的白，在闪电中泛着幽蓝，它对人体的粗粝勾画，几乎比它所包裹的那个人体本身还要可怖。它躺在那里，仿佛从混沌之树的某根枝上掉落下来，带着永恒之雪的憔悴——一颗硕大而畸形的坚果，以一具尸首为它的仁。

===Original===
But the lightning would soon have revealed a more terrible sight still, had there been any eyes to behold it. At midnight, while a peal of thunder was just dying away in the distance, the crust of death flew asunder, rending in all directions; and, pale as his investiture, staring with ghastly eyes, the form of Karl started up sitting on the couch. Had he not been far beyond ordinary men in strength, he could not thus have rent his sepulchre. Indeed, had Teufelsbürst been able to finish his task by the additional layer of gypsum which he contemplated, he must have died the moment life revived; although, so long as the trance lasted, neither the exclusion from the air, nor the practical solidification of the walls of his chest, could do him any injury. He had lain unconscious throughout the operations of Teufelsbürst, but now the catalepsy had passed away, possibly under the influence of the electric condition of the atmosphere. Very likely the strength he now put forth was intensified by a convulsive reaction of all the powers of life, as is not infrequently the case in sudden awakenings from similar interruptions of vital activity. The coming to himself and the bursting of his case were simultaneous. He sat staring about him, with, of all his mental faculties, only his imagination awake, from which the thoughts that occupied it when he fell senseless had not yet faded. These thoughts had been compounded of feelings about Lilith, and speculations about the vampire that haunted the neighbourhood; and the fumes of the last drug of which he had partaken, still hovering in his brain, combined with these thoughts and fancies to generate the delusion that he had just broken from the embrace of his coffin, and risen, the last-born of the vampire race. The sense of unavoidable obligation to fulfil his doom, was yet mingled with a faint flutter of joy, for he knew that he must go to Lilith. With a deep sigh, he rose, gathered up the pall of black velvet, flung it around him, stepped from the couch, and left the study to find her.

===Chinese===
然而那闪电若有人眼去看，很快便会显出一幅更可怖的景象。半夜时分，正当一阵雷声在远处消歇之际，那层死亡的外壳四分五裂，向四面八方炸开；卡尔的身形猛地从卧榻上坐起，与他那身殓衣一样苍白，瞪着一双死人般的眼睛。若非他远逾常人的强健，他绝不能这样挣开自己的坟茔。事实上，倘若托伊费尔斯比尔斯特能照他所想再加那层石膏把活做完，那么生命一醒来的当口，他就必死无疑了；尽管在昏厥持续之时，无论是与空气隔绝，还是胸腔四壁几乎被挤成实块，都伤不了他。在托伊费尔斯比尔斯特动手的全过程里，他一直人事不省；如今这僵直之症却已退去，多半是受了大气中电的影响。他所使出的这一把力气，很可能因生命的全部力量的痉挛性反激而格外猛烈——从类似这种生命活动的中断之中骤然惊醒，往往便是如此。他的回过神来与他的挣破外壳是同时发生的。他坐在那里，四下瞪望，他那一切心智能力之中，醒着的只剩下想象力，而方才他昏死过去时所盘踞其上的念头，尚未消退。这些念头，原是由他对莉莉丝的种种情思，和对出没于左近的吸血鬼的种种揣测揉合而成；他所服下的最后一剂药的烟气，仍在他脑中游荡，与这些念头和遐想交相作用，便生出一种错觉——他以为自己刚刚挣脱棺材的怀抱，作为吸血鬼一族最新生的那一个，从坟中起来了。一种无可回避、须得去履其命数的使命之感，又掺着一丝隐隐的欢喜悸动，因为他知道自己必须到莉莉丝那里去。他长叹一声，站起身来，抓起那块黑丝绒的棺衣，往身上一披，跨下卧榻，便出了画室，去寻她。

===Original===
Meantime, Teufelsbürst had sufficiently recovered to remember that he had left the door of the studio unfastened, and that anyone entering would discover in what he had been engaged, which, in the case of his getting into any difficulty about the death of Karl, would tell powerfully against him. He was at the farther end of a long passage, leading from the house to the studio, on his way to make all secure, when Karl appeared at the door, and advanced towards him. The painter, seized with invincible terror, turned and fled. He reached his room, and fell senseless on the floor. The phantom held on its way, heedless.

===Chinese===
与此同时，托伊费尔斯比尔斯特已恢复到足以记起：自己走时没把画室的门扣上，任何进来的人都会发现他在做什么——而万一他在卡尔之死一事上惹上什么麻烦，这便会大大地对他不利。他正在一条从正屋通往画室的长过道的尽头，正要去把一切收拾停当，这时卡尔出现在门口，朝他走来。画师被一股无法抵御的恐惧攫住，转身便逃。他奔到自己房里，不省人事地栽倒在地板上。那幻影却不顾一切，继续走它的路。

===Original===
Lilith, on gaining her room the second time, had thrown herself on her bed as before, and had wept herself into a troubled slumber. She lay dreaming﻿—and dreadful dreams. Suddenly she awoke in one of those peals of thunder which tormented the high regions of the air, as a storm billows the surface of the ocean. She lay awake and listened. As it died away, she thought she heard, mingling with its last muffled murmurs, the sound of moaning. She turned her face towards the room in keen terror. But she saw nothing. Another light, long-drawn sigh reached her ear, and at the same moment a flash of lightning illumined the room. In the corner farthest from her bed, she spied a white face, nothing more. She was dumb and motionless with fear. Utter darkness followed, a darkness that seemed to enter into her very brain. Yet she felt that the face was slowly crossing the black gulf of the room, and drawing near to where she lay. The next flash revealed, as it bended over her, the ghastly face of Karl, down which flowed fresh tears. The rest of his form was lost in blackness. Lilith did not faint, but it was the very force of her fear that seemed to keep her alive. It became for the moment the atmosphere of her life. She lay trembling and staring at the spot in the darkness where she supposed the face of Karl still to be. But the next flash showed her the face far off, looking at her through the panes of her lattice-window.
For Lottchen, as soon as he saw Lilith, seemed to himself to go through a second stage of awaking. Her face made him doubt whether he could be a vampire after all; for instead of wanting to bite her arm and suck the blood, he all but fell down at her feet in a passion of speechless love. The next moment he became aware that his presence must be at least very undesirable to her; and in an instant he had reached her window, which he knew looked upon a lower roof that extended between two different parts of the house, and before the next flash came, he had stepped through the lattice and closed it behind him.

===Chinese===
莉莉丝这第二回进自己房间，又像先前那样扑到床上，哭得昏昏睡去。她做着梦——尽是些可怕的梦。忽然，她在其中一阵雷声里惊醒——那雷声折磨着高空，正如风暴搅起海洋的表层。她醒着躺在那里，侧耳细听。雷声消歇之际，她以为自己听见，混在它最后那阵沉闷的余响里的，是一声呻吟。她在极度的惊恐之中，把脸转向房里。可她什么也没看见。又一声悠长轻细的叹息传到她耳中，与此同时，一道闪电照亮了全室。在离她的床最远的那个角落里，她窥见一张苍白的脸，再没有别的。她吓得不能出声、不能动弹。接着是一片漆黑，那黑暗仿佛直钻进了她的脑髓。可她却感觉到那张脸正缓缓穿过房间那一片黑色的深渊，朝她躺着的地方挨近。下一道闪电照出，正俯身在她上方的，是卡尔那张死人般的脸，脸上正淌下新鲜的泪。他的其余身形都隐没在黑暗之中。莉莉丝没有晕过去，可仿佛正是她那恐惧的力量把她撑着活了下去。那一时之间，那恐惧成了她生命的空气。她躺着，战栗着，直瞪着黑暗中她以为卡尔的脸仍在那儿的那一处。可下一道闪电却给她看见那张脸已在远处，隔着窗扉的玻璃朝她望着。
因为洛特臣一看见莉莉丝，便觉得自己仿佛经过了第二重的苏醒。她的脸使他怀疑自己究竟是不是吸血鬼；因为他非但不想咬她的手臂、吸她的血，反而几乎要在一种无言的爱之情热里，跌倒在她脚下。下一刻他便意识到，自己这副模样至少是她极不愿见的；转瞬之间他已到了她窗前——他知道那窗子正对着一片较低的屋顶，那屋顶横跨在宅子两个不同的部分之间——在下一道闪电落下之前，他已跨出窗扉、把它在身后合上。

===Original===
Believing his own room to be attainable from this quarter, he proceeded along the roof in the direction he judged best. The cold winter air by degrees restored him entirely to his right mind, and he soon comprehended the whole of the circumstances in which he found himself. Peeping through a window he was passing, to see whether it belonged to his room, he spied Teufelsbürst, who, at the very moment, was lifting his head from the faint into which he had fallen at the first sight of Lottchen. The moon was shining clear, and in its light the painter saw, to his horror, the pale face staring in at his window. He thought it had been there ever since he had fainted, and dropped again in a deeper swoon than before. Karl saw him fall, and the truth flashed upon him that the wicked artist took him for what he had believed himself to be when first he recovered from his trance﻿—namely, the vampire of the former Karl Wolkenlicht. The moment he comprehended it, he resolved to keep up the delusion if possible. Meantime he was innocently preparing a new ingredient for the popular dish of horrors to be served at the ordinary of the city the next day. For the old servant’s were not the only eyes that had seen him besides those of Teufelsbürst. What could be more like a vampire, dragging his pall after him, than this apparition of poor, half-frozen Lottchen, crawling across the roof? Karl remembered afterwards that he had heard the dogs howling awfully in every direction, as he crept along; but this was hardly necessary to make those who saw him conclude that it was the same phantasm of John Kuntz, which had been infesting the whole city, and especially the house next door to the painter’s, which had been the dwelling of the respectable alderman who had degenerated into this most disreputable of moneyless vagabonds. What added to the consternation of all who heard of it, was the sickening conviction that the extreme measures which they had resorted to in order to free the city from the ghoul, beyond which nothing could be done, had been utterly unavailing, successful as they had proved in every other known case of the kind. For, urged as well by various horrid signs about his grave, which not even its close proximity to the altar could render a place of repose, they had opened it, had found in the body every peculiarity belonging to a vampire, had pulled it out with the greatest difficulty on account of a quite supernatural ponderosity; which rendered the horse which had killed him﻿—a strong animal﻿—all but unable to drag it along, and had at last, after cutting it in pieces, and expending on the fire two hundred and sixteen great billets, succeeded in conquering its incombustibleness, and reducing it to ashes. Such, at least, was the story which had reached the painter’s household, and was believed by many; and if all this did not compel the perturbed corpse to rest, what more could be done?

===Chinese===
他以为从这一边可以回到自己的房间，便沿着屋顶朝他所判断的方向走去。冬夜的寒气渐渐地使他完全恢复了正常的心神，不久他便全然明白了自己所处的境况。他打一扇经过的窗前望进去，看那是否是自己房间的窗，却窥见托伊费尔斯比尔斯特——此人此刻正从一见着洛特臣便昏厥过去的那场晕厥里抬起头来。月色清亮，画师在月光里骇然看见那张苍白的脸正从窗外瞪着自己。他以为自从自己昏倒以来那张脸就一直待在那儿，便又坠入一场比先前更深的昏迷。卡尔看见他倒下，真相顿时闪现心头——这邪恶的艺术家把他当成了他自己头一次从昏厥里醒过来时所自以为的那一种东西——即：昔日那位卡尔·沃尔肯利希特的吸血鬼。他一明白过来，便决意尽可能把这错觉维持下去。与此同时，他也在无意之间为次日城中饭桌上将要端出的那道众人共享的恐怖大菜，备下了一味新的佐料。因为除了托伊费尔斯比尔斯特之外，看见他的并非只有那老仆人一双眼睛。可怜的、冻得半僵的洛特臣拖着他那袭棺衣爬过屋顶——这一副模样，还有什么能比它更像一个吸血鬼呢？卡尔后来记起，他一路爬过去时，听见四面的狗都可怕地嚎起来；可即便没有这一层，看见他的人也大可断定那便是约翰·孔茨的同一个幻影——那幻影一直侵扰着全城，尤其是画师隔壁那座宅子——那位体面的参事生前便住在那里，死后却堕化成了这样一个最不体面、身无分文的游魂。令一切闻者愈加惊惶的，是这样一种令人作呕的认定：他们为使全城摆脱这食尸鬼而采取的那些极端手段——已是无所不用其极、再无可做之事——竟全然无效，尽管同样的手段在已知的每一桩同类案子中都奏了效。因为，受了他坟墓周围的种种骇人征兆所催逼——那些征兆使那坟即便紧挨着祭坛也无法成为安息之所——他们掘开了它，在那尸身上发现了吸血鬼应有的一切特征，又因它一种超乎自然的沉重，费尽九牛二虎之力才把它拖出来；那沉重使得踢死他的那匹马——一匹强壮的牲口——几乎拖不动它；末了他们把它砍成碎块、在火上费了二百一十六根大柴，才征服了它的不可燃性，把它烧成灰烬。至少，传到画师家中、为许多人所信的故事是这般讲的；倘若这一切还不足以迫使这不得安息的尸首就范，那还能再做什么呢？

===Original===
When Karl had reached his room, and was dressing himself, the thought struck him that something might be made of the report of the extreme weight of the body of old Kuntz, to favour the continuance of the delusion of Teufelsbürst, although he hardly knew yet to what use he could turn this delusion. He was convinced that he would have made no progress however long he might have remained in his house; and that he would have more chance of favour with Lilith if he were to meet her in any other circumstances whatever than those in which he invariably saw her﻿—namely, surrounded by her father’s influences, and watched by her father’s cold blue eyes.

===Chinese===
卡尔回到自己房间，正在穿衣，忽然想到：老孔茨尸身极度沉重的那一桩传闻，或可拿来加以利用，好叫托伊费尔斯比尔斯特的错觉延续下去——尽管他还不很知道能把这种错觉派什么用场。他深信，自己无论在那宅子里再住多久，都不会有什么进展；而且他若能在任何别的场合下与莉莉丝相遇——而不是总在他素来见她的那种场合，即：被她父亲的影响所环绕、被她父亲那双冷蓝眼睛所监视——便更有几分赢得她的可能。

===Original===
As soon as he was dressed, he crept down to the studio, which was now quiet enough, the storm being over, and the moon filling it with her steady shine. In the corner lay in all directions the fragments of the mould which his own body had formed and filled. The bag of plaster and the bucket of water which the painter had been using stood beside. Lottchen gathered all the pieces together, and then making his way to an outhouse where he had seen various odds and ends of rubbish lying, chose from the heap as many pieces of old iron and other metal as he could find. To these he added a few large stones from the garden. When he had got all into the studio, he locked the door, and proceeded to fit together the parts of the mould, filling up the hollow as he went on with the heaviest things he could get into it, and solidifying the whole by pouring in plaster; till, having at length completed it, and obliterated, as much as possible, the marks of joining, he left it to harden, with the conviction that now it would make a considerable impression on Teufelsbürst’s imagination, as well as on his muscular sense. He then left everything else as nearly undisturbed as he could; and, knowing all the ways of the house, was soon in the street, without leaving any signs of his exit.
Karl soon found himself before the house in which his friend Höllenrachen resided. Knowing his studious habits, he had hoped to see his light still burning, nor was he disappointed. He contrived to bring him to his window, and a moment after, the door was cautiously opened.

===Chinese===
他一穿好衣裳，便悄悄下楼来到画室——此时画室已够寂静，风暴既过，月光把屋子里灌满了它安定的清辉。一个角落里，横七竖八地躺着那副模具的碎片——那模具原是由他自己的身体成形、填满的。画师用过的那袋石膏、那桶水还摆在旁边。洛特臣把所有碎片都归拢到一处，然后摸到一间杂物棚里——他曾见那里堆着种种零星杂物——从那堆里尽可能多地挑出些旧铁件和其他金属件。他又从花园里捡了几块大石头加进去。他把这一切都搬进画室之后，便锁上门，开始把模具的各片拼合起来，一边拼、一边往当中空腔里塞他能弄到的最重的东西，再灌进石膏使之整体凝结；到末了终于完工，又把接缝的痕迹尽可能地抹去，便把它搁在那里让它硬化——他深信，这东西如今定会在托伊费尔斯比尔斯特的想象上，也会在他的筋肉之觉上，留下相当可观的印象。随后他又把其余一切都尽可能恢复到不曾动过的样子；既熟悉这宅子的一切通路，不久他便到了街上，没留下任何出走的痕迹。
卡尔很快便到了他的朋友赫伦拉肯所住的宅子前。知道他勤学的习惯，他原盼望看见他的灯还亮着，果然没有失望。他设法把他引到窗前，片刻之后，门便被小心地打开了。

===Original===
“Why, Lottchen, where do you come from?”
“From the grave, Heinrich, or next door to it.”
“Come in, and tell me all about it. We thought the old painter had made a model of you, and tortured you to death.”
“Perhaps you were not far wrong. But get me a horn of ale, for even a vampire is thirsty, you know.”
“A vampire!” exclaimed Heinrich, retreating a pace, and involuntarily putting himself upon his guard.
Karl laughed.
“My hand was warm, was it not, old fellow?” he said. “Vampires are cold, all but the blood.”
“What a fool I am!” rejoined Heinrich. “But you know we have been hearing such horrors lately that a fellow may be excused for shuddering a little when a pale-faced apparition tells him at two o’clock in the morning that he is a vampire, and thirsty, too.”

===Chinese===
"咦，洛特臣，你从哪儿来？"
"从坟里来，海因里希，或者说是从坟隔壁。"
"进来，把一切都告诉我。我们原以为那老画师把你做成了模型，把你折磨死了。"
"也许你们猜得不算太离谱。可先给我一角杯麦酒喝吧，要知道吸血鬼也是会口渴的。"
"吸血鬼！"海因里希惊呼，退后一步，不由自主地摆出了戒备的架势。
卡尔笑起来。
"我的手是暖的，是不是，老兄？"他说，"吸血鬼是冷的，除了血以外。"
"我真是个傻瓜！"海因里希应道，"可你知道，近来我们所听的那些可怖的事实在太多，凌晨两点钟一个面色苍白的幽灵跑来告诉你他是吸血鬼、而且还口渴——一个人稍微打个寒噤，总该可以被原谅吧。"

===Original===
Karl told him the whole story; and the mental process of regarding it for the sake of telling it, revealed to him pretty clearly some of the treatment of which he had been unconscious at the time. Heinrich was quite sure that his suspicions were correct. And now the question was, what was to be done next?
“At all events,” said Heinrich, “we must keep you out of the way for some time. I will represent to my landlady that you are in hiding from enemies, and her heart will rule her tongue. She can let you have a garret-room, I know; and I will do as well as I can to bear you company. We shall have time then to invent some plan of operation.”
To this proposal Karl agreed with hearty thanks, and soon all was arranged. The only conclusion they could yet arrive at was, that somehow or other the old demon-painter must be tamed.

===Chinese===
卡尔把事情原原本本告诉了他；而为着把它讲出来而在脑中重新审视一遍的那个过程，也颇清楚地向他揭示了若干他当时并不自知的对待。海因里希则确信自己的怀疑没有错。眼下的问题是：接下来该怎么办？
"无论如何，"海因里希说，"我们得让你先躲起来一阵子。我会对女房东说你有仇家在追你，她的心肠自会管住她的舌头。我知道她可以给你一间阁楼；我也会尽我所能来陪你。那时我们便有时间想出一个法子来。"
卡尔满心感激地答应了这番安排，不久一切都定下来了。他们此刻所能得到的唯一结论是：无论如何，那老魔画师总得被驯服。

===Original===
Meantime, how fared it with Lilith? She too had no doubt that she had seen the body-ghost of poor Karl, and that the vampire had, according to rule, paid her the first visit because he loved her best. This was horrible enough if the vampire were not really the person he represented; but if in any sense it were Karl himself, at least it gave some expectation of a more prolonged existence than her father had taught her to look for; and if love anything like her mother’s still lasted, even along with the habits of a vampire, there was something to hope for in the future. And then, though he had visited her, he had not, as far as she was aware, deprived her of a drop of blood. She could not be certain that he had not bitten her, for she had been in such a strange condition of mind that she might not have felt it, but she believed that he had restrained the impulses of his vampire nature, and had left her, lest he should yet yield to them. She fell fast asleep; and, when morning came, there was not, as far as she could judge, one of those triangular leech-like perforations to be found upon her whole body. Will it be believed that the moment she was satisfied of this, she was seized by a terrible jealousy, lest Karl should have gone and bitten someone else? Most people will wonder that she should not have gone out of her senses at once; but there was all the difference between a visit from a real vampire and a visit from a man she had begun to love, even although she took him for a vampire. All the difference does *not* lie in a name. They were very different causes, and the effects must be very different.

===Chinese===
与此同时，莉莉丝那边又怎样了？她也毫不怀疑自己见到的是可怜的卡尔的尸鬼，并以为那吸血鬼照规矩头一个就探访了她，因为他最爱她。这已经够可怕了——假如那吸血鬼并非他所代表的那个本人；可倘若在某种意义上那真是卡尔本人，那至少也给了她一线指望，叫她有望得着一种比她父亲所教她去期盼的更为绵长的存在；而倘若还有一丝像她母亲那样的爱能够存留，哪怕与吸血鬼的诸般习气并存，未来便还有点什么可盼。况且，他虽探访了她，就她所知，并未夺去她一滴血。她不能肯定他未曾咬过她，因为她方才的心境是那样古怪，也许咬了她也未必感觉得到；可她相信他遏制住了自己吸血鬼天性里的冲动，离开了她，惟恐自己终会屈服于那冲动。她沉沉睡去；待到清晨来临，就她所能判断的，她全身上下找不到一个那种三角的、水蛭般的齿孔。说出来谁肯信——她一确信了这一点，竟立刻被一种可怕的嫉妒攫住，生怕卡尔已经跑去咬了别的人？大多数人都会纳罕，她怎么没有当下就发了疯；可一个真正的吸血鬼登门，与一个她已开始爱上了的人登门——哪怕她把他当成吸血鬼——这二者之间，竟有着天壤之别。一切的差别并不都在一个名字上。原因既如此不同，结果自然也要大不相同。

===Original===
When Teufelsbürst came down in the morning, he crept into the studio like a murderer. There lay the awful white block, seeming to his eyes just the same as he had left it. What was to be done with it? He dared not open it. Mould and model must go together. But whither? If inquiry should be made after Wolkenlicht, and this were discovered anywhere on his premises, would it not be enough to bring him at once to the gallows? Therefore it would be dangerous to bury it in the garden, or in the cellar.
“Besides,” thought he, with a shudder, “that would be to fix the vampire as a guest forever.”﻿—And the horrors of the past night rushed back upon his imagination with renewed intensity. What would it be to have the dead Karl crawling about his house forever, now inside, now out, now sitting on the stairs, now staring in at the windows?
He would have dragged it to the bottom of his garden, past which the Moldau flowed, and plunged it into the stream; but then, should the spectre continue to prove troublesome, it would be almost impossible to reach the body so as to destroy it by fire; besides which, he could not do it without assistance, and the probability of discovery. If, however, the apparition should turn out to be no vampire, but only a respectable ghost, they might manage to endure its presence, till it should be weary of haunting them.

===Chinese===
托伊费尔斯比尔斯特早晨下楼时，像个杀人凶手似地溜进了画室。那可怖的白色大块仍躺在那里，在他眼中和他走时一般无二。该拿它怎么办？他不敢打开它。模具与模特须得一同处置。可往哪儿送？倘若有人追查沃尔肯利希特的下落，而这东西在他屋里任何一处被搜出来，岂不就足以叫他立刻上绞架？因此，把它埋在花园里、或地窖里，都是危险的。
"况且，"他战栗着想，"那样岂不是把这吸血鬼永远地留作座上客了。"——昨夜种种恐怖，又以一种更新的烈度扑回他的想象。要叫死去的卡尔永远在他这宅子里爬来爬去，一时在内、一时在外，一时坐在楼梯上，一时在窗外瞪眼探望——那将是怎样一番光景？
他本想把它拖到花园尽头——那儿流过莫尔道河（Moldau）——把它沉到河里；可那样一来，倘若那幽灵继续作祟，便几乎无法够得着尸身去用火烧；何况他一个人也干不成，非人帮忙不可，便又有被发觉的危险。但倘若那幻影终归并非吸血鬼，只不过是个尚算体面的鬼魂，那他们或许还勉强受得了它的存在，直到它自己厌了作祟他们为止。

===Original===
He resolved at last to convey the body for the meantime into a concealed cellar in the house, seeing something must be done before his daughter came down. Proceeding to remove it, his consternation as greatly increased when he discovered how the body had grown in weight since he had thus disposed of it, leaving on his mind scarcely a hope that it could turn out not to be a vampire after all. He could scarcely stir it, and there was but one whom he could call to his assistance﻿—the old woman who acted as his housekeeper and servant.
He went to her room, roused her, and told her the whole story. Devoted to her master for many years, and not quite so sensitive to fearful influences as when less experienced in horrors, she showed immediate readiness to render him assistance. Utterly unable, however, to lift the mass between them, they could only drag and push it along; and such a slow toil was it that there was no time to remove the traces of its track, before Lilith came down and saw a broad white line leading from the door of the studio down the cellar stairs. She knew in a moment what it meant; but not a word was uttered about the matter, and the name of Karl Wolkenlicht seemed to be entirely forgotten.

===Chinese===
他终于打定主意，暂且先把尸首弄进宅里一间隐蔽的地窖去——因为总得在女儿下楼之前做点什么。他着手搬动它时，他的惊恐又大大加重了：他发觉自他这般处置以来，那尸身竟在重量上长了那么多——这几乎使他不再存一丝希望，指望它到头来不是吸血鬼了。他几乎挪它不动；他能叫来帮忙的，只有一个人——就是那个给他做管家兼仆人的老妇人。
他到她房里，把她唤醒，把事情原原本本告诉了她。她多年来对主人忠心耿耿，又因见惯了可怖之事、不像当初那般对惊吓之事那般敏感，当即一口应承帮忙。然而两人合力也抬它不动，只能又拖又推地往前挪；这一番活计做得那样慢，以致他们根本来不及抹去它拖过的痕迹，莉莉丝便已下楼，看见一道宽阔的白印从画室门口一直引到地窖楼梯下。她立刻就明白这是怎么回事；但对此事谁也没有说一句话，卡尔·沃尔肯利希特这个名字，仿佛已全然被遗忘了。

===Original===
But how could the affairs of a house go on all the same when everyone of the household knew that a dead body lay in the cellar?﻿—nay more, that, although it lay still and dead enough all day, it would come half alive at nightfall, and, turning the whole house into a sepulchre by its presence, go creeping about like a cat all over it in the dark﻿—perhaps with phosphorescent eyes? So it was not surprising that the painter abandoned his studio early, and that the three found themselves together in the gorgeous room formerly described, as soon as twilight began to fall.
Already Teufelsbürst had begun to experience a kind of shrinking from the horrid faces in his own pictures, and to feel disgusted at the abortions of his own mind. But all that he and the old woman now felt was an increasing fear as the night drew on, a kind of sickening and paralysing terror. The thing down there would not lie quiet﻿—at least its phantom in the cellars of their imagination would not. As much as possible, however, they avoided alarming Lilith, who, knowing all they knew, was as silent as they. But her mind was in a strange state of excitement, partly from the presence of a new sense of love, the pleasure of which all the atmosphere of grief into which it grew could not totally quench. It comforted her somehow, as a child may comfort when his father is away.

===Chinese===
可一家人谁都知道地窖里躺着一具死尸——一家的日子又怎还能照常过下去？——更何况，它虽白天里一动不动地死着，一到黄昏便会半活过来，以其存在把整座宅子化作一座坟墓，在黑暗中像只猫一样在屋里各处爬来爬去——说不定还瞪着一双磷光闪闪的眼睛？所以也就无怪画师早早便抛下了他的画室，无怪暮色初降之时，三个人便聚到了先前所形容的那间华美的屋子里。
托伊费尔斯比尔斯特已开始对自己画上那些可怖的面孔生出一种退缩之感，也开始对自己头脑里那些怪胎感到厌憎。可他与那老妇人此刻所感到的一切，只是随着夜色加深而愈演愈烈的一种恐惧，一种令人作呕、令人瘫软的惊惶。那下面的东西是不肯安安静静躺着的——至少它在他俩想象的地窖里的那个幻影不肯。然而他们尽可能不去惊动莉莉丝——莉莉丝所知的与他们一样多，却和他们一样沉默。可她的心却处于一种奇异的亢奋之中，部分是因了一种新的爱之感的降临，那份爱的欢愉，是它从中生长起来的那一重重哀愁的氛围所不能完全扑灭的。它竟以某种方式给了她几分慰藉，正如父亲不在身边时，一个孩子也能给人带来慰藉那样。

===Original===
Bedtime came, and no one made a move to go. Without a word spoken on the subject, the three remained together all night; the elders nodding and slumbering occasionally, and Lilith getting some share of repose on a couch. All night the shape of death might be somewhere about the house; but it did not disturb them. They heard no sound, saw no sight; and when the morning dawned, they separated, chilled and stupid, and for the time beyond fear, to seek repose in their private chambers. There they remained equally undisturbed.
But when the painter approached his easel a few hours after, looking more pale and haggard still than he was wont, from the fears of the night, a new bewilderment took possession of him. He had been busy with a fresh embodiment of his favourite subject, into which he had sketched the form of the student as the sufferer. He had represented poor Wolkenlicht as just beginning to recover from a trance, while a group of surgeons, unaware of the signs of returning life, were absorbed in a minute dissection of one of the limbs. At an open door he had painted Lilith passing, with her face buried in a bunch of sweet peas. But when he came to the picture, he found, to his astonishment and terror, that the face of one of the group was now turned towards that of the victim, regarding his revival with demoniac satisfaction, and taking pains to prevent the others from discovering it. The face of this prince of torturers was that of Teufelsbürst himself. Lilith had altogether vanished, and in her place stood the dim vampire reiteration of the body that lay extended on the table, staring greedily at the assembled company. With trembling hands the painter removed the picture from the easel, and turned its face to the wall.

===Chinese===
就寝的时辰到了，却没有一个人起身要走。三人不约而同、不置一词地整夜守在了一处；两个上了年纪的人偶尔打着盹、打个瞌睡，莉莉丝则在卧榻上将息了片刻。整整一夜，那死亡的形影或许就在宅中某处；可它没有来搅扰他们。他们未闻一声、未见一影；待到天明，三人便各自散去，又冷又木，一时之间反倒没了恐惧，各自回房去寻安息。他们在房里也同样未受搅扰。
可几个钟头之后，当画师——因了一夜的惊恐、比平日更显苍白憔悴——走到他的画架跟前时，一种新的迷惘攫住了他。他一直在忙着一幅其所钟爱主题的新作，在其中勾上了那个学生的身影作受苦者。他把可怜的沃尔肯利希特画成刚刚开始从昏厥中苏醒，而一群外科医生并未觉察生命回转的征兆，正全神贯注地解剖着他的一条腿。在一扇敞开的门边，他画了莉莉丝正掩面于一束香豌豆花走过。可他走到画前一看，又惊又惧地发现：那群人中有一位的脸，如今转向了受难者的脸，以一种恶魔般的满足注视着他的苏醒，并竭力阻止旁人发觉。这位施刑之王的脸，正是托伊费尔斯比尔斯特自己。莉莉丝已无影无踪，取而代之立在那里的，是那个摊在台上的躯体的一个影影绰绰的吸血鬼重影，正贪婪地瞪着在场的众人。画师颤着手，把那幅画从画架上取下来，把画面朝墙翻了过去。

===Original===
Of course this was the work of Lottchen. When he left the house, he took with him the key of a small private door, which was so seldom used that, while it remained closed, the key would not be missed, perhaps for many months. Watching the windows, he had chosen a safe time to enter, and had been hard at work all night on these alterations. Teufelsbürst attributed them to the vampire, and left the picture as he found it, not daring to put brush to it again.
The next night was passed much after the same fashion. But the fear had begun to die away a little in the hearts of the women, who did not know what had taken place in the studio on the previous night. It burrowed, however, with gathered force in the vitals of Teufelsbürst. But this night likewise passed in peace; and before it was over, the old woman had taken to speculating in her own mind as to the best way of disposing of the body, seeing it was not at all likely to be troublesome. But when the painter entered his studio in trepidation the next morning, he found that the form of the lovely Lilith was painted out of every picture in the room. This could not be concealed; and Lilith and the servant became aware that the studio was the portion of the house in haunting which the vampire left the rest in peace.

===Chinese===
这当然是洛特臣的手笔。他离开宅子时，带走了一扇私用小门的钥匙——那门极少使用，只要它一直关着，那把钥匙也许好几个月都不会被发觉丢了。他守着窗子，挑了个安全的时辰潜入，彻夜不眠地做了这些改动。托伊费尔斯比尔斯特把它们都归到了吸血鬼头上，便任那幅画保持原样，再不敢动笔。
第二夜的度过方式也与头一夜大致相同。可是在那两个女人心里——她们并不知道前一夜画室里所发生的事——恐惧已开始稍稍消退。然而那恐惧却在托伊费尔斯比尔斯特的心腹深处，以更加凝聚的力量蛰伏下去。可这一夜同样平安度过；天还没亮，那老妇人便已在心里盘算起处置那具尸首的最佳办法来了——既然它看来一点也不打算作祟。可次日清晨，当画师战战兢兢走进画室时，却发觉可爱的莉莉丝的身影，已从屋里每一幅画中被涂了出去。这事瞒不住人；莉莉丝和那仆人于是都明白了：画室是这宅子里吸血鬼专门作祟之处，它在别处便放过了众人。

===Original===
Karl recounted all the tricks he had played to his friend Heinrich, who begged to be allowed to bear him company the following night. To this Karl consented, thinking it would be considerably more agreeable to have a companion. So they took a couple of bottles of wine and some provisions with them, and before midnight found themselves snug in the studio. They sat very quiet for some time, for they knew that if they were seen, two vampires would not be so terrible as one, and might occasion discovery. But at length Heinrich could bear it no longer.
“I say, Lottchen, let’s go and look; for your dead body. What has the old beggar done with it?”
“I think I know. Stop; let me peep out. All right! Come along.”
With a lamp in his hand, he led the way to the cellars, and after searching about a little they discovered it.
“It looks horrid enough,” said Heinrich, “but think a drop or two of wine would brighten it up a little.”
So he took a bottle from his pocket, and after they had had a glass apiece, he dropped a third in blots all over the plaster. Being red wine, it had the effect Höllenrachen desired.
“When they visit it next, they will know that the vampire can find the food he prefers,” said he.

===Chinese===
卡尔把他所玩的那些把戏一五一十讲给朋友海因里希听，海因里希便央求次日夜间也来相陪。卡尔答应了他，心想有个伴儿要惬意得多。于是他们带了两瓶酒和一些吃食，午夜之前便舒舒服服地待在画室里了。他们安安静静地坐了一些时候，因为他们知道，万一被人撞见，两个吸血鬼可不如一个那样吓人，反而可能招来发觉。可海因里希到底按捺不住了。
"我说，洛特臣，咱们去看看吧；你那具死尸。那老叫花子把它怎么处置了？"
"我想我知道在哪儿。等等；让我先瞧一瞧外头。没事了！来吧。"
他提着一盏灯，在头里引路下到地窖，四下找了一会儿，他们便发现了它。
"它瞧着是够吓人的，"海因里希说，"可我想，一两滴酒大概能让它精神些。"
于是他从口袋里掏出一瓶酒，两人各干了一杯之后，他把第三杯一滴滴甩成了斑斑点点，溅得石膏模上到处都是。那是红酒，正起到了赫伦拉肯所希望的效果。
"等他们下次来看它，"他说，"他们便会知道，吸血鬼能找到它所偏爱的食物了。"

===Original===
In a corner close by the plaster, they found the clothes Karl had worn.
“Hillo!” said Heinrich, “we’ll make something of this find.”
So he carried them with him to the studio. There he got hold of the lay-figure.
“What are you about, Heinrich?”
“Going to make a scarecrow to keep the ravens off old Teufel’s pictures,” answered Heinrich, as he went on dressing the lay-figure in Karl’s clothes. He next seated the creature at an easel with its back to the door, so that it should be the first thing the painter should see when he entered. Karl meant to remove this before he went, for it was too comical to fall in with the rest of his proceedings. But the two sat down to their supper, and by the time they had finished the wine, they thought they should like to go to bed. So they got up and went home, and Karl forgot the lay-figure, leaving it in busy motionlessness all night before the easel. When Teufelsbürst saw it, he turned and fled with a cry that brought his daughter to his help. He rushed past her, able only to articulate:
“The vampire! The vampire! Painting!”

===Chinese===
在石膏旁边的一个角落里，他们发现了卡尔从前穿的那身衣裳。
"嘿！"海因里希说，"这一发现可大有用处。"
他便把它们带回了画室。他在那儿拿到了那具人体模型。
"你要干什么，海因里希？"
"做个稻草人，好把乌鸦从老魔鬼（Teufel）的画上赶跑呗。"海因里希一边答着，一边动手把那模型套上卡尔的衣裳。接着他把这东西安在一张画架前，背对着门，好叫它成为画师进门第一眼所见之物。卡尔本打算走之前把这个搬走，因为它与他其余那些布置实在滑稽得格格不入。可两人坐下吃起宵夜，等他们把酒喝完，便都觉得想睡觉了。于是他们起身回家，卡尔却把那具模型给忘了，任它整夜在画架前"忙碌"地一动不动。托伊费尔斯比尔斯特一见之下，大叫一声转身便逃，那喊声把女儿引了来帮忙。他从她身旁冲过去，只能勉强吐出几个字：
"吸血鬼！吸血鬼！在画画！"

===Original===
Far more courageous than he, because her conscience was more peaceful, Lilith passed on to the studio. She too recoiled a step or two when she saw the figure; but with the sight of the back of Karl, as she supposed it to be, came the longing to see the face that was on the other side. So she crept round and round by the wall, as far off as she could. The figure remained motionless. It was a strange kind of shock that she experienced when she saw the face, disgusting from its inanity. The absurdity next struck her; and with the absurdity flashed into her mind the conviction that this was not the doing of a vampire; for of all creatures under the moon, he could not be expected to be a humorist. A wild hope sprang up in her mind that Karl was not dead. Of this she soon resolved to make herself sure.
She closed the door of the studio; in the strength of her new hope undressed the figure, put it in its place, concealed the garments﻿—all the work of a few minutes; and then, finding her father just recovering from the worst of his fear, told him there was nothing in the studio but what ought to be there, and persuaded him to go and see. He not only saw no one, but found that no further liberties had been taken with his pictures. Reassured, he soon persuaded himself that the spectre in this case had been the offspring of his own terror-haunted brain. But he had no spirit for painting now. He wandered about the house, himself haunting it like a restless ghost.

===Chinese===
莉莉丝的良心比他安宁，因此也比他勇敢得多，她径直朝画室走去。她看见那具身影时，也倒退了一两步；可一看见那"卡尔"的背影——她以为那就是卡尔——她便生出一种渴望，想看看另一面那张脸。于是她尽量贴着墙、离得远远地，一圈一圈地绕过去。那身影一动不动。她看见那张脸时所感到的，是一种古怪的震动——那张脸因空洞而令人作呕。紧接着她便觉出了其中的荒唐；而与荒唐一同闪过她心头的，是一个确信：这并非吸血鬼所为；因为月光之下众生之中，最不能指望它是个滑稽家的便是吸血鬼了。一个疯狂的希望在她心里升起：卡尔没有死。这一点她很快便下定决心要亲手弄个明白。
她关上画室的门；凭着新生的希望所赋予的力量，她把那模型脱了衣、放回原位、把衣裳藏好——这一切不过几分钟的功夫；随后，她看见父亲正从最糟的恐惧中缓过来，便告诉他画室里没有任何不该在那儿的东西，并劝他亲自去看。他不但没看见任何人，还发觉自己的画也没再被人动过手脚。他放了心，不久便说服自己：这一回的幽灵不过是他那被恐惧缠住的大脑所生的产物。可他如今再没有作画的心气。他在宅子里游来荡去，自己倒像个不得安息的鬼魂一般，把宅子作祟上了。

===Original===
When night came, Lilith retired to her own room. The waters of fear had begun to subside in the house; but the painter and his old attendant did not yet follow her example.
As soon, however, as the house was quite still, Lilith glided noiselessly down the stairs, went into the studio, where as yet there assuredly was no vampire, and concealed herself in a corner.
As it would not do for an earnest student like Heinrich to be away from his work very often, he had not asked to accompany Lottchen this time. And indeed Karl himself, a little anxious about the result of the scarecrow, greatly preferred going alone.
While she was waiting for what might happen, the conviction grew upon Lilith, as she reviewed all the past of the story, that these phenomena were the work of the real Karl, and of no vampire. In a few moments she was still more sure of this. Behind the screen where she had taken refuge, hung one of the pictures out of which her portrait had been painted the night before last. She had taken a lamp with her into the studio, with the intention of extinguishing it the moment she heard any sign of approach; but as the vampire lingered, she began to occupy herself with examining the picture beside her. She had not looked at it long, before she wetted the tip of her forefinger, and began to rub away at the obliteration. Her suspicions were instantly confirmed: the substance employed was only a gummy wash over the paint. The delight she experienced at the discovery threw her into a mischievous humour.
“I will see,” she said to herself, “whether I cannot match Karl Wolkenlicht at this game.”

===Chinese===
夜幕降临时，莉莉丝回了自己房间。宅中的恐惧之水已开始退去；可画师和他那位老仆从却还迟迟不肯学她的样去睡。
然而宅子一彻底静下来，莉莉丝便无声无息地滑下楼梯，进了画室——此时画室里当然还没有什么吸血鬼——把自己藏进了一个角落。
像海因里希这样一个用功的学生，自然不便常常撇下功课，因此这一回他并未要求陪洛特臣同来。而卡尔自己也因放心不下那稻草人的后果，很愿意独自前去。
莉莉丝在等待可能发生之事的当口，把故事从前到后回想了一遍，愈发确信：这些异象都是真正的卡尔所为，绝非什么吸血鬼。不一会儿，她更加确信了这一点。她藏身的屏风后面，挂着前夜被涂掉她肖像的那幅画之一。她带了一盏灯进画室来，本打算一听见任何接近的动静就把它熄掉；可那吸血鬼迟迟不来，她便开始打量起身边那幅画来。她没看多久，便沾湿了食指尖，在涂掉的地方擦了起来。她的怀疑立刻被证实了：那涂上去的东西，不过是盖在画上的一层胶水。这一发现使她满心欢喜，倒生出一种淘气的心情来。
"我倒要看看，"她自言自语道，"我在这场戏上，能不能比得上卡尔·沃尔肯利希特。"

===Original===
In a closet in the room hung a number of costumes, which Lilith had at different times worn for her father. Among them was a large white drapery, which she easily disposed as a shroud. With the help of some chalk, she soon made herself ghastly enough, and then placing her lamp on the floor behind the screen, and setting a chair over it, so that it should throw no light in any direction, she waited once more for the vampire. Nor had she much longer to wait. She soon heard a door move, the sound of which she hardly knew, and then the studio door opened. Her heart beat dreadfully, not with fear lest it should be a vampire after all, but with hope that it was Karl. To see him once more was too great joy. Would she not make up to him for all her coldness! But would he care for her now? Perhaps he had been quite cured of his longing for a hard heart like hers. She peeped. It was he sure enough, looking as handsome as ever. He was holding his light to look at her last work, and the expression of his face, even in regarding her handiwork, was enough to let her know that he loved her still. If she had not seen this, she dared not have shown herself from her hiding-place. Taking the lamp in her hand, she got upon the chair, and looked over the screen, letting the light shine from below upon her face. She then made a slight noise to attract Karl’s attention. He looked up, evidently rather startled, and saw the face of Lilith in the air: He gave a stifled cry threw himself on his knees with his arms stretched towards her, and moaned﻿—
“I have killed her! I have killed her!”

===Chinese===
屋里一间壁橱里挂着若干套衣裳，是莉莉丝在不同的时候为父亲穿戴过的。其中有一大幅白色的织物，她很容易地把它裹成了裹尸布的样子。借助几支粉笔，她不久便把自己弄得足够怕人，随后把灯放在屏风背后的地板上，又拿一张椅子倒扣其上，使灯光任何一面都透不出来，便再次等候那吸血鬼。她也没有等多久。不久她便听见一扇门动了一下——那声响她几乎认不出来——紧接着画室的门开了。她的心可怕地怦怦直跳，却不是怕那当真是个吸血鬼，而是怀着一份那是卡尔的盼望。能再见他一面，那将是太大的欢喜。她岂不要把她从前所有的冷淡都补偿给他吗！可他如今还会在乎她吗？也许他那颗渴望她这样一颗硬心的人，早已被治愈了。她偷觑了一眼。正是他，千真万确，模样一如既往地俊美。他正举着灯，看他最后那桩"杰作"，而他脸上的神情——哪怕是在端详她的手笔之时——已足以让她知道，他仍爱着她。若不是看见了这一点，她是不敢从藏身处现身的。她提灯在手，登上椅子，从屏风顶上探过头去，让灯光从下面照亮她的脸。随后她弄出一点轻微的声响来引起卡尔的注意。他抬起眼，显然吃了一惊，看见莉莉丝的脸悬在半空：他发出一声压抑的喊叫，扑通跪倒，双臂向她伸去，呻吟着——
"我害死了她！我害死了她！"

===Original===
Lilith descended, and approached him noiselessly. He did not move. She came close to him and said﻿—
“Are you Karl Wolkenlicht?”
His lips moved, but no sound came.
“If you are a vampire, and I am a ghost,” she said﻿—but a low happy laugh alone concluded the sentence.
Karl sprang to his feet. Lilith’s laugh changed into a burst of sobbing and weeping, and in another moment the ghost was in the arms of the vampire.
Lilith had no idea how far her father had wronged Karl, and though, from thinking over the past, he had no doubt that the painter had drugged him, he did not wish to pain her by imparting this conviction. But Lilith was afraid of a reaction of rage and hatred in her father after the terror was removed; and Karl saw that he might thus be deprived of all further intercourse with Lilith, and all chance of softening the old man’s heart towards him; while Lilith would not hear of forsaking him who had banished all the human race but herself. They managed at length to agree upon a plan of operation.

===Chinese===
莉莉丝下来，悄无声息地向他走去。他一动不动。她走到他跟前，说道——
"你是卡尔·沃尔肯利希特吗？"
他嘴唇翕动，却发不出声来。
"你若是吸血鬼，那我便是鬼了。"她说——可这句话只以一声低低的、欢快的轻笑作结。
卡尔一跃而起。莉莉丝的笑声化作一阵抽泣与痛哭，下一刻，鬼已在吸血鬼的怀中。
莉莉丝并不清楚她父亲害卡尔害到了何种地步；而卡尔虽因回想往事而无疑确信那画师给他下过药，却不愿把这层判断说与她听、徒然叫她伤心。可莉莉丝却担心父亲在恐惧除去之后，会反过来发作一阵狂怒与仇恨；而卡尔也看出，他可能由此被剥夺今后与莉莉丝的一切往来，也失去软化那老人之心的一切机会；至于莉莉丝，则断不肯离弃那个把全人类都驱逐在外、唯独留下她自己的人。他们终于设法就一个行动方案达成了协议。

===Original===
The first thing they did was to go to the cellar where the plaster mass lay, Karl carrying with him a great axe used for cleaving wood. Lilith shuddered when she saw it, stained as it was with the wine Heinrich had spilt over it, and almost believed herself the midnight companion of a vampire after all, visiting with him the terrible corpse in which he lived all day. But Karl soon reassured her; and a few good blows of the axe revealed a very different core to that which Teufelsbürst supposed to be in it. Karl broke it into pieces, and with Lilith’s help, who insisted on carrying her share, the whole was soon at the bottom of the Moldau and every trace of its ever having existed removed. Before morning, too, the form of Lilith had dawned anew in every picture. There was no time to restore to its former condition the one Karl had first altered; for in it the changes were all that they seemed; nor indeed was he capable of restoring it in the master’s style; but they put it quite out of the way, and hoped that sufficient time might elapse before the painter thought of it again.

===Chinese===
他们所做的头一件事，便是下到那块石膏模躺着的地窖去——卡尔随身带了一把劈柴用的大斧。莉莉丝一见之下便战栗起来，那斧上还沾着海因里希泼上去的酒渍，她几乎要相信自己到底还是做了吸血鬼的午夜同伙，随他一同来看望他白日里所寄居的那具可怖的尸首。可卡尔很快便安抚了她；几记利斧下去，露出了与托伊费尔斯比尔斯特所以为的大不相同的一颗"心"。卡尔把它砸成碎片，莉莉丝坚持要分担一半的分量，在他的帮助下，整块不久便沉到了莫尔道河的河底，它曾经存在过的一切痕迹都随之而去。天亮之前，莉莉丝的身影又在每一幅画里重新浮现。只是那幅头一个被卡尔改动的画，已来不及恢复原状；因为在那幅画上，那些改动都是货真价实的；何况他本也无能以师父的笔法把它复原；可他们把它彻底藏了起来，只盼在画师再度想起它之前，能过去足够长的时日。

===Original===
When they had done, and Lilith, for all his entreaties, would remain with him no longer, Karl took his former clothes with him, and having spent the rest of the night in his old room, dressed in them in the morning. When Teufelsbürst entered his studio next day, there sat Karl, as if nothing had happened, finishing the drawing on which he had been at work when the fit of insensibility came upon him. The painter started, stared, rubbed his eyes, thought it was another spectral illusion, and was on the point of yielding to his terror, when Karl rose, and approached him with a smile. The healthy, sunshiny countenance of Karl, let him be ghost or goblin, could not fail to produce somewhat of a tranquillising effect on Teufelsbürst. He took his offered hand mechanically, his countenance utterly vacant with idiotic bewilderment. Karl said﻿—
“I was not well, and thought it better to pay a visit to a friend for a few days; but I shall soon make up for lost time, for I am all right now.”

===Chinese===
事毕，莉莉丝任他再三苦留，仍不肯再待下去，卡尔便带上自己从前那身衣裳，在旧房里度过了下半夜，清晨便穿上了那身衣裳。次日托伊费尔斯比尔斯特走进画室时，只见卡尔坐在那儿，仿佛什么事也没发生过，正在完成他昏厥发作时所做的那幅素描。画师一惊，瞪眼，揉眼睛，心想这又是另一场幽灵的幻象，正要向恐惧低头，卡尔却站起身，带着微笑向他走来。卡尔那张健康、阳光的脸，任他是鬼是怪，都不免对托伊费尔斯比尔斯特起几分镇定的作用。他机械地握住他伸来的手，一脸彻底的茫然、近乎痴傻的失神。卡尔说道——
"我前阵子身子不适，便想着到一位朋友那里去住了几天；可我很快就会把耽误的功夫补上，因为我现在已经好了。"

===Original===
He sat down at once, taking no notice of his master’s behaviour, and went on with his drawing. Teufelsbürst stood staring at him for some minutes without moving, then suddenly turned and left the room. Karl heard him hurrying down the cellar stairs. In a few moments he came up again. Karl stole a glance at him. There he stood in the same spot, no doubt more full of bewilderment than ever, but it was not possible that his face should express more. At last he went to his easel, and sat down with a long-drawn sigh as if of relief. But though he sat at his easel, he painted none that day; and as often as Karl ventured a glance, he saw him still staring at him. The discovery that his pictures were restored to their former condition aided, no doubt, in leading him to the same conclusion as the other facts, whatever that conclusion might be﻿—probably that he had been the sport of some evil power, and had been for the greater part of a week utterly bewitched. Lilith had taken care to instruct the old woman, with whom she was all-powerful; and as neither of them showed the smallest traces of the astonishment which seemed to be slowly vitrifying his own brain, he was at last perfectly satisfied that things had been going on all right everywhere but in his inner man; and in this conclusion he certainly was not far wrong, in more senses than one. But when all was restored again to the old routine, it became evident that the peculiar direction of his art in which he had hitherto indulged had ceased to interest him. The shock had acted chiefly upon that part of his mental being which had been so absorbed. He would sit for hours without doing anything, apparently plunged in meditation.﻿—Several weeks elapsed without any change, and both Lilith and Karl were getting dreadfully anxious about him. Karl paid him every attention; and the old man, for he now looked much older than before, submitted to receive his services as well as those of Lilith. At length, one morning, he said in a slow thoughtful tone﻿—
“Karl Wolkenlicht, I should like to paint you.”

===Chinese===
他立刻坐下，对师父的种种神态不置一词，继续画他的画。托伊费尔斯比尔斯特一动不动地瞪了他几分钟，忽然转身出去了房间。卡尔听见他匆匆下到地窖楼梯。片刻之后他又上来。卡尔偷觑了他一眼。他还站在原地，无疑比先前更满腹惶惑，可他的脸也再表不出更多的东西来了。他终于走到自己的画架前坐下，长叹一声，仿佛松了口气。可他虽坐在画架前，那一整天却一笔也未画；卡尔每鼓起勇气偷瞄一眼，都见他还在瞪着自己。他发觉自己的画都已被恢复原状，这无疑也促使他得出了与其余事实相同的结论——无论那结论究竟是什么——多半是：自己一直被某种邪门的力量所戏弄，且足有大半个星期之久，完全中了邪。莉莉丝早已关照过那老妇人——她在老妇人面前是说一不二的；而那两人脸上都看不出一丝一毫那种似乎正慢慢把他自己的脑子烧成玻璃的惊愕，他便终于彻底满意地认定：除了他自己内心之外，处处一切早都好好的；而他这一结论，在不止一层意义上，倒也并不算错。可待到一切又恢复旧日的常规之后，便显而易见：他向来沉浸其中的那一路艺术，已不再能引起他的兴致。那场震动主要作用于他那被如此全神投入的那部分心神之上。他往往一坐几个钟头无所事事，仿佛沉入深思。——几个星期过去了，毫无变化，莉莉丝和卡尔都为他忧心忡忡。卡尔对他悉心照料；那老人——他如今看上去比从前苍老了许多——也顺从地接受着他的侍奉，一如接受莉莉丝的侍奉。终于，某一日清晨，他以一种缓慢而若有所思的声调说道——
"卡尔·沃尔肯利希特，我想画你。"

===Original===
“Certainly, sir,” answered Karl, jumping up, “where would you like me to sit?”
So the ice of silence and inactivity was broken, and the painter drew and painted; and the spring of his art flowed once more; and he made a beautiful portrait of Karl﻿—a portrait without evil or suffering. And as soon as he had finished Karl, he began once more to paint Lilith; and when he had painted her, he composed a picture for the very purpose of introducing them together; and in this picture there was neither ugliness nor torture, but human feeling and human hope instead. Then Karl knew that he might speak to him of Lilith; and he spoke, and was heard with a smile. But he did not dare to tell him the truth of the vampire story till one day that Teufelsbürst was lying on the floor of a room in Karl’s ancestral castle, half smothered in grandchildren; when the only answer it drew from the old man was a kind of shuddering laugh and the words “Don’t speak of it, Karl, my boy!”

===Chinese===
"当然，先生。"卡尔答道，一跃而起，"您要我在哪儿坐？"
于是沉默与停滞的坚冰被打破了，画师又画又绘；他艺术的泉源再度流淌起来；他给卡尔画了一幅美的肖像——一幅没有邪恶、没有苦痛的肖像。他一画完卡尔，便又开始画莉莉丝；画完她之后，他又特意构思了一幅画，专为把他们两个一同画进去；在这幅画里，既无丑陋、也无酷刑，取而代之的是人的情感与人的希望。那时卡尔便知道，他可以向他说起莉莉丝了；他说了，对方含笑倾听。可那吸血鬼一事的真相，他一直不敢向他吐露——直到有一天，托伊费尔斯比尔斯特正躺在卡尔祖传城堡中一间屋子的地板上，被一群孙辈几乎整个埋了起来；那事从他那里只引出老人一声战栗的轻笑，和一句话："别再提它了，卡尔，我的孩子！"

## The Giant’s Heart / 巨人之心

===Original===
There was once a giant who lived on the borders of Giantland where it touched on the country of common people.

Everything in Giantland was so big that the common people saw only a mass of awful mountains and clouds; and no living man had ever come from it, as far as anybody knew, to tell what he had seen in it.

===Chinese===
从前，在巨人国（Giantland）的边境上——那里与凡人之地相接——住着一个巨人。

巨人国里样样东西都大得惊人，凡人远远望去，只看见一片骇人的崇山峻岭和重重云雾；据人所知，也从来没有哪个活人从那里面走出来过，向世人讲述自己曾在其中见过的光景。

===Original===
Somewhere near these borders, on the other side, by the edge of a great forest, lived a labourer with his wife and a great many children. One day Tricksey-Wee, as they called her, teased her brother Buffy-Bob, till he could not bear it any longer, and gave her a box on the ear. Tricksey-Wee cried; and Buffy-Bob was so sorry and so ashamed of himself that he cried too, and ran off into the wood. He was so long gone that Tricksey-Wee began to be frightened, for she was very fond of her brother; and she was so distressed that she had first teased him and then cried, that at last she ran into the wood to look for him, though there was more chance of losing herself than of finding him. And, indeed, so it seemed likely to turn out; for, running on without looking, she at length found herself in a valley she knew nothing about. And no wonder; for what she thought was a valley with round, rocky sides, was no other than the space between two of the roots of a great tree that grew on the borders of Giantland. She climbed over the side of it, and went towards what she took for a black, round-topped mountain, far away; but which she soon discovered to be close to her, and to be a hollow place so great that she could not tell what it was hollowed out of. Staring at it, she found that it was a doorway; and going nearer and staring harder, she saw the door, far in, with a knocker of iron upon it, a great many yards above her head, and as large as the anchor of a big ship. Now, nobody had ever been unkind to Tricksey-Wee, and therefore she was not afraid of anybody. For Buffy-Bob’s box on the ear she did not think worth considering. So spying a little hole at the bottom of the door which had been nibbled by some giant mouse, she crept through it, and found herself in an enormous hall. She could not have seen the other end of it at all, except for the great fire that was burning there, diminished to a spark in the distance. Towards this fire she ran as fast as she could, and was not far from it when something fell before her with a great clatter, over which she tumbled, and went rolling on the floor. She was not much hurt however, and got up in a moment. Then she saw that what she had fallen over was not unlike a great iron bucket. When she examined it more closely, she discovered that it was a thimble; and looking up to see who had dropped it, beheld a huge face, with spectacles as big as the round windows in a church, bending over her, and looking everywhere for the thimble. Tricksey-Wee immediately laid hold of it in both her arms, and lifted it about an inch nearer to the nose of the peering giantess. This movement made the old lady see where it was, and, her finger popping into it, it vanished from the eyes of Tricksey-Wee, buried in the folds of a white stocking like a cloud in the sky, which Mrs. Giant was busy darning. For it was Saturday night, and her husband would wear nothing but white stockings on Sunday. To be sure he did eat little children, but only *very* little ones; and if ever it crossed his mind that it was wrong to do so, he always said to himself that he wore whiter stockings on Sunday than any other giant in all Giantland.

===Chinese===
就在这边境的另一侧，紧挨着一片大森林的边上，住着一个农夫，带着他的妻子和一大群孩子。有一天，小淘气崔克西（Tricksey-Wee）——大伙儿都这么叫她——把哥哥胖墩鲍勃（Buffy-Bob）捉弄得再也忍不住，他便在她耳朵上扇了一巴掌。小淘气崔克西哭了起来；胖墩鲍勃又懊悔又羞愧，也跟着哭了起来，一头钻进了树林里。他去了好久好久，小淘气崔克西开始害怕起来，因为她十分疼爱哥哥；她又懊恼自己先去招惹他、又跟着哭闹，心里难受得不行，最后也跑进了树林去找他——尽管迷路的可能性比找到他的可能性还大。果然，事情眼看着就要这样收场了；她一路只顾跑、没看方向，不知不觉闯进了一个全然陌生的山谷。这也难怪；因为她当作是岩石环抱的山谷的所在，其实不过是一棵巨树两条树根之间的空隙，那棵树就长在巨人国的边境上。她翻过空隙的一边，朝远处一个黑乎乎、圆顶顶的东西走去，她以为那是一座山；可走近了才发现，那东西就在她跟前，是个凹进去的大洞，大得她根本看不出是从什么东西里挖出来的。她盯着瞧，看出那是一扇门；再凑近细看，瞧见门在里面老远的地方，门上有一只铁门环，高高悬在她头顶不知多少码开外，大得像大船上的铁锚。原来，从来没有人欺负过小淘气崔克西，因此她谁也不怕。胖墩鲍勃那一巴掌，她根本不放在心上。于是，她瞧见门底下有个小洞，是被哪只巨人老鼠啃出来的，便钻了进去，发现自己置身于一座硕大无朋的大厅里。要不是尽头有一团大火在烧，远远只剩一个火星，她根本看不见大厅的那一头。她拼命朝那团火跑去，离火不远时，"哐当"一声巨响，一个东西落在了她面前，她被绊了一跤，在地上滚了出去。好在伤得不重，她一骨碌便爬了起来。这才看清绊倒她的东西，像是一只大铁桶。再仔细一瞧，原来是一只顶针；她抬头想看看是谁掉的，却见一张巨大的脸正俯在她上方，鼻梁上架着一副眼镜，大得像教堂里的圆窗，正四处找寻那只顶针。小淘气崔克西连忙用两只胳膊抱起顶针，往上举了大约一英寸，凑近那张四处张望的巨人婆（giantess）的鼻子。这一动，老奶奶便瞧见了顶针在哪儿，她手指一戳，顶针便从小淘气崔克西眼前消失，埋进了一只白色长袜的褶子里——那袜子像天上的一团云——巨人太太正忙着织补它。原来这天是星期六晚上，她丈夫星期天非白袜子不穿。不错，他确实吃小孩，可只吃*很小很小*的小孩；万一他偶尔想起这样做到底不对，也总安慰自己说：在整个巨人国里，没有哪个巨人星期天能穿上比他更白的袜子。

===Original===
At the same instant Tricksey-Wee heard a sound like the wind in a tree full of leaves, and could not think what it could be; till, looking up, she found that it was the giantess whispering to her; and when she tried very hard she could hear what she said well enough.

“Run away, dear little girl,” she said, “as fast as you can; for my husband will be home in a few minutes.”

“But I’ve never been naughty to your husband,” said Tricksey-Wee, looking up in the giantess’s face.

“That doesn’t matter. You had better go. He is fond of little children, particularly little girls.”

“Oh, then he won’t hurt me.”

“I am not sure of that. He is so fond of them that he eats them up; and I am afraid he couldn’t help hurting you a little. He’s a very good man though.”

“Oh! then﻿—” began Tricksey-Wee, feeling rather frightened; but before she could finish her sentence she heard the sound of footsteps very far apart and very heavy. The next moment, who should come running towards her, full speed, and as pale as death, but Buffy-Bob. She held out her arms, and he ran into them. But when she tried to kiss him, she only kissed the back of his head; for his white face and round eyes were turned to the door.

===Chinese===
就在这时，小淘气崔克西听见了一阵响声，像风吹过满叶的大树，她想不出那会是什么；抬头一瞧，才发现是巨人婆在跟她悄声说话；她竖起耳朵使劲听，才勉强听清她说的话。

"快逃吧，亲爱的小姑娘，"她说，"能跑多快就跑多快；我丈夫再一会儿就要回家了。"

"可我从没对你丈夫使过坏呀。"小淘气崔克西仰起脸，望着巨人婆说。

"那不要紧。你最好还是走吧。他喜爱小孩，尤其是小女孩。"

"哦，那他就不会伤害我了。"

"那我可说不准。他太喜爱他们了，喜爱到要把他们一口吃掉；我怕他难免会弄伤你一点点。不过，他是个很好的人。"

"哦！那么——"小淘气崔克西开口道，心里有点害怕；可她话还没说完，就听见一阵脚步声，步与步之间隔得老远，每一步都沉重无比。紧接着，谁该拼命朝她跑来、脸白得像死人一样？不是别人，正是胖墩鲍勃。她张开双臂，他一头扑进她怀里。可她想亲他时，却只亲到了他的后脑勺；因为他那苍白的脸和圆圆的眼睛，都死死盯着门口那一边。

===Original===
“Run, children; run and hide!” said the giantess.

“Come, Buffy,” said Tricksey; “yonder’s a great brake; we’ll hide in it.”

The brake was a big broom; and they had just got into the bristles of it when they heard the door open with a sound of thunder, and in stalked the giant. You would have thought you saw the whole earth through the door when he opened it, so wide was it; and when he closed it, it was like nightfall.

===Chinese===
"快跑，孩子们；快跑去藏起来！"巨人婆说。

"来，胖墩，"崔克西说，"那边有一大丛荆棘；我们藏到里面去。"

原来，那"荆棘丛"是一把大扫帚；他们刚钻进帚毛里，就听见门开了，声若雷鸣，巨人昂首阔步走了进来。他一开门，你会以为透过门缝看见了整个大地——门开得那样宽；他一关门，屋子里便像夜幕降临。

===Original===
“Where is that little boy?” he cried, with a voice like the bellowing of a cannon. “He looked a very nice boy indeed. I am almost sure he crept through the mousehole at the bottom of the door. Where is he, my dear?”

“I don’t know,” answered the giantess.

“But you know it is wicked to tell lies; don’t you, my dear?” retorted the giant.

“Now, you ridiculous old Thunderthump!” said his wife, with a smile as broad as the sea in the sun, “how can I mend your white stockings and look after little boys? You have got plenty to last you over Sunday, I am sure. Just look what good little boys they are!”

Tricksey-Wee and Buffy-Bob peered through the bristles, and discovered a row of little boys, about a dozen, with very fat faces and goggle eyes, sitting before the fire, and looking stupidly into it. Thunderthump intended the most of these for pickling, and was feeding them well before salting them. Now and then, however, he could not keep his teeth off them, and would eat one by the by, without salt.

===Chinese===
"那个小男孩在哪儿？"他用炮声般洪亮的嗓门喝道，"他看着确实是个挺不错的小男孩。我几乎敢肯定，他是从门底下老鼠洞钻进来的。亲爱的，他在哪儿？"

"我不知道。"巨人婆答道。

"可你知道撒谎是作恶吧，亲爱的，嗯？"巨人反唇相讥。

"得了吧，你这可笑的老雷霆步（Thunderthump）！"他妻子说道，笑得像阳光下的大海一般开阔，"我既要替你补白袜子，又要替你看小男孩，怎么顾得过来？够你过了星期天再穿的，我敢肯定已经备下了。你瞧瞧，这些小男孩多乖！"

小淘气崔克西和胖墩鲍勃从帚毛缝里往外瞧，看见一排小男孩，约莫一打，个个脸蛋滚圆、眼睛鼓突，坐在火堆前呆呆地望着火。雷霆步本打算把其中大多数拿去腌制，所以先喂得他们肥肥壮壮，再下盐。不过有时候他也忍不住嘴，会顺手拿一个当下吃了，不加盐。

===Original===
He strode up to the wretched children. Now, what made them very wretched indeed was, that they knew if they could only keep from eating, and grow thin, the giant would dislike them, and turn them out to find their way home; but notwithstanding this, so greedy were they, that they ate as much as ever they could hold. The giantess, who fed them, comforted herself with thinking that they were not real boys and girls, but only little pigs pretending to be boys and girls.

“Now tell me the truth,” cried the giant, bending his face down over them. They shook with terror, and everyone hoped it was somebody else the giant liked best. “Where is the little boy that ran into the hall just now? Whoever tells me a lie shall be instantly boiled.”

“He’s in the broom,” cried one dough-faced boy. “He’s in there, and a little girl with him.”

===Chinese===
他大步走向那群倒霉的孩子。要说他们实在倒霉——是因为他们心里明白：只要自己能忍住不吃、瘦下来，巨人就会嫌恶他们，把他们赶出去自寻回家路；可尽管如此，他们贪嘴至极，还是拼命往肚里塞。喂他们的巨人婆则自我安慰：他们算不得真孩子，不过是些装作孩子的小猪罢了。

"快跟我说实话，"巨人喝道，把脸俯到他们上方。他们吓得直哆嗦，每个人都盼着巨人最喜欢的是别人。"刚才跑进大厅的那个小男孩在哪儿？谁要对我撒谎，立刻下锅煮了。"

"他在扫帚里，"一个面团脸的男孩喊道，"他在里面，还有一个小女孩跟他在一起。"

===Original===
“The naughty children,” cried the giant, “to hide from *me*!” And he made a stride towards the broom.

“Catch hold of the bristles, Bobby. Get right into a tuft, and hold on,” cried Tricksey-Wee, just in time.

The giant caught up the broom, and seeing nothing under it, set it down again with a force that threw them both on the floor. He then made two strides to the boys, caught the dough-faced one by the neck, took the lid off a great pot that was boiling on the fire, popped him in as if he had been a trussed chicken, put the lid on again, and saying, “There, boys! See what comes of lying!” asked no more questions; for, as he always kept his word, he was afraid he might have to do the same to them all; and he did not like boiled boys. He like to eat them crisp, as radishes, whether forked or not, ought to be eaten. He then sat down, and asked his wife if his supper was ready. She looked into the pot, and throwing the boy out with the ladle, as if he had been a black beetle that had tumbled in and had had the worst of it, answered that she thought it was. Whereupon he rose to help her; and taking the pot from the fire, poured the whole contents, bubbling and splashing, into a dish like a vat. Then they sat down to supper. The children in the broom could not see what they had; but it seemed to agree with them, for the giant talked like thunder, and the giantess answered like the sea, and they grew chattier and chattier. At length the giant said﻿—

===Chinese===
"调皮的小东西，"巨人吼道，"竟敢躲着*我*！"说罢便朝扫帚跨了一步。

"抓紧帚毛，小鲍勃。钻进一簇毛里头，抓牢了。"小淘气崔克西喊道，喊得正是时候。

巨人抄起扫帚，看看底下什么也没有，便又重重一放，力道大得把他们俩都摔在了地上。接着他两步跨到那群男孩跟前，一把掐住面团脸男孩的脖子，揭开火上正咕嘟嘟煮着的一口大锅的盖子，像塞一只捆好的小鸡似的把他丢了进去，盖回盖子，说道："瞧见没有，孩子们！这就是撒谎的下场！"便不再追问了；因为他说到做到，怕自己不得不把他们如法炮制；而他不喜欢水煮的男孩。他喜欢吃脆生生的，就像萝卜——不论那萝卜分叉与否——本就该脆生生地吃。说罢他坐下，问妻子晚饭可曾备好。她朝锅里瞧了一眼，用勺子把那男孩舀了出来——就像舀出一只栽进锅里、被烫得半死的黑甲虫——答道，她想差不多了。于是他起身帮忙；把锅从火上端下，把里面咕嘟冒泡、四处飞溅的物事，整锅倒进一只大桶似的盘子里。两人便坐下用晚饭。扫帚里的孩子看不见他们吃的是什么；可那东西似乎很对他们胃口，因为巨人说话如雷鸣，巨人婆应声如海涛，他俩越聊越起劲。终于，巨人说道——

===Original===
“I don’t feel quite comfortable about that heart of mine.” And as he spoke, instead of laying his hand on his bosom, he waved it away towards the corner where the children were peeping from the broom-bristles, like frightened little mice.

“Well, you know, my darling Thunderthump,” answered his wife, “I always thought it ought to be nearer home. But you know best, of course.”

“Ha! ha! You don’t know where it is, wife. I moved it a month ago.”

“What a man you are, Thunderthump! You trust any creature alive rather than your wife.”

Here the giantess gave a sob which sounded exactly like a wave going flop into the mouth of a cave up to the roof.

“Where have you got it now?” she resumed, checking her emotion.

“Well, Doodlem, I don’t mind telling *you*,” answered the giant, soothingly. “The great she-eagle has got it for a nest egg. She sits on it night and day, and thinks she will bring the greatest eagle out of it that ever sharpened his beak on the rocks of Mount Skycrack. I can warrant no one else will touch it while she has got it. But she is rather capricious, and I confess I am not easy about it; for the least scratch of one of her claws would do for me at once. And she *has* claws.”

I refer anyone who doubts this part of my story to certain chronicles of Giantland preserved among the Celtic nations. It was quite a common thing for a giant to put his heart out to nurse, because he did not like the trouble and responsibility of doing it himself; although I must confess it was a dangerous sort of plan to take, especially with such a delicate viscus as the heart.

===Chinese===
"我这颗心呐，我心里总不大踏实。"他一边说，一边没有把手按在自己胸口上，反倒朝孩子们张望的那个角落挥了挥手——孩子们正从帚毛缝里像受惊的小老鼠似的探头偷看。

"哎呀，你知道的，我亲爱的雷霆步，"他妻子答道，"我一向觉得它该放得离家近一些。不过，当然啦，你最清楚。"

"哈！哈！你可不知道它在哪儿，老婆。一个月前我把它挪了地方。"

"你这人啊，雷霆步！你宁可相信世上随便哪个活物，也不肯信你老婆。"

说到这里，巨人婆抽泣了一声，听上去活像一个浪头"啪"地灌进山洞洞口、直撞到洞顶。

"这会儿你把它搁哪儿了？"她强压着情绪，又开口问道。

"唉，杜德伦（Doodlem），告诉你倒也无妨。"巨人和缓地答道，"那大母鹰如今把它当了窝里的蛋。她日日夜夜都蹲在上面，一心想孵出一只前所未有、最了不起的鹰来——一只曾在裂天峰（Mount Skycrack）的岩石上磨过喙的鹰。有她在，我敢担保谁也碰它不得。只是她脾气有点反复无常，我承认这事让我心里不安；因为她随便一爪子挠着，就能一下子要了我的命。何况她的爪子，*可真是利*。"

若有谁怀疑我这段故事，请去查阅保存在凯尔特诸族中的若干巨人国编年史。巨人把自己的心送去寄养，原是稀松平常之事，因为他懒得自个儿操心、也嫌担责任；尽管我不得不承认，这种法子颇为凶险，尤其是像心脏这般娇嫩的脏器。

===Original===
All this time Buffy-Bob and Tricksey-Wee were listening with long ears.

“Oh!” thought Tricksey-Wee, “if I could but find the giant’s cruel heart, wouldn’t I give it a squeeze!”

The giant and giantess went on talking for a long time. The giantess kept advising the giant to hide his heart somewhere in the house; but he seemed afraid of the advantage it would give her over him.

“You could hide it at the bottom of the flour-barrel,” said she.

“That would make me feel chokey,” answered he.

“Well, in the coal-cellar. Or in the dust-hole﻿—that’s the place! No one would think of looking for your heart in the dust-hole.”

“Worse and worse!” cried the giant.

“Well, the water-butt,” suggested she.

“No, no; it would grow spongy there,” said he.

“Well, what *will* you do with it?”

“I will leave it a month longer where it is, and then I will give it to the Queen of the Kangaroos, and she will carry it in her pouch for me. It is best to change its place, you know, lest my enemies should scent it out. But, dear Doodlem, it’s a fretting care to have a heart of one’s own to look after. The responsibility is too much for me. If it were not for a bite of a radish now and then, I never could bear it.”

Here the giant looked lovingly towards the row of little boys by the fire, all of whom were nodding, or asleep on the floor.

===Chinese===
这半天工夫，胖墩鲍勃和小淘气崔克西一直竖着耳朵细听。

"哎呀！"小淘气崔克西心想，"要是我能找到巨人那颗狠毒的心，我非得狠狠捏它一下不可！"

巨人和巨人婆又絮絮叨叨谈了好一阵。巨人婆不住劝他把心藏在屋里某处；可他似乎怕这样一来，便落了她手里的把柄。

"你可以把它藏在面粉桶底呀。"她说。

"那会叫我透不过气来。"他答。

"那，煤窖里。要不，垃圾洞里——那地方才好呢！谁也不会想到去垃圾洞里找你的心。"

"越发糟了！"巨人嚷道。

"那，蓄水桶呢。"她提议。

"不，不；搁那儿它会发海绵似的。"他说。

"那，你打算怎么办？"

"我在原处再搁一个月，然后就把它交给袋鼠女王（Queen of the Kangaroos），她会替我放在她的育儿袋里带着。你知道，最好常常挪挪地方，免得被仇家嗅出踪迹。可是，亲爱的杜德伦，自个儿拥有一颗心、要照看它，真是一件烦人的操心事儿。这担子对我太重了。要不是偶尔还能咬一口'萝卜'提提神，我简直承受不住。"

说到这里，巨人含情脉脉地望向火边那一排小男孩——他们个个都在打盹，要么就在地板上睡熟了。

===Original===
“Why don’t you trust it to me, dear Thunderthump?” said his wife. “I would take the best possible care of it.”

“I don’t doubt it, my love. But the responsibility would be too much for *you*. You would no longer be my darling, lighthearted, airy, laughing Doodlem. It would transform you into a heavy, oppressed woman, weary of life﻿—as I am.”

The giant closed his eyes and pretended to go to sleep. His wife got his stockings, and went on with her darning. Soon the giant’s pretence became reality, and the giantess began to nod over her work.

===Chinese===
"你为什么不把它交给我呢，亲爱的雷霆步？"他妻子说，"我会把它照看得好好的。"

"这我不怀疑，亲爱的。可这担子对你来说也太重了。你就再也不是我那心头轻快、无忧无虑、爱笑爱乐的杜德伦了。它会把你变成一个沉重、压抑、厌倦人生的女人——就像我一样。"

巨人闭上眼睛，假装睡着了。他妻子拿起他的袜子，接着织补。不久，巨人的假睡弄假成真，巨人婆也在活计上头打起盹来。

===Original===
“Now, Buffy,” whispered Tricksey-Wee, “now’s our time. I think it’s moonlight, and we had better be off. There’s a door with a hole for the cat just behind us.”

“All right,” said Bob; “I’m ready.”

So they got out of the broom-brake and crept to the door. But to their great disappointment, when they got through it, they found themselves in a sort of shed. It was full of tubs and things, and, though it was built of wood only, they could not find a crack.

“Let us try this hole,” said Tricksey; for the giant and giantess were sleeping behind them, and they dared not go back.

“All right,” said Bob.

He seldom said anything else than “All right.”

===Chinese===
"喂，胖墩，"小淘气崔克西悄声说，"现在正是时候。我瞧外头像是月光地，我们还是走吧。咱们身后就有一扇门，门上有个猫洞。"

"好嘞。"鲍勃说，"我准备好了。"

于是他们从扫帚丛里钻出来，蹑手蹑脚走到门前。可令他们大失所望的是，钻过门去，却发现自己进了一间棚屋模样的地方。里面堆满了大桶之类的东西，虽说全是用木头搭的，他们却找不到一道缝。

"我们试试这个洞吧。"崔克西说；因为巨人和巨人婆就在身后睡熟了，他们不敢往回走。

"好嘞。"鲍勃说。

除了"好嘞"，他很少说别的话。

===Original===
Now this hole was in a mound that came in through the wall of the shed, and went along the floor for some distance. They crawled into it, and found it very dark. But groping their way along, they soon came to a small crack, through which they saw grass, pale in the moonshine. As they crept on, they found the hole began to get wider and lead upwards.

“What is that noise of rushing?” said Buffy-Bob.

“I can’t tell,” replied Tricksey; “for, you see, I don’t know what we are in.”

The fact was, they were creeping along a channel in the heart of a giant tree; and the noise they heard was the noise of the sap rushing along in its wooden pipes. When they laid their ears to the wall, they heard it gurgling along with a pleasant noise.

===Chinese===
原来，这洞口开在一道穿墙而入、沿着地面延伸了一程的土埂上。他们爬了进去，发觉里头一片漆黑。但摸着洞壁往前走，不久便来到一道小裂缝前，透过裂缝，他们看见月光下苍白的青草。他们再往前爬，发现洞开始变宽，并且渐渐往上倾斜。

"那哗哗的水声是什么？"胖墩鲍勃问。

"我说不上来，"崔克西答道，"因为，你瞧，我也不知道咱们这是在什么东西里头。"

原来呀，他们正沿着一条通道，在一棵巨树的心材里穿行；他们听见的那阵声响，正是树液顺着木质的管道奔流的声音。当他们把耳朵贴在洞壁上，便听见它汩汩流淌，悦耳动听。

===Original===
“It sounds kind and good,” said Tricksey. “It is water running. Now it must be running from somewhere to somewhere. I think we had better go on, and we shall come somewhere.”

It was now rather difficult to go on, for they had to climb as if they were climbing a hill; and now the passage was wide. Nearly worn out, they saw light overhead at last, and creeping through a crack into the open air, found themselves on the fork of a huge tree. A great, broad, uneven space lay around them, out of which spread boughs in every direction, the smallest of them as big as the biggest tree in the country of common people. Overhead were leaves enough to supply all the trees they had ever seen. Not much moonlight could come through, but the leaves would glimmer white in the wind at times. The tree was full of giant birds. Every now and then, one would sweep through, with a great noise. But, except an occasional chirp, sounding like a shrill pipe in a great organ, they made no noise. All at once an owl began to hoot. He thought he was singing. As soon as he began, other birds replied, making rare game of him. To their astonishment, the children found they could understand every word they sang. And what they sang was something like this:﻿

===Chinese===
"这声音听来又和善又美好，"崔克西说，"是水流的声音。那么，它一定是从某处流向某处。我想我们最好继续走，总会走到一个地方的。"

这会儿往前走可不大容易了，因为他们得像爬山一样往上攀；通道也变宽了。他们累得几乎力竭，终于看见头顶有了光亮；从一道裂缝爬出去，到了露天，发现自己正置身于一棵巨树分杈的地方。四周是一片宽阔、高低不平的所在，从其中朝四面八方伸出粗枝大杈，最小的一根，也抵得上凡人之地里最大的树。头顶的叶子，多得足以挂满他们平生所见过的所有树木。月光难得漏得下来，但风一吹，叶片时时泛出银白。树上栖满了巨大的鸟。不时有一只"扑啦"一声掠过，动静很大。不过除了偶尔一两声啁啾——像大管风琴里一声尖锐的哨管——它们并不吵闹。忽然，一只猫头鹰叫了起来。他自以为是在歌唱。他一开口，别的鸟儿便纷纷应和，尽情地拿他打趣。令孩子们惊奇的是，他们发现自己竟能听懂鸟儿唱的每一个字。它们唱的大致是这样的：

===Original===
> “I will sing a song.
> I’m the Owl.”
>
> “Sing a song, you Singsong
> Ugly fowl!
> What will you sing about,
> Night in and Day out?”
>
> “Sing about the night;
> I’m the Owl.”
>
> “You could not see for the light,
> Stupid fowl.”
>
> “Oh! the Moon! and the Dew!
> And the Shadows!﻿—tu-whoo!”

===Chinese===
> "我要唱一支歌。
> 我是猫头鹰。"
>
> "唱一支歌吧，你这油腔滑调的
> 丑鸟！
> 你要唱些什么，
> 从夜晚唱到天明？"
>
> "唱夜晚；
> 我是猫头鹰。"
>
> "有光的时候你什么也瞧不见，
> 蠢笨的鸟。"
>
> "哦！月亮！还有露水！
> 还有那些影子！——呼——呜！"

===Original===
The owl spread out his silent, soft, sly wings, and lighting between Tricksey-Wee and Buffy-Bob, nearly smothered them, closing up one under each wing. It was like being buried in a down bed. But the owl did not like anything between his sides and his wings, so he opened his wings again, and the children made haste to get out. Tricksey-Wee immediately went in front of the bird, and looking up into his huge face, which was as round as the eyes of the giantess’s spectacles, and much bigger, dropped a pretty courtesy, and said﻿—“Please, Mr. Owl, I want to whisper to you.”

“Very well, small child,” answered the owl, looking important, and stooping his ear towards her. “What is it?”

“Please tell me where the eagle lives that sits on the giant’s heart.”

“Oh, you naughty child! That’s a secret. For shame!”

And with a great hiss that terrified them, the owl flew into the tree. All birds are fond of secrets; but not many of them can keep them so well as the owl.

===Chinese===
猫头鹰张开他那无声、柔软、狡黠的翅膀，一落落在小淘气崔克西和胖墩鲍勃之间，几乎把他们闷得透不过气——一边腋下塞了一个。那感觉像是被埋进了一床羽绒被里。可猫头鹰最不喜侧肋和翅膀之间夹着东西，便又张开翅膀，孩子们赶紧溜了出来。小淘气崔克西立刻走到鸟儿跟前，仰头望着他那张硕大的脸——那张脸圆得像巨人婆眼镜的镜片，而且大得多——端端正正行了一个漂亮的屈膝礼，说道——"猫头鹰先生，劳驾，我想跟您说句悄悄话。"

"很好，小孩子。"猫头鹰答道，一副神气的模样，把耳朵俯向她，"什么事？"

"劳驾，请告诉我，那只蹲在巨人心上的鹰住在哪儿。"

"哎呀，你这调皮的孩子！那可是个秘密。不害臊！"

说罢，猫头鹰"嘶"地大喝一声，把他们吓了一跳，便飞进树里去了。所有的鸟儿都爱听秘密；但能像猫头鹰这样守口如瓶的，可没几个。

===Original===
So the children went on because they did not know what else to do. They found the way very rough and difficult, the tree was so full of humps and hollows. Now and then they plashed into a pool of rain; now and then they came upon twigs growing out of the trunk where they had no business, and they were as large as full-grown poplars. Sometimes they came upon great cushions of soft moss, and on one of them they lay down and rested. But they had not lain long before they spied a large nightingale sitting on a branch, with its bright eyes looking up at the moon. In a moment more he began to sing, and the birds about him began to reply, but in a different tone from that in which they had replied to the owl. Oh, the birds did call the nightingale such pretty names! The nightingale sang, and the birds replied like this:﻿

===Chinese===
于是孩子们继续向前，因为他们也不知道还能怎么办。一路坑坑洼洼、十分难走，树上满是鼓包和凹洞。他们不时"啪嗒"踩进一汪雨水里；不时撞见从树干上不该长枝条的地方冒出来的细枝——那一根根都有成年的杨树那么粗。有时他们走到一大团柔软的青苔垫子上，便在其中一块上躺下歇息。可他们没躺多久，便瞧见一只硕大的夜莺栖在枝头，亮晶晶的眼睛仰望着月亮。不一会儿他便唱了起来，周围的鸟儿也跟着应和，只是语调和应和猫头鹰时大不相同。哎呀，那些鸟儿给夜莺起了好多动听的名字！夜莺唱着，鸟儿们这样应和：

===Original===
> “I will sing a song.
> I’m the nightingale.”
>
> “Sing a song, long, long,
> Little Neverfail!
> What will you sing about,
> Light in or light out?”
>
> “Sing about the light
> Gone away;
> Down, away, and out of sight﻿—
> Poor lost Day!
> Mourning for the Day dead,
> O’er his dim bed.”

===Chinese===
> "我要唱一支歌。
> 我是夜莺。"
>
> "唱一支歌吧，长啊长，
> 小小常鸣鸟（Neverfail）！
> 你要唱些什么，
> 光进来，还是光出去？"
>
> "唱那已经逝去了的光；
> 唱那落下去、远去了、
> 消失不见的——
> 可怜迷失的白昼！
> 在白昼昏暗的床榻旁，
> 为死去的白昼哀伤。"

===Original===
The nightingale sang so sweetly, that the children would have fallen asleep but for fear of losing any of the song. When the nightingale stopped they got up and wandered on. They did not know where they were going, but they thought it best to keep going on, because then they might come upon something or other. They were very sorry they had forgotten to ask the nightingale about the eagle’s nest, but his music had put everything else out of their heads. They resolved, however, not to forget the next time they had a chance. So they went on and on, till they were both tired, and Tricksey-Wee said at last, trying to laugh﻿—

“I declare my legs feel just like a Dutch doll’s.”

“Then here’s the place to go to bed in,” said Buffy-Bob.

They stood at the edge of a last year’s nest, and looked down with delight into the round, mossy cave. Then they crept gently in, and, lying down in each other’s arms, found it so deep, and warm, and comfortable, and soft, that they were soon fast asleep.

===Chinese===
夜莺唱得那样动听，孩子们要不是怕错过半个音符，早睡着了。夜莺停下之后，他们便起身继续漫游。他们不知道自己在往哪儿走，但觉得最好还是一直走下去，因为那样说不定能撞见点什么。他们很懊恼，竟忘了向夜莺打听鹰巢的事，可那歌声把其余一切都从他们脑子里赶跑了。不过他们下定决心，下次再有机会，绝不忘记。于是他们走啊走啊，两人都走累了，最后小淘气崔克西强笑着说道——

"我说，我的两条腿现在就像荷兰木偶的腿一样了。"

"那这儿就是睡觉的地方了。"胖墩鲍勃说。

他们站在一只去年的旧巢边沿，欢欢喜喜地朝下望进那个圆圆的、铺满青苔的洞穴。接着他们轻轻地爬了进去，彼此相拥着躺下，发觉那地方又深、又暖、又舒服、又柔软，没多会儿便沉沉睡去了。

===Original===
Now, close beside them, in a hollow, was another nest, in which lay a lark and his wife; and the children were awakened, very early in the morning, by a dispute between Mr. and Mrs. Lark.

“Let me up,” said the lark.

“It is not time,” said the lark’s wife.

“It is,” said the lark, rather rudely. “The darkness is quite thin. I can almost see my own beak.”

“Nonsense!” said the lark’s wife. “You know you came home yesterday morning quite worn out﻿—you had to fly so very high before you saw him. I am sure he would not mind if you took it a little easier. Do be quiet and go to sleep again.”

“That’s not it at all,” said the lark. “He doesn’t want me. I want him. Let me up, I say.”

He began to sing; and Tricksey-Wee and Buffy-Bob, having now learned the way, answered him:﻿

===Chinese===
就在他们紧旁边的一个凹窝里，还有一只巢，里头住着一只云雀和他的妻子；一大清早，孩子们就被云雀夫妇的一场争吵吵醒了。

"让我起来。"云雀说。

"还不到时辰呢。"云雀的妻子说。

"到了。"云雀相当粗鲁地说，"天色已经很稀薄了。我几乎能瞧见自个儿的喙了。"

"胡说！"云雀的妻子说，"你明知道昨天早上你回家时累得半死——你得飞得那么那么高才瞧见他。我肯定，你要是稍微悠着点，他也不会介意的。乖，安静些，接着睡吧。"

"根本不是那么回事。"云雀说，"不是他要我，是我要他。我说，让我起来。"

他唱了起来；小淘气崔克西和胖墩鲍勃这会儿已经摸着了门道，便应和他：

===Original===
> “I will sing a song.
> I’m the Lark.”
>
> “Sing, sing, Throat-strong,
> Little Kill-the-dark.
> What will you sing about,
> Now the night is out?”
>
> “I can only call;
> I can’t think.
> Let me up﻿—that’s all.
> Let me drink!
> Thirsting all the long night
> For a drink of light.”

===Chinese===
> "我要唱一支歌。
> 我是云雀。"
>
> "唱吧，唱吧，金嗓子，
> 小小逐暗者。
> 黑夜已经过去了，
> 你要唱些什么？"
>
> "我只能呼唤；
> 我不能思想。
> 让我上去——就这样。
> 让我畅饮！
> 整整一夜我干渴难当，
> 只求饮一口光。"

===Original===
By this time the lark was standing on the edge of his nest and looking at the children.

“Poor little things! You can’t fly,” said the lark.

“No; but we can look up,” said Tricksey.

“Ah, you don’t know what it is to see the very first of the sun.”

“But we know what it is to wait till he comes. He’s no worse for your seeing him first, is he?”
